# Tales of the Dark Moon: the season as a player plays it

Week 1 Day 1 to Week 6 Day 7, in playing order. Generated from the game's own data by `scripts/gen-playthrough.ts` (2026-10-17). **Do not publish: it holds the whole season.**

**How to read it.** Quoted text (`>`) is exactly what the player reads. Lines in *⚙ italics* describe the gameplay: what the screen offers, the rule, the numbers. Where the story branches, every branch is given, each labelled. The party is a sample of four: you, Mei, Dev and Arjun (the game fills in the real friends' names and the party's size).

## How the game plays (the rules in brief)

- **The app and the party.** *Tales of the Dark Moon* is a story game in a chat app for a fixed party of 2 to 6 real friends, over 6 weeks. A storyteller called MYTHIOS tells it from stored text. Each friend plays one character, under their own name, with one of six classes (Warrior, Artificer, Sage, Trickster, Assassin, Archer).

- **A week** is six weekdays (Days 1 to 6), each played alone in about 8 to 12 minutes, then a live Gathering on Day 7, played together. A day opens at 5:00am party time.

- **Places:** your own thread (the day's chapter, private questions to Garrick), the party chat (one result line per friend, Garrick's morning line, talk, the Gathering), and the tabs: Record (your numbers), Bag (Relics, Seeker Chest, Dark Moon Verses), Store (gear and Relics).

- **Attributes:** Might, Craft, Intellect, Charm, Agility, Aim, each in a tier: Novice 1–25 (+0), Apprentice 26–50 (+2), Adept 51–75 (+4), Expert 76–100 (+6), Master 101+ (+8).

- **A Challenge:** pick one of two Attributes the card offers, roll d20 + its tier bonus − the DC; 1 or more passes, a tie fails. Weekday DC by week: 6, 7, 7, 8, 9, 9. The card says "You succeed on X+". Pass: +500g and the Attribute grows. Fail: −150g, and a worse branch of the scene. Both branches reach the same plot. [PRESS ON], on some days after a pass: 4 harder, +200g and one extra detail.

- **Fights:** 100 HP each side, one tap a round, damage = the hit's margin × 5. Gear on two tracks (Weapon 14–18, Armor 27–31, Levels 1–5) bought in the Store. Nobody dies: at 0 HP, "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.

- **Relics** (150g): an Attribute Relic gives +4 on one roll; a Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for one fight. Relics can be given to friends; gold cannot.

- **The Seeker Fragment:** each day a short old text with two missing words appears in every friend's Seeker Chest; each word is a jumble of letters to unscramble (+25g a word). Six fragments make the week's map, which helps at the Gathering.

- **Garrick**, the innkeeper who travels with the party, is in the party chat: he answers when a message names him, from stored lines (AI small talk only about the road, food and weather, filtered). He never lies, promises or predicts. He can also be asked privately in your own thread and at the fire.

- **After the chapter:** the last light (one optional lead and a clue), then the Campfire (shared notes, tonight's question, the riddle), then bed.

- **The Gathering:** the Approach (a group vote, one friend rolls), the Battle (everyone fights their own enemy at once), Notice and [LOOK CLOSER], the United Effort (one shared job, turns), the Lore Reveal with Match and Ask, the Twist, the Cliffhanger.

- **Each new day opens with "the road so far":** a short recap of yesterday (and of any day a friend missed).

# Week 1: The List

*⚙ This week: weekday DC 6; weekday enemy DEF 22, ATK 15; the Gathering calls on Might. The week's question at the fire: "Why are our names on that list?"*

## Week 1, Day 1

### Your thread: the chapter, "The forest road"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who is this man, and what does he want with me?*

> The old forest road has not been mended in a generation. Roots have shouldered up through it and the ruts are full of last year's leaves. Overhead the trees lean so close that the afternoon comes down through them green and thin, like light through bottle glass. It smells of wet bark. Nothing sings.
>
> Your wagon has been complaining about the road for miles. Then the axle gives with a crack that goes off through the trees, and the whole cart lurches down onto one corner and is still.
>
> In the quiet afterwards, something moves in the treeline.
>
> A man steps out onto the road with his blade already drawn. He does not ask for your purse. He does not say anything at all.

**FIGHT: A MAN ON THE ROAD**

> He means it. So must you.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 22 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 15 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. You strike first. A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: +300g. At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> Steel rings on steel, and the wood swallows the sound. Then, between one blow and the next, he breaks off. One look at you, level and unhurried, and he is gone into the trees before you can lay a hand on him. For a few strides you can hear him in the bracken. Then you cannot.

*⚙ If you lost the fight:*

> You are on your back in the road, with leaf-mould in your mouth and the green roof turning slowly overhead. He could finish it. He looks down at you for a long moment, and then he is gone into the trees.

> The road is very quiet. Somewhere off among the trunks, a single bird starts up again.

*⚙ A choice: [LOOK WHERE HE STOOD] or [LOOK AT WHAT HE WAS AT]. Pick in any order until every branch has been seen.*

*⚙ If you pick [LOOK WHERE HE STOOD]:*

> A scrap of parchment in the leaf-mould, half burned. Its scorched edge flakes at your touch and still smells faintly of smoke. It is not writing and it is not a drawing. Somebody laid it over something hard and raised, and rubbed charcoal across it until the shape came through.
>
> You turn it to the light and back again. You do not know the shape.

*⚙ If you pick [LOOK AT WHAT HE WAS AT]:*

> Your cargo box, slewed half off the tilted wagon bed. The lock is jammed. The scratches round it are fresh, bright in the dull old metal. They are not yours.
>
> He was at this before you ever saw him.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** He held back. Every blow came a half-beat late.

- **Craft:** The lock was not forced open. It was forced shut.

- **Intellect:** A robber goes for the purse and the horse. He went for neither.

- **Charm:** He never once looked angry. He looked like a man doing a job.

- **Agility:** He was in the trees before your axle went. He was waiting for it.

- **Aim:** That axle did not snap. It was cut most of the way through.

**CHALLENGE: OPEN THE BOX**

> You need to know what he wanted in here, and the light is going on a road he knows better than you do.

*⚙ Pick one: Might (force it), or Craft (work the jammed mechanism free). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 6: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 7+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: it costs you skin and most of an hour of daylight, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> The lock fights you for a moment. Then something inside it gives with a small, dull click. The lid comes up in your hands while there is still light enough to see by.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> It does not want to give, and it takes its time about it. It takes some of your skin as well. When the lid comes up at last your knuckles are bleeding and the trees have gone from green to black.

> You go through the box twice, by what light is left. Everything you own is here, folded the way you folded it. Nothing has been taken.
>
> Something has been put in.
>
> A second scrap, clean and unburned: a list, in a neat, steady hand. **Four names. Yours is one of them.** Under them a single line that is not a name.
>
> **The Hollow Wake Inn, on the drove road.**

> You leave the wagon where it lies and walk the last of the dusk down to the crossroads. Where your road meets the drove road an old boundary stone stands half-buried in the verge, weathered past reading. Moss furs its north side. Something in a crack at its foot catches the last of the light.

*⚙ A button: [PULL IT OUT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Thin, dense, and cold in a way nothing should be at this time of year. It lies across your palm like a sliver of winter that has forgotten to melt. It is not paper. It has not aged the way paper would.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> You sit a while on the stone with the list in one hand and the cold thing in the other. The dark comes down through the trees, and somewhere an owl tries its voice.
>
> A man broke into your box to leave you a list with your name on it.
>
> **One day north: the Hollow Wake Inn. Somebody wants you there.**

### The Seeker Fragment (in your Seeker Chest)

*⚙ Waiting in every friend's Seeker Chest from the morning; solved whenever you like, usually after the chapter or at the fire.*

*⚙ Found at: The boundary stone. Two words to unscramble: [C R E T S D T A E] and [L S E C O].*

> Five fell. Five were turned against him. Four were _____ after. One stayed _____.

*⚙ Solved:*

### The last light (Fan Out)

*⚙ After the chapter, before the fire: follow ONE lead, or go straight to the fire. One quick Challenge on the week's DC, no gold, no growth. The clue always comes back; a pass also gives the first letter of one of today's fragment words. Friends who pick the same lead share one board card.*

**Lead: the list, by the last light**: Intellect (read it again, line by line), or Craft (hold it to the light and look at the ink)

Clue found, "The even hand":

> Not one name is crossed out or written over. Every line sits level, and the inn's line is in the same hand as the names.

**Lead: the trees where he went**: Agility (go after him into the trees), or Aim (pick out his trail in the dusk)

Clue found, "Two sets of boots":

> Twenty paces in, his prints meet another man's. The two go on together, at a walk.

**Lead: the bottom of the cargo box**: Craft (take the box apart, board by board), or Intellect (go through it a third time)

Clue found, "The oilcloth":

> Under your things lies a square of oilcloth you never packed, folded small. Whatever he put in the box, he wrapped it first against the wet.

*⚙ On a failed roll, the clue still comes, with: "It takes you until full dark, but you find it."*

## Week 1, Day 2

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Your axle gave on the old forest road, and a silent man came out of the trees, fought you, and was gone, leaving a half-burned charcoal rubbing of a shape you did not know. Nothing was taken from your cargo box. Something had been put in: a list of four names in a neat, steady hand, yours among them, and the name of an inn. At the crossroads a thin fragment lay waiting in the stone, cold as a sliver of winter. The Hollow Wake Inn is one day north, and somebody wants you there.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The Hollow Wake Inn"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who are the others on the list, and who wrote it?*

> A day on the drove road, between hedges nobody has cut in years. At the end of it, low under its own dark thatch, stands the Hollow Wake Inn. One window is lit. The sign over the door has been painted so many times that whatever it showed has gone soft at the edges, like a face on an old coin.
>
> The taproom is warm and low and smells of woodsmoke. Underneath the smoke is the smell of ale soaked a long time into old boards. There are three strangers in it who are not drinking.
>
> Mei has a torn page flat on the table under one hand. Dev is turning one over and over. Arjun's is folded small, the way you fold a thing you have read too many times.
>
> You put yours down beside them. The same neat hand. The same names, each with a date beside it.
>
> Mei moves along the bench to make room. The fire settles in the grate. Nobody has to explain.

> The innkeeper sets down the cups. He sets down one more than there are of you, at the empty end of the table, and goes back behind his bar. He has a cloth over one shoulder. He moves the way a man moves in a room he has crossed ten thousand times, with no hurry in him at all.

*⚙ A button: [ASK HIM ABOUT THE LIST]*

> You begin to ask. Before you are halfway through he gives you his name, plainly, the way he might give you the price of a room.
>
> *"Garrick."*
>
> He comes round the end of the bar and looks at the pages for a while, not touching them. Then he goes back, reaches under the counter, and brings out a satchel, worn soft at the corners.
>
> *"Woman called Meira. Scholar. Sat right there most nights, two years near enough. Cheapest wine in the house, and tipped like a queen."*
>
> He puts it on the table.
>
> *"Left this. Hasn't been back in three weeks."*

> Inside is a notebook, packed to the margins in the hand you all know by now. It smells of dust and lamp smoke. The pages have gone soft with handling. Your names are in it. Every one of you.
>
> Beside yours, in the same neat hand: [the town you were born in], and a second name, [your grandparent's name].
>
> And near the back, where the binding shows, some pages are gone.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The satchel strap has been mended three times. *(Adept and up:)* She carried this a long way, often.

- **Agility:** Dev's knuckles are skinned. So are Arjun's. *(Adept and up:)* Every one of you fought somebody to get here.

- **Intellect:** The notes are in two inks. *(Adept and up:)* She came back to these pages years apart.

- **Charm:** The innkeeper has not asked a single one of you your name.

- **Craft:** The binding thread is cut, not torn. *(Adept and up:)* Whoever took those pages took care.

- **Aim:** There is one seat in this room that sees both doors. *(Adept and up:)* It is the one he says was hers.

**CHALLENGE: FIT THE PAGE**

> Your page came out of this book. Prove it, and find what else is on the leaf it came from.

*⚙ Pick one: Intellect (match the hand, line by line, to the entries on either side), or Craft (match the torn edge to its stub, fibre to fibre). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 6: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 7+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: it takes you the whole evening, with strangers watching you get it wrong, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You bring the candle close and try your page against the stubs, one after another. Then one of them takes it, every rag and fibre of the tear meeting its fellow. Your stub fits like a key. There are four stubs, and four of you.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> You try it against every stub but the right one. The candle burns down. The strangers watch you get it wrong. Mei, in the end, reaches over and turns your page the other way up. It fits. There are four stubs, and four of you.

**[PRESS ON]: READ THE STUBS**

*⚙ Offered only after a pass: what is left on the torn edges themselves. 4 harder (DC 10). [PRESS ON] or [LEAVE IT]. Pass: +200g, and one more detail; fail costs nothing.*

*⚙ If you pressed on and passed:*

> Tilt them to the candle and you see it.
>
> Each stub has a faint straight line scored down it. She did not rip these out. She cut them against a rule, one at a time, with care.
>
> She meant to send them.

> So the list is hers. She wrote your names in a book, and cut them out, and then she stopped coming here. And each of you was set upon on the road, within the same few days, and came away holding a page.

> On the facing leaf, one line will not give itself up to any of you. It is in some private shorthand, tight as wire. The longer you look at it, the less it says.

*⚙ A button: [ASK HIM ABOUT THE LINE NOBODY CAN READ]*

> He does not look up from the bar.
>
> *"That's a rock. Split lightning-wise, about so high. Two hours north."*
>
> He goes on wiping.
>
> *"She'd sit right there. I doubt she thought I was listening."*

> Once you know the place, the rest of the page gives way. It names none of you.
>
> It is about a child. Taken, and held somewhere near that rock. No date. Only the place, and the plain sense, in every line of it, that whatever was happening there was not over.

*⚙ A button: [ASK HIM TO TAKE YOU THERE]*

> He sets down the glass. In the quiet room it is the loudest thing there is.
>
> *"I've a business to run."*

*⚙ A button: [TELL HIM NOBODY ELSE CAN READ HER]*

> A long pause. A log shifts in the grate and throws up a few sparks. He looks at the satchel, and the pages, and each of you in turn.
>
> *"...As far as the Widow's Tooth. That's all I'm agreeing to."*

> You pay before you go up. Dev puts down more than the cups were worth and nobody counts it. The innkeeper sweeps the coins into his apron without a word.
>
> The fire has burned down to a red murmur. The extra cup is still at the empty end of the table. Nobody has touched it.

> By the door, an old verse is carved into the beam, so old the wood has grown round the letters. Under it somebody has worked a small hollow into the timber.

*⚙ A button: [FEEL INSIDE]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Cold enough to feel through the warm wood.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> Upstairs, by a candle, you turn the notebook's last pages. The old house ticks and settles round you. Two lines in her hand, written years apart, in different inks:
>
> *"Scattered on purpose, each piece safe alone. Only together do they mean anything."*
>
> *"The old tellings of the Seekers speak of stones. The oldest records don't call them stones at all. They call them shards."*
>
> You do not know what she meant. Somewhere below you, a door is barred for the night.

## Week 1, Day 3

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Morning. The fire's been going an hour, and the kitchen smells of yesterday's loaf toasting on the hob. I've wrapped a heel of it and a wedge of cheese for each of you, in cloth, so it keeps. Two hours to the rock, and an empty stomach makes every one of them longer.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> At the Hollow Wake Inn the rest of the list was waiting, each of them holding a torn page in the same neat hand. The innkeeper, Garrick, set down one cup more than there were of you, then brought out the satchel of Meira, a scholar who sat in his taproom most nights for two years and has not been back in three weeks. Your pages were cut from her notebook, and two lines in her hand say that the pieces were scattered on purpose, and that the oldest records call the Seekers' stones shards. Her last readable page tells of a child held near a split rock called the Widow's Tooth. It lies two hours north, and whatever happened there has not finished.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The Widow's Tooth"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: is the child still at the rock?*

> Two hours north the road runs straight at a rock taller than a house, split down the middle as if something struck it once, long ago. You see it a long way off, black against a white sky. It does not get any friendlier as you come.
>
> It is the first time the four of you have walked anywhere together. Garrick agreed to come as far as the Widow's Tooth and not a step further. In the two hours it takes to get there he has complained about the road, his boots and the weather, in that order, and seems to be enjoying himself.
>
> Somewhere along the way the question is put to him: how did he know this place from one line of scribble? He answers it to the air.
>
> *"People tell a man with a cloth in his hand everything,"* Garrick says. *"They forget he's got ears."*

> There is nobody at the rock. Close to, it is dark with old rain and furred with grey lichen. The wind makes a low, hollow note in the split.
>
> There are tracks at its foot: several people, not long ago, and a cart. And low on the stone, half worn away, something has been cut into it.

*⚙ A choice: [GO TO THE MARKING] or [FOLLOW THE CART-RUTS]. Pick one; only the picked branch plays.*

*⚙ If you pick [GO TO THE MARKING]:*

> You crouch at the stone and put your fingers to the cut lines. They are cold, and gritty under your touch. That is where you are, with your back to the bracken, when the bracken moves.

*⚙ If you pick [FOLLOW THE CART-RUTS]:*

> The ruts turn off the road toward a line of trees, and a thread of smoke beyond them. You are looking that way, so you see him first: a man lying flat in the bracken, watching the rock.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The ruts are deep going in and shallow coming out. *(Adept and up:)* The cart left lighter than it came. Or it left something here.

- **Agility:** The bracken is flattened in one place only. *(Adept and up:)* He has been lying there since before dawn.

- **Intellect:** Nobody watches an empty rock. *(Adept and up:)* So it is not empty to him, or it will not be for long.

- **Charm:** He is not afraid of you. He is afraid of being seen by you.

- **Craft:** The cart has one wheel that pulls left. *(Adept and up:)* You would know its track again.

- **Aim:** From where he lies he can see the road both ways and the village smoke. *(Adept and up:)* It is a post, and it has been used before.

**FIGHT: THE MAN IN THE BRACKEN**

> He comes up running, and he is not running away.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 22 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 15 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. Who strikes first is set by your choice ([FOLLOW THE CART-RUTS] lets the enemy strike first). A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: +300g. At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If you strike first:*

> You saw him first: you strike before he is set.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He was behind you: he strikes first.

> He does not say a word. Not when he is hit, and not when he hits. The moment there is a gap he takes it, and he is away through the trees toward the smoke, faster than any of you.
>
> Arjun goes twenty yards after him and stops. Arjun stands there a moment longer, listening, then turns back.

> You had him by the collar for a moment, close enough to smell him. Woodsmoke, and lamp oil.

> You go back to the rock.
>

>
> It fits. Every raised line sits under its line of charcoal, and there is charcoal still, ground into the edges of the stone. She laid her paper here and rubbed. This rock is what the scrap is a picture of.
>
> For a moment nobody says anything. The wind goes on sounding its one low note in the split.
>
> Meira stood where you are standing. And the man who came at you on the forest road was carrying her work.

> Under the overhang, on a dry shelf out of the wind, something small is lying on its side.

>

> There is a crack in the stone under the marking, wide enough for a hand.

## Week 1, Day 4

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> The dew came down heavy in the night, and every frond of bracken between here and the road is holding a cupful of it. Tuck your trousers into your boots, unless you fancy squelching all morning. There's tea, and I've put a little honey in it.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Garrick walked you north to the Widow's Tooth, grumbling about the road, his boots and the weather, and enjoying every word of it. A watcher in the bracken fought you and ran for the village smoke. Meira's rubbing fitted a marking low on the rock, line for line: she had been here, and the man on the forest road was carrying her work. Under the overhang lay a child's carved horse, worn soft with holding. Garrick has not gone back to his inn, and the ruts lead to a village where every door will be shut when you come.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Fenmark"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: what happened in this village, and why will nobody say?*

> Fenmark sits low at the edge of the fen: reed thatch and peat smoke, along a single lane of cobbles with mud between. It goes quiet the moment you come down the lane. Doors do not quite shut. Nobody is standing in one, either.
>
> The lookout from the rock came through here ahead of you. You can tell by the gate at the far end of the street, left swinging. It knocks softly on its post in the wind.
>
> Mei takes the left side of the lane, Dev the right. You take the well.

> Behind the well, on an upturned bucket: a bowl of barley, half eaten, the spoon still standing in it. You hold your hand over it. It is still warm.
>
> Whoever was eating it left in a hurry, and not long ago.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The bucket was not knocked over. It was set down. *(Adept and up:)* Whoever sat here is small, and careful.

- **Agility:** One heel-mark in the mud, deep at the toe. They went at a run. *(Adept and up:)* Toward the back lanes, not the road.

- **Intellect:** Barley and nothing else, in a house bowl. This is somebody's ordinary dinner. *(Adept and up:)* They live here, and they did not expect to be found.

- **Charm:** Every shutter on the street is closed but one. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody wants to be asked.

- **Craft:** The spoon is whittled, not bought, and whittled badly. *(Adept and up:)* A child's knife did that.

- **Aim:** From this bucket you can see the whole lane and nobody in the lane can see you. *(Adept and up:)* It was chosen for watching.

*⚙ A choice: [LOOK FOR FOOTPRINTS] or [KNOCK NEXT DOOR]. Pick one; the choice colours the moment, the story is the same.*

**CHALLENGE: FIND WHO LEFT IT**

> Somebody in this village watched something, and is hiding from you. Find them before the whole street knows you are looking.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (follow the fresh prints round the back lanes), or Charm (coax the neighbour at her half-open door into pointing). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 6: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 7+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you search the wrong end of the village first, in full view, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> The prints are small and quick, and they double back twice. You do not follow them. You walk round the grain store the other way, sit down on the step in the thin sun, and wait.
>
> After a while a boy of about fourteen edges out of the gap behind it, all elbows and held breath. He has seen you see him. He does not run.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> You try three doors on the wrong side of the street, and three faces watch you do it through the cracks. By the time you think of the grain store the boy has bolted from behind it, straight into Arjun, who was coming the other way.
>
> He is shaking. Arjun lets go of his sleeve and steps back.

> He looks at each of you in turn. He is counting you.

*⚙ A choice: [SHOW HIM THE LIST] or [ASK WHO WAS TAKEN] or [SAY NOTHING. WAIT.]. Pick one; the choice colours the moment, the story is the same.*

> For a while he says nothing at all. He looks once down the empty lane. Then it comes out low and fast.
>
> *"Three nights ago. They came in the dark with no torches. They did not knock anything over. They did not say anything they did not have to. Like they'd done it before."*
>
> *"They took Tamsin. She's nine. She was counting when they carried her out. She counts when she's frightened."*
>
> He swallows.
>
> *"One of them had a knife I'd never seen the like of. Curved. Black handle. He saw me at the window. He did not even walk faster. He just said they'd know if I talked."*

> While he talks he hums, without noticing, a rhyme with no tune to speak of. His grandmother's, he says. She sang it here, at the well.
>
> You look at the well. Its stone lip is older than every house in the street, green-black and sweating with damp. Worn into it, just where a hand would rest, is a hollow.

*⚙ A button: [REACH IN]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Your fingers close on something thin and cold. Well-water beads on it and will not soak in.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> Boots on the cobbles.
>
> An old man is coming down the street with two younger ones behind him, and the two younger ones walk straight past him and stand between you and the well.

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> The boy is still standing next to you. He does not move away.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> The boy is behind Arjun. One of the men looks at him, and he looks at the ground.

*⚙ A choice: [STAND WHERE YOU ARE] or [STEP BACK, HANDS OPEN] or [PUT YOURSELF IN FRONT OF THE BOY]. Pick one; the choice colours the moment, the story is the same.*

> Nobody draws. Nobody needs to. The message is plain enough.
>
> The elder says the things an elder says: that Fenmark has no quarrel with travellers, that the road out is the same as the road in.
>
> But you are watching his hands, and his hands are not angry. They are afraid. He is not hiding what happened here. He is terrified of what happens to this village if it is known that somebody talked.
>
> He steps in close, as if to see you off. His voice drops until only you can hear it. His breath smells of peat smoke and no sleep.
>
> *"Please. There's a woman two hours east who's seen that same curved blade before. Ask her. Don't say I sent you."*
>
> Then, louder, for the street: *"Go on, now."*

> Garrick is already holding the gate at the bottom of the lane. He has found the one dry place to stand in all of Fenmark, and he looks as if he would like you to notice.
>
> The boy catches up with you there, out of breath.
>
> *"A man ran through at first light. He did not stop for anyone. He took the east path."*

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> It is long past noon when Fenmark lets you go, and the light is already thinning over the fen. Behind you, one by one, the shutters close.
>
> **East. Two hours. He has half a day on you, and he is heading for the only other person alive who has seen that knife.**

## Week 1, Day 5

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Frost on the cart ruts this morning, thin as paper, and it crackles when you step on it. The kettle's been singing since before the light. Tea's in the pot, strong enough to stand a spoon in, and nobody leaves until their hands are warm.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Fenmark went quiet as you came down its muddy lane, its far gate still knocking where the lookout had run through. Behind the well a frightened boy told you what he saw: three nights ago quiet men took Tamsin, who is nine and counts when she is frightened, and one of them carried a curved knife with a black handle. The elder would not be seen to help, but under his breath he begged you to find a woman two hours east who has seen that knife before. It was long past noon when Fenmark let you go. The man has half a day on you, and he is heading for the only other person alive who has seen that knife.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The old charcoal camp"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who has seen the curved knife, and what happened that time?*

> The camp is an old one: burning-pits long cold, a trade that left years ago. The earth is black to a spade's depth. When the wind stirs it, the whole clearing smells of a fire that went out before you were born. But the path in is worn too clean for that to be all of it.
>
> You reach it after dark. At the edge of the clearing stands a shed, with no light in it. You knock. Nobody answers.

*⚙ A choice: [LOOK AT THE STEP] or [LOOK AT THE FIRE] or [LOOK AT THE PATH]. Pick one; only the picked branch plays.*

*⚙ If you pick [LOOK AT THE STEP]:*

> Swept this morning. Somebody lives here, and is proud enough to sweep.

*⚙ If you pick [LOOK AT THE FIRE]:*

> Cold on top. Warm underneath, when you push a hand into the ash. Banked, not dead. Somebody means to come back to it.

*⚙ If you pick [LOOK AT THE PATH]:*

> Two sets of prints on it, both fresh. A woman's, going out. And over them a man's, coming in, at a run.
>
> He is here.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The axe is gone from the block. The block is still wet. *(Adept and up:)* Whoever lives here took it with them, and not for wood.

- **Agility:** A shed with one door and a shuttered back window. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody inside it could be gone before you knocked twice.

- **Intellect:** Nobody keeps a place this tidy and this hidden unless they are waiting for something.

- **Charm:** There is one bowl, one cup, one stool. *(Adept and up:)* She has been alone a long time.

- **Craft:** The shutters bar from the inside and the outside both. *(Adept and up:)* She built this to be watched from.

- **Aim:** There is a gap in the trees that frames the whole clearing. *(Adept and up:)* You are being looked at through it now.

> Something moves behind the shed. You hear a blade being worked into wood, and then a breath, held too late.

**FIGHT: THE RUNNER FROM FENMARK**

> He got here first. He is trying the shutters.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 22 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 15 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. Who strikes first is set by your choice ([LOOK AT THE PATH] lets the enemy strike first). A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: +300g. At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If you strike first:*

> You read the path: you are ready for him.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He hears you before you see him.

> The same silence. The same way of leaving: the first gap, and gone, north through the trees this time.
>
> Mei has a hand on Dev's arm, to stop Dev following into the dark. Dev does not shake it off.

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> He has left his knife behind, stuck in the shutter where he was working it. A plain kitchen knife. There is nothing curved about it.

> A twig goes.
>
> She is at the edge of the trees, where the starlight gives out, and she has been there for some time. A woman with a woodcutter's axe held low and ready, and her eyes going from face to face.
>
> *"Which of you carries the curved knife?"*

*⚙ A choice: [SHOW HER YOUR EMPTY HANDS] or [TELL HER WHO YOU JUST DROVE OFF] or [HOLD OUT THE CARVED HORSE]. Pick one; only the picked branch plays.*

*⚙ If you pick [SHOW HER YOUR EMPTY HANDS] or [TELL HER WHO YOU JUST DROVE OFF]:*

> The axe comes down by inches. Then her eyes go to the carved horse in your other hand, and stop.

*⚙ If you pick [HOLD OUT THE CARVED HORSE]:*

> The axe comes down all at once.

> She takes the little horse in both hands. She has to sit.
>
> Garrick builds the fire up without being asked. He coaxes the banked coals until they catch, and the clearing jumps into orange light. Then he puts a cup into her hands. She holds it as if it were the first warm thing she has touched in a month.
>
> *"That's my sister's boy's,"* she says. *"I carved it."*

> For a while there is only the fire. Then it comes, all of it, two winters' worth.
>
> *"Curved. Black-handled. I know it. Same man took him two winters back. Same time of year. Same stretch of country. Nobody ever found him."*
>
> She turns the horse over.
>
> *"Where was it?"*
>
> You tell her. She nods, slowly, as if she had always known the rock.

> You count it out, because somebody has to.
>
> Meira has been gone three weeks. Tamsin was taken this week. The child in Meira's notes, held near the rock, cannot be Tamsin.
>
> Meira was writing about this woman's nephew. About the last one.
>
> And it is the same time of year.

> The fire ticks and settles. She says a thing twice while she talks, hardly hearing herself: *"The power that gives is also the power that takes."* She cannot say where she first heard it. Her eyes go, when she says it, to the old dead tree behind the shed, hollow at the foot.

>

>
> **North. The same man, the same country, the same season. If it comes round like a calendar, the time is now.**

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Long road north today, and a cold one, by the look of the sky. I've filled the flasks and packed bread for the walking. The bread's yesterday's, but it's honest, and it travels better than I do.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You reached the old charcoal camp after dark, and the runner from Fenmark was there before you; he fought without a word and fled north. The woman who lives there knew the carved horse at once: she carved it for her sister's boy, taken by the same black-handled knife two winters ago, at this same time of year. So the child in Meira's notes was never Tamsin; it was him. By the fire she said one thing twice without hearing herself: the power that gives is also the power that takes. At first light she pointed you north, and if it comes round like a calendar, the time is now.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The ford"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where did they take her?*

> By midday the old charcoal road comes down to a river. It is shallow enough to wade, but brown and fast and full of the hills' rain. It talks to itself over the stones. A worn stone stands at the edge to show the safe line across.
>
> The near bank is churned to mud. A cart went over: its ruts come out on the far side and go on.
>
> One set of prints does not. Small ones. They come down to the water's edge and stop.
>
> The river is rising. The edge of the mud is going as you watch, crumb by crumb.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The cart sat low coming down the bank. *(Adept and up:)* It rides high on the far side.

- **Agility:** The small prints are evenly spaced. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody was dragging her. Not yet.

- **Intellect:** A cart that crosses and a child who does not. *(Adept and up:)* They parted here on purpose.

- **Charm:** Dev has stopped talking. Everybody has.

- **Craft:** The left wheel pulls. *(Adept and up:)* It is the cart from the Widow's Tooth.

- **Aim:** On the far bank, nothing small. *(Adept and up:)* Not one print.

**CHALLENGE: READ THE WATERLINE**

> The river is taking the prints. Whatever they say, they will say it once.

*⚙ Pick one: Aim (pick out the line of them from the bank before it goes), or Agility (get down the bank to the water's edge, and read them close before the river takes the rest). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 6: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 7+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: the water takes them, and you will never know how she came to it, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You have them. Small prints, walking, right to the edge. Her own feet. She was not hurt, and she was not carried here.
>
> Then two deep prints facing her, a man's, with the heels dug in. He lifted her. And after that there is only water.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> A brown tongue of river slides over the mud, and when it draws back the mud is smooth. There were prints. Now there is the cart, and nothing.
>
> Arjun finds the last of it, further down: two deep heel-marks, a man's, facing the water. He lifted something. After that there is only water.

**[PRESS ON]: WADE OUT**

*⚙ Offered only after a pass: find where they went from the water. 4 harder (DC 10). [PRESS ON] or [LEAVE IT]. Pass: +200g, and one more detail; fail costs nothing.*

*⚙ If you pressed on and passed:*

> The cold takes you at the knees, then at the waist. The current leans its whole weight on you.
>
> Mid-stream, on the upstream side of the ford-stone, a scrape of fresh paint on the rock at the height of a gunwale.
>
> There was a boat.

> She was carried into the river. The cart went on without her. No tracker alive can follow from here.

> Under the waterline, at the foot of the ford-stone, where you would only see it in a low year:

*⚙ A button: [REACH DOWN]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Cold beyond the cold of the river.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> That is six.
>
> Six of these old scraps in a week, some of them hidden longer than anyone can say, and not one of you has missed one. You have been putting them in your pockets like a child picking up stones.
>
> Meira wrote it: *each piece safe alone. Only together do they mean anything.*

*⚙ A button: [LAY THEM OUT]*

> You set them side by side on a flat rock, still warm from the noon sun. The river is loud beside you.
>
> They move.
>
> Not far. The width of a finger. Each one turns a little, and settles against the next, as if they had been waiting for exactly this and exactly these hands. And then they are not six things. They are one.
>
> A map. Old, and exact, in a hand none of you knows.
>
> It runs across the ford and on. And the place it ends is marked with a shape.
>
> Mei puts the burned rubbing down beside it, and squares its edge to the map's with one finger.
>

> For a long moment nobody touches either of them. The trail you have lost and the map you are holding point the same way.

> That night you stop at a roadside inn, a smoky, crowded place of drovers and dogs. A ham hangs in the chimney. Garrick orders for the table. When the cups come he pours one more than there are of you, and sets it at the empty place.

## Week 1, Day 7: the Gathering

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> At the ford a child's small prints came down to the water and stopped where a man's heels had dug in to lift her, and there the trail ended. On a sun-warmed rock the six cold fragments slid together into one map, ending at the shape from Meira's rubbing. That night, at a roadside inn, Garrick poured one cup more than there were of you: family habit, he said, one for whoever can't be with us. The way was read out over supper, and a courier who had come in for a bed paid his bill and left without taking it. Cross at first light, and you will be there by nightfall; whatever is at the place the map marks, the season for it is now.

### The Gathering (live, the whole party together in the party chat)

*⚙ That morning, in each friend's thread:*

> Tonight: the Gathering. The map ends across the ford, at the mark from Meira's rubbing. Tonight calls on Might.

*⚙ Lobby at the time the party picked (poll on Day 4); starts once everyone is in, or any friend may start after 10 minutes, or by itself after 30. MYTHIOS posts the story a passage at a time; [GO ON] moves it on. About 20 to 30 minutes with the table talking.*

#### Arrival

> Evening finds you where the map said you would be: an old mine cut into the far riverbank, its mouth half drowned by the river.
>
> There is torchlight just inside. There should not be.
>
> While you watch, it moves from one side of the mouth to the other, unhurried, and stops.
>
> Garrick knows a way in. He has known it since he was a boy, he says.

#### The Approach

*⚙ A group vote (majority after 30 seconds; the vote shows who is best at each option): HOW DO YOU GO IN?*

1. **GARRICK'S WAY**: a dry side-passage up the bank. You come in above them.
2. **THE DROWNED MOUTH**: waist-deep, with the river loud enough to cover you. You come in below them.

> Either way the last stretch is the same: a gallery floored with timbers nobody has trusted in fifty years. Far beneath them, in the dark, is the river.

**THE APPROACH: THE ROTTEN GALLERY**

*⚙ One friend steps up (whoever has rolled fewest Approaches) and rolls for the party, in front of everyone: By the passage → Aim (pick out, from above and by their own torchlight, the boards that will still bear weight); By the mouth → Agility (cross the timbers without a sound). DC 8 (+2 if the party solved the week's map). Pass: +500g · **and the party strikes first**. Fail: a timber cracks like a dropped plate, and **they strike first**. The result sets who strikes first in the Battle.*

*⚙ If the Approach passed:*

> You are among them before a single head turns.

*⚙ If the Approach failed:*

> Every torch in the mine swings your way.

#### The Battle

> Four of them, between you and a small shape on the floor at the back of the gallery. Not one of them says a word.

> **This is a Battle. All of you fight at once, and it ends when the last enemy falls.**

**THE BATTLE: MINE GUARD**

*⚙ Every friend fights their own Mine Guard at once (DEF 23, ATK 16), in lockstep rounds every 10 seconds; the fight rules are the weekday ones. No condition. A friend below 50 HP sends a help-call to the chat; a friend who has won joins another fight. The party wins when the last enemy falls: +380g each. A fallen friend is "DEFEATED." and stays for the night.*

*⚙ If the party strikes first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He strikes first.

> It is over.
>
> A dropped torch rolls a little way on the boards and stops. Then there is no sound in the mine but the river.
>
> She is at the back of the gallery with her wrists tied, and she is humming. A counting song, very quietly, the way you hum to keep from crying.
>
> Whoever reaches her first kneels slowly, so as not to loom over her. The knots are small and hard. Whoever tied them has tied a great many. They take a long time, and all that time she does not look away from your hands.
>
> She does not stop when the ropes come off. She hums it all the way out into the air.
>
> Outside, after the mine, the evening is cold and very wide. She stands in it with her face turned up to the sky, breathing, still humming.
>
> You have been saying her name for three days. Now you can see her. Tamsin is about nine.
>
> There is a small cut across her palm. It has been bound in clean white linen. Somebody took the time.

#### Notice

*⚙ Notice: each friend gets one private line for their highest Attribute (a fallen friend gets the "from the floor" line):*

- **Might:** They hit hard enough to drive you back, and never once hard enough to finish it.
- **Agility:** There is one more bedroll in this gallery than there are men on the floor.
- **Intellect:** Nobody in a mine has clean linen by accident. Somebody brought it here for this.
- **Charm:** She has stopped humming only once: when the lamp swung toward the end of the tunnel, and what lies there.
- **Craft:** Half the pit-props are new timber. Somebody has been keeping this place standing.
- **Aim:** Through the mine mouth, on the far bank: a horse, tied, saddled.
- **From the floor:** From down here you can see light between the gallery's floorboards, and it is not torchlight: it is the river, a long way below, catching the torches.

> Look closer, if you like. It is a roll, and a miss costs nothing.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [LOOK CLOSER]*

*⚙ [LOOK CLOSER]: an optional roll on that Attribute (DC 8); a pass adds +75g and this line:*

- **Might:** And the one in front of you kept glancing back at the girl. He was guarding her from the fight.
- **Agility:** The blanket is still warm.
- **Intellect:** It was cut with a knife, cleanly, from a longer piece. There is more of it somewhere.
- **Charm:** She will not look at it. She will look at you instead, and count.
- **Craft:** The newest prop was cut this week.
- **Aim:** It is facing away, and somebody has just untied it.

#### The United Effort

> The way on is back across the gallery. Dev is three steps ahead of you when the floor gives.
>
> There is no warning. A timber that has waited fifty years goes all at once. Dev drops through it to the chest and stops there, jammed, arms flung wide over boards that are already splitting. Below Dev's boots there is nothing but the sound of the river, a long way down in the dark.
>
> Nobody can walk out there. The boards that are holding Dev will not hold a second pair of feet.
>
> A rope is no use. Dev's two arms are all that is holding Dev up, and neither can let go to catch one.
>
> One of you can go out flat, on your belly, with the rest standing on the stone at the gallery's edge and holding your ankles, and haul. An arm has one haul like that in it. Then the next of you.
>
> **This is a United Effort. One job, and you take it in turns.**

*⚙ If one friend plays alone:*

> It is you. You are jammed to the chest over the dark, and nobody is coming. Splinters bite your palms. The river's cold breath comes up past your legs. You get an elbow onto sounder wood, a hand's breadth at a time.

**THE UNITED EFFORT: THE DROP**

*⚙ Two bars at 100 (60 for a friend alone): "The Drop" (the thing in the way) and "Dev HP" (the thing at risk). Friends take turns, nobody twice until all have gone. Each turn is a Might Challenge against DC 6. Pass: 10 + d20 off the thing in the way. Fail: 10 + d20 off the thing at risk. It ends when either bar hits 0. Won: +300g to every friend present. One Relic per roll (+4). The story lines it can say, as the bars fall:*

- **On a pass:**
  - *(early)* You get a fistful of Dev's collar and pull until your shoulder cracks. Dev comes up a hand's breadth: enough to get one elbow onto sounder wood.
  - *(early)* Another hand's breadth. Dev's other arm is over the timber now.
  - *(middle)* Dev's ribs are clear of the hole. The boards complain, and hold.
  - *(middle)* You have Dev by the belt. There is a knee on the edge, then not, then there again.
  - *(late)* Dev is out to the hips, lying along a beam you would not have trusted with a cat.
  - *(late)* One leg is up. Dev is laughing, or something like it.
  - *(the end)* Dev comes out of the hole like a cork, and the two of you are dragged back across the boards by the ankles, fast, onto stone.
- **On a fail:**
  - *(early)* The board under your chest cracks like a dropped plate, and you are hauled back by the ankles. A broken end swings round and takes the skin off Dev's ribs.
  - *(early)* Something gives under Dev's arm. A splinter as long as a knife goes in above the elbow. Dev says nothing.
  - *(middle)* A timber-end works loose overhead and comes down across Dev's shoulder. Dev goes white.
  - *(middle)* The boards shift, and close on Dev's chest like a hand. For a moment Dev cannot get a breath.
  - *(late)* Dev's arms are shaking, and there is blood running off one elbow into the dark.
  - *(late)* Dev has stopped looking at any of you, and is looking down.
  - *(the end)* The boards go, all of them, and Dev goes with them into the dark. A long moment. Then, far below, water.
- **Between turns:** Somewhere behind you, out in the air, a counting song is still going. / Below Dev's boots, the river goes on saying the same thing. / For a while now there has been no sound but breathing, and wood.

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> Dev comes up out of the hole all at once, and you all go down together on the stone, gasping. You go round by the wall after that, one at a time, where the stone carries the boards.
>
> Behind the place where they held her, the tunnel ends in a slab of fallen roof as big as a barn door. Cold air breathes round its edges from a room beyond. It takes all of you, and it goes up in one heave. The room beyond has not been walked in: the dust lies as it fell.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> The last board goes, and Dev goes with it. The dark takes the sound.
>
> You find Dev where the river brings out what the mine drops: at the drowned mouth, grey, coughing, wrapped round a pit-prop, alive.
>
> It is an hour before all of you are together again, and another before the hole is bridged with what timber is left.
>
> Behind the place where they held her, the tunnel ends in a slab of fallen roof as big as a barn door, with a room beyond. It takes all of you to lift it, and nobody is careful. You go in tired, wet and all at once, and the floor is a mess of your own bootprints before anyone thinks to look at it.
>
> Whatever the dust in there could have told you, it will not tell you now.

#### The discovery

> The room is small, square, and cut by hands a very long time ago. It is dry: the first dry air since the river. Your torches burn straight up in it. Nothing drips. The river, which has been in your ears all night, cannot be heard in here at all.
>
> The map ended at a mark. Here is the mark.
>
> It is cut into the chamber wall at the height of your chest: the shape from Meira's rubbing, the shape at the end of the map. The burned scrap, held up to it, fits, as you knew it would.
>
> But the mark is not a carving. It is a hole. Something sat in it, something made to that shape, and it is gone.

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> The dust on the rim has settled again. Whatever was here was lifted out. Not tonight. Not centuries ago either.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> There was dust on the rim. Your own sleeves have wiped it. When this was emptied, nobody can now say.

> Under the empty place, scratched into the stone itself: **four names.**
>
> None of them is yours. The first is Tamsin's.
>
> The scratches are rough and uneven, made with a knife point and no care. Somebody bends close with a torch and reads them through once, in silence. Nobody reads them aloud.

#### The turn

*⚙ A question to the table (anyone answers in the chat; MYTHIOS never grades it):*

> *Your names were on a list. So are these. What is different about them?*

*⚙ After sixty quiet seconds:*

> *Look at the first name again.*

*⚙ After ninety, the answer:*

> They are children's names.

> **This was never about you.**

> Then why were your names on that list?
>
> The wall does not say.

#### The Cliffhanger

> The chamber is very still.
>
> In the lamplight, Garrick counts the names twice, silent.
>
> *"Three more,"* he says finally.
>
> And from the mine mouth, far behind you: hooves. One horse, already at a full gallop, going away.
>
> By the time anyone reaches the mine mouth there is nothing to see but the dark, and the sound of it fading.

*⚙ The summary card:*

- **THE MINE** · Gathering 1
- You earned: [gold] · [growth, if you rolled]
- The party: Battle won · the drop: [Dev hauled out / Dev fell]
- You learned: **Tamsin is alive.** Something was taken from that wall before you came. Her name is scratched beneath it, with three more.
- Tomorrow: **three names, and somebody who knows which is next**

# Week 2: The Wind Stone

*⚙ This week: weekday DC 7; weekday enemy DEF 23, ATK 16; the Gathering calls on Intellect. The week's question at the fire: "Why these children, and not any others?"*

## Week 2, Day 1

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Evening found you at an old mine on the far bank, with torchlight moving inside where no torchlight should be. Garrick knew a way in; he had known it since he was a boy, he said. Over a gallery of rotten timbers, four silent men stood between you and a small shape at the back, and none of them spoke while you fought. Then you found Tamsin, wrists tied, humming her counting song, a cut on her palm bound in clean white linen: somebody had taken the time. Behind where they had held her, under a slab of fallen roof, lay a small chamber cut by hands, very old and very still. On its wall was the shape from Meira's rubbing, and the burned scrap fitted it. But it was not a carving. It was a hollow, made to hold something of that shape, and it was empty. Beneath it, scratched into the stone, were four names, none of them yours, and the first was Tamsin's. They are children's names. This was never about you, and why your names were on that list, the wall does not say. Garrick counted the names twice in silence and said, “Three more.” Then, from the mine mouth, came hooves: one horse at full gallop, going away.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The second name"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: three more names on the wall. Which child is next, and can we get there first?*

> The mine is quiet again. The hoofbeats are long gone, and only the river is left, loud in the drowned mouth below. Garrick's count is still hanging in the dark.
>
> *Three more.*

*⚙ A button: [ASK HIM WHAT HE KNOWS]*

> He is wiping the soot from a lamp glass with the corner of his cloth, and he doesn't look up.
>
> *"I can tell you where the next name on that wall lives. That's where I'd start."*

> At first light you find a carter bound for Fenmark who will take Tamsin home. She is still humming.
>
> She will not get up onto the wagon. She stands in the road with her bandaged hand held against her chest, looking from one of you to the next. She settles on you.

*⚙ A choice: [CROUCH DOWN TO HER] or [ASK WHAT THE SONG IS] or [SAY NOTHING. WAIT.]. Pick one; the choice colours the moment, the story is the same.*

> *"I wasn't asleep,"* she says. *"I was counting."*
>
> *"One of them said, 'The mill boy next.' And the other one said to hush."*
>
> Then she lets the carter lift her up. She does not look back, and she does not stop humming. You can hear the counting song a long way down the road, thinner and thinner, until the wheels have it.

> The second name scratched on that wall is **Corin**.
>
> And in Meira's notes, in the shorthand only Garrick has been able to place, there is a line he puts his finger on: a family at a working mill, two days south-east.

> The Widow's Tooth is a day and a half behind you. Garrick agreed to come that far, and no further. He is shouldering a pack.

*⚙ A button: [POINT THAT OUT]*

> He settles the strap on his shoulder. *"Worst guests I've ever had. Good tippers, though."*
>
> Then he sets off down the road ahead of you, as if it had been his idea.

> Two days down the mill road, through country that gets emptier the further you go. First the farms thin out, then the fences. By the second afternoon there is only sheep-bitten grass, and a wind off the hills that never quite drops.
>
> At evening you come to the last farmhouse before the mill. An old man is splitting wood in the yard. He does not stop when he sees you coming. He only shifts his grip on the axe.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** He is splitting wood he does not need. The stack behind him would see out two winters. *(Adept and up:)* He wants that axe in his hand.

- **Agility:** There are fresh ruts in the lane, and he has not once looked at them. *(Adept and up:)* He knows whose they are.

- **Intellect:** A man afraid of strangers goes indoors. He has stayed where he can see the lane. *(Adept and up:)* It is not you he is watching for.

- **Charm:** There is a child's face at the window, and a hand that pulls it back. *(Adept and up:)* He has somebody to be careful for.

- **Craft:** The door-frame is older than the house: a beam from something that stood here first. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody built round it on purpose.

- **Aim:** From his chopping-block he can see a long way up the lane toward the mill. *(Adept and up:)* He chose where to stand.

**CHALLENGE: THE MAN WITH THE AXE**

> He saw something on this road, and he has decided not to have seen it.

*⚙ Pick one: Charm (get him talking), or Aim (watch where his eyes keep going, and look there first). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: he tells you to be on your way, and means it, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> He looks at you a long while over the axe. Then he sets it down, blade into the block, and waves you to the bench by his door. The wood of it is worn pale by years of sitting.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> He tells you to be on your way, and means it. It is Garrick who gets him talking in the end. He leans on the gate and offers a plain word about the weather, then the price of flour: the one subject no farmer can resist. By then the light has gone, and the old man says what he must and not a word over.

> It comes out a piece at a time.
>
> *"Three nights back. A cart went up the lane to the mill after dark. No lamps. No voices. Moving like they'd done it before."*
>
> *"Before dawn it came back down the same way, and turned upriver."*
>
> *"I've lived on this road sixty years. Nobody honest drives it in the dark."*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> And one thing more, which you did not have to ask for.
>
> *"One wheel on it was dragging. I'd know that cart again by the sound alone."*

> *No lamps. Moving like they'd done it before.*
>
> The boy at Fenmark said that, in nearly those words, a week ago and a whole county away.
>
> The same people.

> You are too late to stop it. But you know which way it turned.

> The beam over his door is older than the house. There is a loose board in it, where he swears nobody has looked in his lifetime.

*⚙ A button: [PRISE THE BOARD]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Behind it: something thin, and colder than the iron nails beside it.
>
> The old man watches you take it out. He does not ask what it is.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> He lets you sleep in his barn. It smells of hay and old apples, and the wind finds every gap in the boards. Garrick pours the old man's cider into one more cup than there are of you, and sets it on an upturned crate at the empty place.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **The mill is an hour on. At first light: what did the family see?**

*⚙ The day summary card:*

- **THE MILL ROAD** · Day 1
- You earned: [gold] · Charm or Aim [growth]
- You learned: the next name is **Corin**, the miller's boy. A cart with no lamps went up to the mill three nights ago and came back before dawn. It turned upriver. The same people who took Tamsin.
- *On a failure:* You missed something on the mill road. Someone at the fire may have seen it.
- Fragment: 1 found, locked
- Tomorrow: **the mill, at first light**

*⚙ Solved:*

> The old man says the horse never balked at his gate or at the fork, in full dark and with no lamp. It took the mill lane at a steady walk.

Clue found, "Past the gate":

> The ruts run past his gate without a check. By that gate, in plain sight of the lane, a child's wooden horse lies on its side in the grass.

## Week 2, Day 2

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> An hour to the mill, and the mist is still lying in the bottom of the valley like milk in a saucer. Porridge is on, with a pinch of salt, the way it should be. Eat it while it steams. Cold porridge is a sorry thing on a grey morning, and I made this one with care.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Tamsin went home to Fenmark on a carter's wagon, but first she told you what she heard while she was held: “The mill boy next.” The second name on the mine wall is Corin, and Meira's notes place his family at a working mill. Garrick, who had agreed to come only as far as the Widow's Tooth, shouldered a pack and came too. At the last farmhouse an old man told you a cart with no lamps went up to the mill three nights ago and came back before dawn, turning upriver: the same people who took Tamsin. The mill is an hour on; at first light, what did the family see?

### Your thread: the chapter, "The mill"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: what happened at the mill?*

> The mill sits an hour on, in a fold of the valley where the stream has been made to work. You hear it before you see it: the slow knock and splash of the wheel.
>
> The wheel is turning. That is almost worse than if it were not.
>
> Nobody comes out to meet you. Three things in the yard might speak before anybody does.

*⚙ A choice: [THE LATCH] or [THE LANE] or [THE WHEEL'S FOOTING]. Pick one; it plays, then the others follow.*

*⚙ If you pick [THE LATCH]:*

> It hangs by one nail. It was broken from outside, with a single push, by somebody who did not need to try twice.

*⚙ If you pick [THE LANE]:*

> Ruts in, and ruts out, four nights old. One cart. It did not turn round in the yard. Somebody led the horse about by its head, so as not to make a sound.

*⚙ If you pick [THE WHEEL'S FOOTING]:*

> The stone under the wheel-housing is far older than the mill built on it. Worn almost smooth into its face is a shape you know: the one on the burned rubbing. The one at the Widow's Tooth.

> Mei is still crouched in the lane. Dev has the rubbing held flat against the footing, and is not moving.

> A woman is standing in the mill door. She has been watching you since you came into the yard. There is flour worked deep into the creases of her hands: old flour, from before.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The grain sacks are stacked two high where they were three. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody here has had the heart to lift.

- **Agility:** She is standing where she can reach the door-bar without looking. *(Adept and up:)* She has practised that.

- **Intellect:** The table behind her is laid for three. *(Adept and up:)* She has not been able to stop.

- **Charm:** She looks at your hands before she looks at your faces. *(Adept and up:)* She is making sure you are not them.

- **Craft:** The wheel is turning, and there is nothing in the hopper. *(Adept and up:)* She keeps it running so that the house will not be quiet.

- **Aim:** From that doorway you can see the whole length of the lane. *(Adept and up:)* She watched the cart go.

**CHALLENGE: THE MILLER'S WIFE**

> She will not say a word until she is sure of you, and she has no reason to be.

*⚙ Pick one: Charm (get the rest of it gently), or Intellect (notice what she is not saying, and ask for exactly that). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: she stops talking, and it costs you the morning, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

> When she speaks at last, it is to the yard, not to you.
>
> *"My boy. Four nights gone. Big man, quiet, didn't even bother hiding his face — like he didn't care who saw."*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> She tells you the rest at once, as if it had been waiting behind her teeth.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> And there she stops. It takes the rest of the morning to earn it back. Arjun mends the latch, and nobody asks her anything at all. The sun is high before she says it, from the doorway, and she does not ask you in.

> *"He asked for my boy by name. And he knew the night he was born — knew it better than I did."*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> Then she opens her hand. In it is a horn button, with a twist of dark thread still through it.
>
> *"My boy got hold of his sleeve,"* she says. *"The man never even looked down."*

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> At the doorway, without a word, she presses a horn button into your hand, with a twist of dark thread still through it.

> Behind her the wheel goes round, and round, grinding nothing.
>
> They are not taking children.
>
> They are collecting particular ones.

**[PRESS ON]: THE RUTS**

*⚙ Offered only after a pass: walk the lane, and read the cart itself. 4 harder (DC 11). [PRESS ON] or [LEAVE IT]. Pass: +200g, and one more detail; fail costs nothing.*

*⚙ If you pressed on and passed:*

> The left wheel pulls. You have seen that track before, coming down a muddy bank to a ford.
>
> It is the same cart.

> In the wheel-housing, above the footing, where years of spray should have worn anything away:

*⚙ A button: [REACH IN]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Thin, and dense, and cold in a way that running water should long since have taken out of it.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> Garrick has said nothing all morning. On the way out he stops by the grain sacks, lifts one back up onto the stack where it used to be, and follows you down the lane.
>
> Upriver, the sky over the hills has thickened and come down low. The wind is getting up in the willows.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Upriver. The ruts are four nights old, and there is weather coming.**

*⚙ The day summary card:*

- You earned: [gold] · Charm or Intellect [growth]
- You learned: **Corin was asked for by name, and by the night he was born.** A big, quiet man took him four nights ago and did not hide his face. The mark from the rubbing is cut into the mill's footing.

## Week 2, Day 3

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Rain's been at it half the night, steady as a drum, and the ground outside has turned to soup. I've hung your socks along the fire like bunting. Warm, if not quite dry. Don't ask me whose are whose; I've stopped guessing.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> The mill wheel was still turning, and the old stone under it carries the shape from the burned rubbing. The miller's wife told you a big, quiet man took her boy Corin four nights ago and never hid his face; he asked for Corin by name, and by the night he was born. She pressed a horn button into your hand, with a twist of dark thread still through it. They are not taking any child who comes to hand; they are taking particular ones. The ruts go upriver, four nights old, and there is weather coming.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Upriver"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where did the cart go?*

> The rain came in the night. By morning the ruts are running with water, and you follow them upriver at a pace that leaves no breath for talk.
>
> The river has risen in the dark. It runs fat and loud beside you, and the rain still comes in fits off the hills, cold on the back of the neck.
>
> The tracks hold until the riverbank turns to stone. Then they stop.
>
> There is no ford here. There is no reason for a cart to have come this far.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The thorn on the left has been cut back and tied, not broken. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody tends it.

- **Agility:** No bird is singing on the slope above. *(Adept and up:)* Something up there has kept them off for days.

- **Intellect:** A cart does not vanish. It was unloaded here, or it went up. *(Adept and up:)* There is nowhere to unload.

- **Charm:** In the one patch of mud, a child's shoe-print, pointing uphill. *(Adept and up:)* He walked this part.

- **Craft:** The left wheel pulled. *(Adept and up:)* It left the bank at the grey slab.

- **Aim:** High on the slope there is a line where the bracken lies the wrong way. *(Adept and up:)* A path, seen from the side.

**CHALLENGE: WHERE THE TRACKS STOP**

> Somebody has been keeping a way open here without keeping it in sight.

*⚙ Pick one: Craft (read the cart's own marks, and find where it left the bank), or Agility (slip through the thorn, by its gaps, across what looks like an old path). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you find the path by walking up it, in plain view and making a great deal of noise, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You find it from above. You come down onto the path through the wet bracken, quietly, with the whole length of it laid out below you.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> It costs the afternoon. You come out onto the path at the bottom of it, scratched and filthy and cursing, and go up it the way the cart did.

> Where the path narrows between two rocks, there is a dog.
>
> It is the size of a calf. Its chain is long enough to reach both walls. The staple the chain hangs from is bright new iron, as new as the ruts.
>
> It does not bark. It has been taught not to. It only stands up, and watches you come.

**FIGHT: THE DOG ON THE CHAIN**

> There is no way round it.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 23 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 16 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. Who strikes first is set by today's Challenge: you on a pass, the enemy on a failure. A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: no gold (today's Challenge is the paid beat). At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If you strike first:*

> You came from above: you strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> It heard you on the path: it strikes first.

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> Its breath smokes in the wet air. It backs away to the end of its chain and lies down with its sides heaving, and lets you by. Its eyes follow you all the way past.

*⚙ If you lost the fight:*

> It has you down, and it does not let go until Mei and Arjun drag you through by the collar. Then it walks back to the middle of the path, and lies down across it again.
>
> Your sleeve is in rags. Your arm will remember its teeth for days.

> Beyond the dog, a man is lying on the path.
>
> Not the boy. A stranger, dead a week. Whoever keeps this path has been stepping over him.
>
> Garrick crouches beside him and turns the dead man's collar up over his face. It is the first kindness anybody has done him in a week.
>
> The dead man has a crude lockpick in one hand. It is still jammed into a small stone housing, built into the hillside itself.

> It is old work, far older than the path, as old as the places your fragments have lain. And it is sealed.
>
> It would not open for him.

*⚙ A button: [SET YOUR HAND TO IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> It gives at once, as if it had been waiting. The air inside is dry and still, and has not moved in a long time. What lies in it is cold.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> A pebble skitters down the slope above you.
>
> When you look up there is nothing there: only a branch, still swinging, and no wind.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Where was he trying to get to? The path goes on past him, and up.**

*⚙ The day summary card:*

- **UPRIVER** · Day 3
- You earned: [gold] · Craft or Agility [growth]
- You learned: the cart went onto **a hidden path into the hillside**, kept clear and guarded. A stranger died here trying to open an old stone lock, as old as the places your fragments lay. **It opened for you.**
- Fragments: 3 found
- Tomorrow: **further up, to wherever the path ends**

### The Seeker Fragment (in your Seeker Chest)

*⚙ Waiting in every friend's Seeker Chest from the morning; solved whenever you like, usually after the chapter or at the fire.*
