## Week 2, Day 4

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> The path climbs steeply from here, and every rock on it is still slick from the rain. Walk where I walk, slow and sure, with one hand free for the hillside. And if I slip, do me the kindness of stepping somewhere else.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> After a night of rain the ruts led upriver until the bank turned to stone, and a hidden path climbed into the hillside past a great dog on a new chain. Beyond it lay a stranger a week dead, his pick still jammed in an old stone lock built into the hill. The lock would not open for him. It opened at once for you, and there was a cold fragment inside. Somebody on the slope above was watching, and the path goes on past the dead man, and up.

### Your thread: the chapter, "What the hillside hides"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: what is at the end of the hidden path?*

> The path climbs for an hour through bracken and scree. Up here the wind has an edge to it, and it brings you lamp-smoke before it brings you anything else.
>
> The path ends at a hole in the hill.
>
> It is a shaft, squared off with timber, with a ladder going down into lamplight. There are voices at the bottom.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The ladder is new ash, pegged, and strong enough for men carrying a load. *(Adept and up:)* They mean to use this place for a long time.

- **Agility:** The third rung from the top is worn pale on the left. *(Adept and up:)* Everyone who climbs this steps in the same place.

- **Intellect:** Lamp-smoke, coming up steadily, and no cooking in it. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody lives down there. They work there.

- **Charm:** The voices are not arguing, and they are not laughing. *(Adept and up:)* They are saying the same thing, together.

- **Craft:** The shaft is old. The timbering is this year's. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody found it, and opened it again.

- **Aim:** Straight down: a lamp, a patch of floor, and the edge of something that is not floor. *(Adept and up:)* A foot. Small, and bare.

**CHALLENGE: DOWN THE SHAFT**

> One ladder. Whatever is down there, you will see it for as long as you are not seen.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (go down without a sound), or Charm (go down as if you belong, past the one posted at its foot). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you are seen from the top, and you see almost nothing, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You are halfway down before anyone looks up, and for the length of three breaths you see all of it. The air coming up past you is warm, and smells of tallow and wet stone.
>
> A chamber cut into the hill. A ring of lamps set on the floor. Hooded figures, more of them than there are of you, moving round the ring without hurry, setting things in their places.
>
> And in the middle, on his back on the stone, a boy. His wrists are tied. His hands lie open, palms up, and there is not a mark on them.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> A lamp swings up the shaft while you are still at the top, and a watchman's eyes meet yours.
>
> Behind him: lamplight, and hooded shapes, and nothing you could swear to. But under the voices there is another sound. A boy, crying, very quietly.

> He is alive.

> Then a shout, and every hooded head below turns.
>
> There is one ladder. One of you at a time, into their knives.
>
> You are driven back from the shaft, into the dark. Below you, somebody hauls the ladder away. A second lamp is lit at the foot of the shaft.
>
> They know you are here now.

> Garrick is the last to reach you. He sits down in the roots, breathing hard. *"That went about as well as it looked."*
>
> Somebody laughs, once, before they can stop it. It is the first laugh anyone has had in days.

> You are all flat among the roots at the shaft's mouth, getting your breath back, when your hand finds something in the dark.

*⚙ A button: [CLOSE YOUR FINGERS ON IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Rimed with frost, in the dark.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Is there another way into this hill, before they finish? You have tonight to find it.**

*⚙ The day summary card:*

- **THE SHAFT** · Day 4
- You earned: [gold] · Agility or Charm [growth]
- You learned: **Corin is alive.** He is held in a chamber cut into the hill, in a ring of lamps, by hooded figures who are making something ready. They know you are outside.
- *On a pass:* there are more of them than of you, and his hands are not yet cut. There is still time.
- *On a failure:* You missed something at the shaft. Someone at the fire may have seen it.
- Fragments: 4 found
- Tomorrow: **the hillside, tonight**

### The Seeker Fragment (in your Seeker Chest)

*⚙ Waiting in every friend's Seeker Chest from the morning; solved whenever you like, usually after the chapter or at the fire.*

> The _____ went out, and he went down alone into the oldest rooms of the house. When he came back up, his hand was _____, and he told us to sleep.

*⚙ Solved:*

> The moon went out, and he went down alone into the oldest rooms of the house. When he came back up, his hand was bleeding, and he told us to sleep.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The hillside by night"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who are these people, and what are they making ready?*

> Dark comes up out of the valley and over the hill. Far below, at the foot of the shaft, their lamp burns on.
>

*⚙ A choice: [WALK THE HILL] or [READ MEIRA'S PAGES]. Pick one; it plays, then the others follow.*

*⚙ If you pick [READ MEIRA'S PAGES]:*

> By the last of the light, in a margin, pressed hard into the paper:
>
> *"Not one of these fragments names him. Neither does anything else I can find — every record of him scraped clean. Whoever he was, someone wanted him forgotten."*

> Mei comes back off the hill with a shake of the head. Arjun is holding a page under the lamp with one finger on the margin.

> No fragment has ever given him a name. Where they speak of him at all, they say only *him*.

> Supper is whatever is left: hard bread, and the end of the farmer's cheese. Garrick's share ends up on somebody else's plate. He does not say where it went.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** In one place the ground rings hollow when you stamp. *(Adept and up:)* Not a door. A roof.

- **Agility:** Something is moving on this hill that is not one of you. *(Adept and up:)* It knows the ground better than you do.

- **Intellect:** They took their ladder away. *(Adept and up:)* Then they have another way out, or they do not mean to leave.

- **Charm:** Nobody has said the boy's name since the shaft. *(Adept and up:)* Everybody is thinking it.

- **Craft:** The stones of the sheepfold were cut once, and cut well. *(Adept and up:)* This hill was built on before it was grazed.

- **Aim:** Down at the shaft: two lamps now, where there was one. *(Adept and up:)* They have set a watch on you.

> A hooded figure comes up over the shoulder of the hill, alone, walking the way a man walks his own yard.

**FIGHT: THE ONE SENT UP TO LOOK**

> He has not come to talk.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 23 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 16 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. Who strikes first is set by your choice ([WALK THE HILL] lets the enemy strike first). A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: +300g. At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

>
> Sewn to it, no bigger than a thumbnail: a circle of black cloth.
>
> At the cuff, a snapped end of dark thread, the same twist as the thread still through the horn button from the mill.

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> You hit him hard enough to wind him, and still he made no sound. You heard his teeth come together on it.

> Before the dark takes him he stops, lifts a shuttered lamp, and opens it twice, toward the shaft.
>
> The hill knows you are still out here. Whatever is being made ready down there will not wait for you.

> He fought without a word. And then he ran.

> Where he stood to make his signal, the turf is worn through to an old cut stone, with a hollow in it the size of a hand.

>

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

Before the dark took him he opened a lamp twice toward the shaft. Now there is a clock: last week six cold slivers showed you a way nobody was watching, and you have five.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The other way in"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: is there another way in, and how long is left?*

> Your second night on the hill. Two lamps burn at the foot of the shaft, and the ladder has not come back.
>
> It is your watch. You lie on your belly at the lip, with the cold coming up out of the ground into your elbows.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** Under your palm the ground is trembling, very slightly. *(Adept and up:)* It is not the river.

- **Agility:** The two at the foot of the shaft change places every little while. *(Adept and up:)* They are tired. They have been at this for days.

- **Intellect:** They have not come up after you, and they have not put their lamps out. *(Adept and up:)* They are not afraid of you. They are only keeping you where you are.

- **Charm:** The chanting has started again. *(Adept and up:)* Whatever they were making ready is nearly ready.

- **Craft:** A sound comes up the shaft, and then the same sound comes up through the ground, a moment later. *(Adept and up:)* There is more hollow under this hill than one room.

- **Aim:** The lamplight at the bottom dims and brightens. *(Adept and up:)* People are crossing in front of it, and all of them the same way.

**CHALLENGE: READ THE HILL**

> You cannot see them. You can hear them, and you can watch their light.

*⚙ Pick one: Aim (count the shapes crossing the lamp, and place them), or Might (haul yourself up to the crack above the lamp, and look down into it). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you know only that there are many, and that they are busy, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> Shape by shape, you place them as they cross the light. You have them: a knot of them at the lamps, a steady file beyond, all going the same way. And one who does not move at all, and is small.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

## Week 2, Day 7: the Gathering

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> On your second night at the lip of the shaft the chanting started again, and the light below said they were nearly ready. The sixth fragment lay among the roots, not hidden at all, and the six made a map again: an older room, deeper in the same hill, with a shaft of its own. The way was read aloud twice across the dark, so that everyone on watch had it. Two hundred paces along, under a fall of old stone, you found that shaft's mouth unguarded; within the hour, far down in the rock, the picks began. They are digging toward the old room: be in it first.

### The Gathering (live, the whole party together in the party chat)

*⚙ That morning, in each friend's thread:*

> Tonight: the Gathering. The old shaft goes down to a room older than the mill, and they are digging toward it. Tonight calls on Intellect.

*⚙ Lobby at the time the party picked (poll on Day 4); starts once everyone is in, or any friend may start after 10 minutes, or by itself after 30. MYTHIOS posts the story a passage at a time; [GO ON] moves it on. About 20 to 30 minutes with the table talking.*

#### Arrival

> The map was right. Deeper in the hill than the working chamber where they drove you back, down a shaft of its own, there is a stair. It was cut so long ago that its edges have worn soft as soap. The air on it is cold, and has not been breathed in a long time.
>
> At its foot is a second room, older than the one above it, older than the mill, older than anyone's memory of this valley.
>
> You hear the chanting before you see the light. Low, even, patient: the same few words, over and over, too muffled to make out. It is not the sound of people in a hurry. It is the sound of people who have done this many times before.

#### The Approach

*⚙ A group vote (majority after 30 seconds; the vote shows who is best at each option): HOW DO YOU GO DOWN?*

1. **NOW, UNDER THE CHANT**: the chanting swallows the scrape of your boots.
2. **WAIT, AND COUNT**: hold at the stair's turn until you can set every voice in its place.

> Either way there is still the stair, with no rail and a long drop on the open side.

**THE APPROACH: THE SOFT STAIR**

*⚙ One friend steps up (whoever has rolled fewest Approaches) and rolls for the party, in front of everyone: Going now → Agility (take the stair without a slip); Having waited → Aim (mark each tread by the lamplight from below, and call the footing). DC 9 (+2 if the party solved the week's map). Pass: +500g · **and the party strikes first**. Fail: a boot goes out from under someone, and **they strike first**. The result sets who strikes first in the Battle.*

*⚙ If the Approach passed:*

> You reach the last step together, steady, blades already out.

*⚙ If the Approach failed:*

> You arrive in a clatter of steel on stone.

#### The Battle

> You come off the last step into lamplight.
>
> Four robed figures stand in a ring around a stone table, lamps at their feet, their shadows thrown huge and wavering up the walls. On the table lies the miller's boy. He is awake, with his wrists bound, and his eyes find yours over the shoulder of the one holding the knife.
>
> Nobody shouts. The robed figures simply turn, all at once, as if they had been expecting you. The chant does not even break.

**THE BATTLE: THE ROBED**

> **The chant:** while more than half of them are still standing, the chant holds, and each of them is harder to put down.

*⚙ Every friend fights their own The Robed at once (DEF 24, ATK 17), in lockstep rounds every 10 seconds; the fight rules are the weekday ones. Condition: The chant: while more than half of them are standing, each has DEF +1. A friend below 50 HP sends a help-call to the chat; a friend who has won joins another fight. The party wins when the last enemy falls: +520g each. A fallen friend is "DEFEATED." and stays for the night.*

*⚙ If the party strikes first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He strikes first.

> It is close and ugly: lamps kicked over, shadows lurching across the ceiling. And the strangest part is the chanting. The ones still standing keep the words going as they fight, and some keep them going as they fall, as if the words matter more than their lives.
>
> When the last of them goes down, the room goes enormously quiet.
>
> The boy is alive. There's a cut across his palm, clean and shallow, already bound in white linen — someone took the time to bind it. Whatever they needed from him, they'd already taken. The wound is small. The relief in the room is real. It looks, to everyone standing in it, like a win.

> Garrick reaches him first, kneeling, checking the bandage with careful hands, the same relief on his face as on everyone's: *"You saved that boy. Whatever else happened here, you saved him."*

> When the boy can talk again he does not tell you his name. You have had it all week. He asks for yours.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [TELL HIM YOUR NAME]*

> He says every name back under his breath, to keep it. Garrick's is last. *"Garrick,"* he says, and the boy says it back, twice.

> Behind the stone table, at chest height: another socket, cut with the same deliberate care — empty. The dust at its rim is barely disturbed. Whatever sat here left recently, not centuries ago.

> The rubbing, laid against it, fits, the way the first one did.

#### Notice

*⚙ Notice: each friend gets one private line for their highest Attribute (a fallen friend gets the "from the floor" line):*

- **Might:** The one you fought never once stepped away from the table. He was not defending himself. He was defending the place.
- **Agility:** There is a hole broken through the far wall, the rubble still sharp. They did not come down by your stair.
- **Intellect:** The knife has been wiped, and laid square to the table's edge. Nobody in this room was in a hurry.
- **Charm:** Corin watches every face. The one thing he will not look at is the wall behind the table.
- **Craft:** The linen on his hand is tied with a knot you have seen before. On Tamsin's.
- **Aim:** Beside the table, half trodden underfoot: a small book bound in black cloth, one corner smoking against a fallen lamp.
- **From the floor:** Down here the chant is still going. One of them, face down an arm's length from you, is mouthing it into the stone.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [LOOK CLOSER]*

*⚙ [LOOK CLOSER]: an optional roll on that Attribute (DC 9); a pass adds +75g and this line:*

- **Might:** Even falling, he fell toward it.
- **Agility:** The pick marks are a day old at most. They broke through just ahead of you.
- **Intellect:** And there is very little blood. A cut that shallow was all they wanted.
- **Charm:** When the lamplight swings that way, he shuts his eyes.
- **Craft:** It is not a knot anybody ties in haste.
- **Aim:** It has fallen open. On its first page: a black circle.

#### The black circle

> Somebody lifts the book out from under the fallen lamp and beats the sparks out of its corner. It is small, bound in black cloth, and still warm from the flame. The cloth is worn shiny in one place, where a thumb has held it a great many times.

> This belongs to *them*: their scripture, the kind of thing a man carries against his chest. On its first page, under a black circle painted like a moon with no light in it, is a title: *The First Book of the Order of the Dark Moon.*
>
> The scrap of cloth torn from the scout's coat, two nights ago on the hillside, is laid down beside it. The same circle.
>
> For a moment nobody says anything. The spilled lamps tick as they cool.
>
> The kidnappers have a name. And somewhere in these pages is the one they pray to.

#### The United Effort

> You have the black book in your hands when the far wall begins to sound.
>
> It comes from the hole they dug: boots, a great many, and lamplight growing in it like water rising in a pipe. They left with what they came for. Now they are coming back for what they left behind: their dead, and their book.
>
> There are more lamps in that tunnel than you can count. This is not a fight.
>
> But they cut that tunnel in a night and a day, through ground nobody had disturbed in a thousand years, and it is standing on green timber and hope. Take out the right prop, and the hill will shut it for you. Take out the wrong one, and the fall comes down on your side of it.
>
> Every fall changes what is holding what. So one of you looks, and calls it, and the nearest boot does the rest. Then the next of you looks again.

**THE UNITED EFFORT: THE BREACH**

*⚙ Two bars at 100 (60 for a friend alone): "The Tunnel" (the thing in the way) and "The Room" (the thing at risk). Friends take turns, nobody twice until all have gone. Each turn is a Intellect Challenge against DC 7. Pass: 10 + d20 off the thing in the way. Fail: 10 + d20 off the thing at risk. It ends when either bar hits 0. Won: +300g to every friend present. One Relic per roll (+4). The story lines it can say, as the bars fall:*

- **On a pass:**
  - *(early)* You see it: not the big prop, the little wedge under it. It goes with one kick, and a cart-load of hill sits down in the tunnel's mouth.
  - *(early)* The cross-piece. It was carrying nothing until the first fall; now it is carrying everything. Out it comes. The lamplight in the hole narrows to a slot.
  - *(middle)* Left-hand post, low down. The roof behind it comes down in a long soft rush, and the boots on the far side stop.
  - *(middle)* You call it, and you are right. Somewhere in the dust, on their side, a man is coughing.
  - *(late)* One frame is holding all of it now. You can see which leg.
  - *(late)* The slot is a hand wide. A lamp is pushed up to it from the other side, and an eye.
  - *(the end)* The last leg goes. The hill shuts like a book. The dust takes a long time to settle, and when it has there is no hole, no light, and no sound from the other side at all.
- **On a fail:**
  - *(early)* The big prop, the obvious one. It was holding up your side. A slab of the old wall leans out and comes down across the floor, and one of the lamps is under it.
  - *(early)* Wrong. The roof over the hole stays where it is. The roof over your heads lets go a barrow-load of stone.
  - *(middle)* The fall comes your way. When the dust thins, the far end of the room is rubble, and the stone table is the edge of what is left.
  - *(middle)* A crack runs across the ceiling from the hole to the stair, as quick as a lizard.
  - *(late)* There is room to stand, and not much more. Everything you have not got in your hands is under stone.
  - *(late)* The foot of the stair is going.
  - *(the end)* The room comes in.
- **Between turns:** Corin has put both hands over his ears. / On the stone table, the lamps the fight kicked over are guttering. / Whoever is coming is not hurrying. They have never needed to.

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> The room is yours.
>
> Somebody sets the spilled lamps upright again, one by one, and the dark draws back to the walls. Corin sits with his back against the stone table, watching the fall.
>
> The scorched corner of the book has fused a dozen leaves into one brittle black wafer. There is time, now, to get them apart whole.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> You are on the soft stair with the boy before you have decided to go, and what was the room is filling up behind you. The last thing you see in it is lamplight coming through the hole, and patient hands beginning to clear the stone.
>
> At the lip of the old shaft, in the night air, you count what you came out with. The black book is in three pieces inside somebody's coat. And Corin is holding something out to you with both hands: a book bound in plain oiled leather. He took it from the niche by the lamp as you carried him past.

#### The Verse of the Answering

> *From the First Book of the Order of the Dark Moon — The Verse of the Answering.*
>
> *Hear how the world nearly ended, and who would not let it.*
>
> *There was a war before this order had a name. It came out of the east like weather, and like weather it hated no one. It only arrived, and kept arriving.*
>
> *Where it passed, the towns burned, and the rivers below them ran grey with ash, and then red. Kings were raised against it and spent, like coin no one expected to see again. Cities older than their own songs went quiet in a single season. Every throne that stood against it fell. Every throne that knelt fell too, only more slowly.*
>
> *At the last there was one kingdom left, and one wall, and one king upon it.*

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"…one wall, and one king upon it."*
>
> From the farmhouse on the mill road

*⚙ The right row: "We held his last wall the night it was meant to fall. Not one of us expected to see morning."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [READ ON]*

> *He had opened his gates to every people the war had driven from their homes, until his city held what was left of the world inside it. His armies were a handful of tired men who still answered to their names — and he knew the name of every man on his wall. His allies were names cut into stone. His own household had packed what little they could carry, for a flight that had nowhere left to go.*
>
> *It is told that he walked that wall every night of the siege; that he did not sleep; that he did not weep where anyone could see. It is told that the enemy's fires outnumbered the stars. And it is told that on the last evening their herald rode up to the gate and named the hour of the city's ending — dawn — and that the king heard him out, and thanked him, and sent him home with bread.*
>
> *He had been asked to kneel. He would not.*
>
> *And that night, while the city waited for its last dawn, the moon went dark.*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [READ ON]*

> *His house had kept a thing since before his house had a name. No one living could say what it was. It was old; it was not to be sold, or lost, or given away; that was all the keeping had ever passed down. That, and a few pages in a hand no one could read whole — of which, that night, by the light of a single candle, he could read one line:*
>
> *Here the page changes. One line is set apart, in an older hand: words older than the Order's own name, and nothing like the plain, careful hand the Seekers left in every fragment.*
>
> **"Blood beneath the dark moon — and it answers."**
>
> *So the king went down alone into the oldest room of his house, with the moon gone out above him and the enemy's fires waiting for morning. He set the thing on the stone before him. He opened his palm with his own knife. He had nothing left to lose, and he said so aloud, and he gave it his blood.*
>
> *It answered.*
>
> *And what answered him went out of that room, over the wall, and into the dark where the enemy's camp lay waiting for dawn.*

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"He opened his palm with his own knife."*
>
> From the roots at the shaft's mouth, the night they drove you back

*⚙ The right row: "The moon went out, and he went down alone into the oldest rooms of the house. When he came back up, his hand was bleeding, and he told us to sleep."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [READ ON]*

> *No one who stood on the wall has left us their telling. Ours comes from his own house — from those who watched from the high windows while the city slept, and told it after, and never told it any other way. In the dark, the enemy's camp rose: every man of it, all at once, the way a field of birds lifts at a single sound. They did not arm. They did not cry out. They walked out past their own fires into the night, and they did not stop. By morning the fires were cold, the tents stood empty, and the horses wandered loose in the fields. No one ever found where the men had gone.*
>
> *The city that had been told to expect its ending climbed onto its walls and looked out on nothing, and wept. It is told that they wept for him that morning, every one of them, for joy. That was the morning of the answering. The war that had eaten the world was broken, and the world, which had been ending, did not end.*
>
> *And the world he saved took back his name. It scraped it from its stones and burned it from its books, and taught its children the story of that war with him cut out of it.*

> You know this war. Every child in the land does. You learned it on a bench with a slate on your knees, the way everyone did. *The Grey Host broke at Highwall of a fever in its camp, and Highwall was spared by luck.* That is how you were taught it.
>
> A fever. Luck. No king anywhere in it.
>
> And in Meira's notes, two nights ago, pressed hard into the paper: *"Not one of these fragments names him. Neither does anything else I can find — every record of him scraped clean. Whoever he was, someone wanted him forgotten."*
>
> The Order says no one who stood on that wall left a telling. Your first fragment begins: *"We held his last wall the night it was meant to fall."*
>
> Somebody did leave one. They hid it in a beam, behind a loose board.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [READ ON]*

> *So the faithful remember him, and give the marked blood in his name.*
>
> *Until the throne rise whole.*

> *Until the throne rise whole.* Those were the words under the chant, too muffled to make out from the stair. It is what they were saying over Corin, while the knife was out.

#### The ledger

> It is Corin who shows you. Still shaking, he points past the table to a niche in the wall, where a lamp still burns beside another book. It is nothing like the Order's scripture. This one is bound in plain oiled leather, the kind a merchant keeps his accounts in.
>
> It is a ledger.
>
> Page after page of children's names. Beside each name, a place. Beside each place, a date, and the miles to the next. The newest pages are in one small, careful hand. The oldest are in other hands, faded almost to nothing. And beside many of the old names, a single word, written the same way every time: *given.*

> *given.*

> The newest page has four names on it. They are the four from the wall in the mine. Two are the children you've saved: Tamsin, the girl from Fenmark, and Corin, the boy beside you now. The other two you haven't found yet.

#### The turn

> The miller's wife, at her own door: *"He knew the night he was born — knew it better than I did."*

> The sliver from the well at Fenmark: *"Born when the dark has eaten the moon, marked before the first cry's through."*

> And Meira's page, the one that names all of you. Beside each name she wrote a date: the night each of you was born.

*⚙ A question to the table (anyone answers in the chat; MYTHIOS never grades it):*

> *What do these nights have in common?*

*⚙ After sixty quiet seconds:*

> *Read the well's sliver again.*

*⚙ After ninety, the answer:*

> Every one of them is a night the moon went dark.

> Meira's page lies open beside the ledger. Your names, in her neat hand, and beside each one the night you were born.
>
> A week ago, in a dry room under the river, you asked why your names were on her list.

> **You're marked too. Every one of you.**

> Nobody says anything for a long moment. Somebody turns a hand over in the lamplight and looks at the open palm.

#### Ask

> **You may ask Garrick.** Each of you, one question.

*⚙ ASK: a board of questions; each friend taps one. Sealed questions the night needs are answered after two quiet minutes if nobody asks them.*

- **DO YOU KNOW HIS NAME?**: *"Malakor."* Asked how: *"My grandmother taught me that name."*
- **HAVE YOU HEARD OF THIS ORDER?**: *"I know the name."* Asked how: *"My family's been in these hills longer than the roads have."*
- **DO YOU KNOW WHAT THE THING WAS?**: *"I know what that page says."*
- **WHY DON'T THEY TAKE US? WE'RE MARKED TOO**: *"Look at the ledger. Every name in it is a child's."*

#### The Cliffhanger

> Garrick reads the ledger over someone's shoulder, and for the first time since you have known him he goes quiet in a different way: slower, more careful, like a man choosing his words.
>
> *"This hand's been keeping books like this for years,"* he says at last. *"This isn't new, and it isn't finished. Two places. Two children."*
>
> He closes the ledger and tucks it inside his coat, the same easy way he keeps everything else.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [ASK HIM FOR IT]*

> He hands it straight over. *"Here. Read every page."*

> Corin is still watching the place where the ledger went. When he speaks, it is barely a whisper.
>
> *"There was a girl down here with me. They took her away, not long before you came."* He swallows. *"She said they told her she was going somewhere warm."*

# Week 3: The Fire Stone

*⚙ This week: weekday DC 7; weekday enemy DEF 24, ATK 17; the Gathering calls on Agility. The week's question at the fire: "What do they need Wren for?"*

## Week 3, Day 1

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Down the old shaft and a stair worn soft with age, you came to the oldest room under the mill hill, where robed figures chanted round a stone table with Corin on it. You fought them, and Corin is alive, but his palm was already cut and bound in white linen: whatever they needed from him, they had taken. Behind the table was another socket, cut with the same care and empty, the dust at its rim barely disturbed, and the burned rubbing fitted it. Beside the table lay their scripture, a small black book with a black circle on its first page, the same circle as the scrap from the scout's coat. It tells of a war that ate the world, and of one king on the last wall who would not kneel, and who gave his own blood on the night the moon went dark. The world he saved took back his name. His faithful remember him, and give the marked blood in his name. In a niche by a lamp lay a ledger of children's names, places and dates, and its newest page held the four names from the mine wall. The marked are those born on a night the moon went dark, as Corin was. Meira wrote your birth nights beside your names, and they are dark-moon nights too: you are marked, every one of you. Garrick read the ledger, tucked it inside his coat, and said, “This hand's been keeping books like this for years. This isn't new, and it isn't finished. Two places. Two children.” Then Corin told you about a girl held down there with him, taken away not long before you came. She said they told her she was going somewhere warm.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Somewhere warm"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: a girl was held here too, and moved "somewhere warm". Who is she, and where is that?*

> *Somewhere warm.*
>

>
> A pause.
>
> *"She said they told her she was going home first."*

> The ledger is opened to its newest page, and held flat under the lamp. Four names. Beside the third, in that same small, careful hand: *Wren — Ashcombe.*
>
> In Meira's satchel the same name turns up in her own neat hand, on her list of children she was worried for. Beside it is a knot of her shorthand. Garrick tilts the page to the light and reads the knot off without a pause.
>
> *"Ashcombe's a shepherds' village on the edge of the burned country,"* he says. *"A long day west. And above it she's marked a place. The Blackglass."*

- **Charm:** Corin has not let go of somebody's sleeve since you came out of the hill. *(Adept and up:)* He is afraid you will not be there when he looks.

### The last light (Fan Out)

**Lead: Corin, on the walk down**: Charm (walk beside him and let him talk), or Aim (look him over in the morning light)

Clue found, "The grey wool":

> Corin has a child's woollen mitten tucked in his belt that is not his. The wool is rough and grey, and it smells of sheep.

**Lead: where the cart stood, below the shaft**: Craft (read what the cart left in the grass), or Intellect (work out what they loaded)

## Week 3, Day 2

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> A long day west today, out to where the green hills give up and the burned country begins. There's honey for the oats this morning; I found a jar at the bottom of the pack. Take a second spoon. The jar's deep, and the road is long.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Corin remembered the girl who was held with him. Her name was Wren, her home was always warm, and they told her she was going home first. The ledger and Meira's list both named her village, Ashcombe, on the edge of the burned country, a long day west. Before dawn one of them came up out of the hill alone, fought you without a word and fled, and below the shaft a cart and a child's small prints turned west. You carried Corin home to the mill, and he told his mother every one of your names. Why would anyone take a stolen child back to where she was born?

### Your thread: the chapter, "Ashcombe"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: did Wren come home?*

> On the second morning the west road climbs, and the grass along it grows thin and brown. Somewhere on the way the wind begins to smell of old smoke, though nothing is burning anywhere you can see.
>
> Ashcombe sits where the green hills give out and the black ones begin: low stone houses, roofs weighted with slabs against the wind. The ground is warm underfoot, like a hearthstone the morning after. The springs run hot, and steam lifts off the trough by the first house.
>
> Every door in the village shuts as you come up the street. Strangers took a child from here two weeks ago. You are strangers.
>
> One door is the last to shut. Behind it, nobody moves away.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** Every roof here is held down with stones a man could barely lift. *(Adept and up:)* These are people used to holding on to things.

- **Agility:** A curtain moves in every house but one. *(Adept and up:)* In that one, somebody is standing right behind the door.

- **Intellect:** Two weeks since a child was taken, and nobody is out searching. *(Adept and up:)* They were told something that made searching seem useless.

- **Charm:** The last door to shut, shut slowest. *(Adept and up:)* She wanted a longer look at you.

- **Craft:** The well-rope is new, and the bucket is old. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody here still mends things. This is not a village that has given up.

- **Aim:** At the end of the street the green stops, as if it had been cut with a knife. *(Adept and up:)* After that, everything is black.

**CHALLENGE: THE LAST DOOR**

> She is on the other side of it, and she has not walked away.

*⚙ Pick one: Charm (talk to her through the door until she opens it), or Intellect (know what will open it: the ledger's page, and her daughter's name in a stranger's hand). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: the door stays shut, and you lose the day's light waiting, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> A bolt draws back. The door opens a hand's width and stays there while she looks at you, one face at a time.
>
> Then it opens the rest of the way, and she stands back to let you in. The house smells of peat, and of bread that nobody has eaten.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> The door stays shut. The light goes off the street, and the shutters stay closed all down it.
>
> But at dusk she comes out to your fire herself, with a shawl over her head, because nobody else in the world is looking for her daughter. You never see past her door.

*⚙ A choice: [WHO TOOK HER?] or [WHY HER?] or [WHAT DID THEY SAY?]. Pick in any order until every branch has been seen.*

*⚙ If you pick [WHO TOOK HER?]:*

> *"Two of them. Grey coats, good boots. Polite. They asked for her by name, and they knew the night she was born."*

*⚙ If you pick [WHY HER?]:*

> She looks at the ground. *"The night the moon went out. Here, a baby born on a night like that is given a chip of black glass off the hill, to keep the dark off. The old women pressed one into her fist before she'd even cried."*
>
> She almost smiles. *"She never lost it. She's picked up every chip she's found since. Her pockets rattle with them."*
>
> The smile goes. *"It didn't keep them off."*
>
> You know that night. Every one of you was born on one like it.

*⚙ If you pick [WHAT DID THEY SAY?]:*

> *"He said they'd no wish to keep her. That they'd only need her the once."*
>
> She says it the way you would repeat something you cannot make sense of.
>
> *"He looked me in the eye when he said it. I've been lied to by better men than him. That wasn't what this was."*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> On the sill by the door, chips of black glass are set out in a row, smallest to largest, the way a child sets things out.
>
> There is dust on the sill. There is none on the chips.

> At the well, a boy is selling polished chips of black glass off a board. He has been watching you the whole time. When the grown-ups have gone in he holds one out, and does not ask for the coin.
>
> Then, in a whisper, with one eye on the doors, he tells you what he saw. He was up after dark last night, where he should not have been. A cart came through the village without lamps. It did not stop. At the foot of the black road it was left standing empty, and whoever was in it went on up, on horses, toward the Blackglass.
>
> Nobody has told her mother.

> If Wren was in that cart, she passed within a mile of her own door.

> Garrick knows the black road. *"Where it forks, the shepherds take the left,"* he says, and when he catches somebody looking at him, he nods up at the black hills. *"My family's been in these hills longer than the roads have."*

> At the edge of the village the pound wall, where the shepherds pen their flocks, is built on older stones than any house in Ashcombe: huge, square-cut, set without mortar. They are as warm as bread. All but one gap.

*⚙ A button: [REACH INTO THE GAP]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> The only cold thing in Ashcombe. Cold enough to ache the fingers.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> Above the last roof the black road goes up into the hills. As the evening comes on, the warm ground under the village ticks softly, cooling.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **What does anyone need a child for, once? The Blackglass: half a day up the black road, the place Meira marked.**

## Week 3, Day 3

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> The ground here keeps its warmth all night, like a hearth banked for the morning. Best bed I've had in a week, and nobody charged me for it. Fill your flasks at the hot spring before we go. The black road is a dry one, and the water here comes out ready for tea.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> In Ashcombe, Wren's mother told you that two polite men in grey coats had taken her two weeks ago. They asked for her by name, and they knew the night she was born, a night the moon went out. One of them said they would only need her the once, and he looked her in the eye when he said it. The night before you came, a cart with no lamps carried Wren past her own door and up the black road. Garrick knew that road. “My family's been in these hills longer than the roads have.” Half a day up it lies the Blackglass, the place Meira marked. What does anyone need a child for, once?

### Your thread: the chapter, "The Blackglass"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: why the Blackglass? What is up there?*

> Half a day up the black road, where the shepherds take the left fork, the hill opens out and you see it.
>
> The Blackglass is a whole shoulder of hillside where the rock once ran like wax, and cooled: smooth, black, faintly rippled, as if a wave of stone had frozen halfway down the slope.
>
> Nothing grows on it. The wind slides off it without a sound. The shepherds do not walk their flocks across it, and do not say why.
>
> Meira's page points to its far edge.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The glass is warm through the soles of your boots. *(Adept and up:)* The heat is coming from underneath.

- **Agility:** It is rippled, like sand under shallow water. *(Adept and up:)* The ripples all run one way. There is a grain to it.

- **Intellect:** No lichen. No moss. Not one seed. *(Adept and up:)* Whatever burned this did more than burn it.

- **Charm:** The shepherds never told you not to cross. *(Adept and up:)* They only stopped talking when you asked.

- **Craft:** Glass like this takes the heat of a furnace. *(Adept and up:)* A whole hillside of it, all at once.

- **Aim:** On all that smooth black there is one mark that is not a ripple. *(Adept and up:)* On the far side, just above the crack.

**CHALLENGE: CROSS THE GLASS**

> It is slick as ice, and as warm as skin.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (straight over, and fast), or Aim (pick out the safe line from the edge, and take it slowly). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you go down hard, and slide, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You reach the far side clean, without once putting a hand to the glass. All the way over, a dark shape walks upside down beneath your boots: your own, in the black.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> You go down hard, and slide twenty feet before a ripple in the glass catches you. For a moment you lie still, with your cheek on the warm glass and your heart going. Bruised, shaken, and there all the same.

> At the far edge the glass takes its toll. Where it has cracked, its edges are as sharp as knives, and you come off it with one boot split open along the sole.
>
> Garrick crosses last, the slow way, testing every step with the toe of his boot before he trusts it.
>
> *"My knees have opinions about this glass. None of them good."*
>
> He lowers himself onto the first rock he comes to, and rubs them.

> Worn into the glass is a shape you know.
>
> Beside it, set into the rock as if the glass had cooled around it, is a housing of carved stone: old work, cut with the same care as the lock the dead man could not open, on the path upriver.

*⚙ A button: [SET YOUR HAND TO IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> It gives at the first touch. The glass round it holds the sun's warmth; the housing is a cold spot in it, and what is inside is colder still.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> Three children. Three marks.
>
> Tamsin, at Fenmark, under the Widow's Tooth. Corin, at the mill. Wren, at Ashcombe, under the Blackglass.
>
> The places Meira marked and the children in the ledger are one list.

*⚙ A button: [WRITE IT IN HER NOTEBOOK]*

> It goes into Meira's notes, under her last entry, in your own hand. Your letters look clumsy under hers.

> Hoof-scratches cross the glass a few yards from the mark, and run on, deeper into the burned country.
>
> The riders did not even slow.

**[PRESS ON]: READ THE SCRATCHES**

*⚙ Offered only after a pass: follow them to the edge of the glass. 4 harder (DC 11). [PRESS ON] or [LEAVE IT]. Pass: +200g, and one more detail; fail costs nothing.*

*⚙ If you pressed on and passed:*

> Where the horses came off the glass the scratches bunch and deepen, and you can count them.
>
> Six horses. One of them is carrying double, and the second rider weighs almost nothing.

> You camp in the lee of the glass, where the ground is warm enough to sleep on.
>
> That evening, on Meira's plain page about the Blackglass, there is a line you all know, copied out in her hand: the first fragment any of you ever opened, *"Five fell…"* And squeezed into the margin beside it:
>
> *"Five, the fragment says. Every other record I can find says four. I cannot find a fifth anywhere, and I have looked. A poet's count, perhaps — a number that sounded better than the truth."*

> In the morning your split boot is whole again: the sole stitched back on, tight and neat, with waxed thread, while you slept. Garrick is winding what is left of the thread back onto its bobbin.

*⚙ A button: [THANK HIM]*

> *"Needed doing."*
>
> He tucks the bobbin away inside his coat, and that is the end of it.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **They rode straight past the one thing Meira marked. So what are they riding to? On, into the black ridges.**

- You learned: **three children, three marks.** Every child was taken from beside one of the places Meira marked. The riders passed this one without slowing.
- Fragments: 3 found

## Week 3, Day 4

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> There's ash on the air this morning, fine as flour and none of it fit for bread. It settles on your sleeves and in the creases of your hands. Tie a cloth over your mouth before we set off. It makes the walking easier, and the conversation is none the worse for it.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You crossed the Blackglass, a hillside where the rock once ran like wax, and split a boot on its far edge. Beside a shape you knew, an old stone housing opened at your touch. Tamsin, Corin and Wren were each taken from beside a place Meira marked, and you wrote it into her notebook: three children, three marks, one list. That evening you found her note in the margin beside the first fragment's “Five fell”: “Five, the fragment says. Every other record I can find says four. I cannot find a fifth anywhere, and I have looked.” By morning Garrick had mended your boot, and the riders had passed the mark without slowing. What are they riding to, out in the black ridges?

### Your thread: the chapter, "The black ridges"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: is she still alive?*

> The burned country is ridge after ridge of black rock, under a sky that looks paler for it. The soil between the ridges is ash-grey, and crunches like old snow. Dead trees stand bone-white, still upright after nobody knows how long.
>
> No bird sings out here. By noon your own footsteps are the loudest thing for miles.
>
> You are walking point when you stop, and crouch.
>
> In one of the hoof-scratches lies a chip of black glass, worn smooth by a thumb.

*⚙ A button: [PICK IT UP]*

> You turn it over in your palm. It is no bigger than a fingernail: the kind of thing a child carries for luck.
>
> Fifty paces on, there is another.
>
> She is dropping them.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The dead trees are still standing. *(Adept and up:)* Nothing here has rotted. Nothing has lived here to rot them.

- **Agility:** Each chip lies where a horse slowed for a rise. *(Adept and up:)* She picks her moments.

- **Intellect:** She has a pocketful, and a long way to go. *(Adept and up:)* She is spacing them. She is counting.

- **Charm:** The men who have her have not noticed a child's hand opening and closing. *(Adept and up:)* She has made herself very easy to overlook.

- **Craft:** That ridge ahead has a corner on it. *(Adept and up:)* Rock does not weather square.

- **Aim:** A hundred yards off the riders' line there is a shadow that is too straight. *(Adept and up:)* Nothing out here is straight unless somebody made it so.

> It is a doorway, cut into the side of a ridge: squared, deliberate, half buried under a fall of rock. The riders went by a hundred yards away, and never turned their heads.

*⚙ A choice: [KEEP TO THE TRAIL] or [GO TO THE DOORWAY]. Pick one; only the picked branch plays.*

*⚙ If you pick [KEEP TO THE TRAIL]:*

> You stay on the hoof-scratches for another mile, and find a third chip, and bring it back. Mei and Arjun are already at the fall of rock, with their sleeves rolled.

*⚙ If you pick [GO TO THE DOORWAY]:*

> You go to the fall of rock with the others and stand looking at it. Dev keeps on along the trail, and comes back within the hour with a third chip in hand.

**CHALLENGE: THE BURIED DOORWAY**

> One wrong stone, and the rest of the fall comes with it.

*⚙ Pick one: Craft (work out which stones can come away), or Might (shift the biggest of them, and trust the rest to hold). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: half the fall comes down, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> Stone by stone the fall comes away, and what is left above it holds. A thin trickle of grit runs down, and stops. The way opens cleanly.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> Half the fall comes down with a roar and a choking cloud of black dust. When it clears, the doorway is open, and you are all the same colour.

> Inside is a room cut from the ridge. Old soot blackens its ceiling, from fires that went out centuries ago. Cut into the walls at sleeping height, facing each other across the floor, is a row of niches, each just long enough for a person to lie in. The air in here is cool, and smells of nothing at all.
>
> For a moment nobody goes any further in.
>
> Whoever wrote your fragments slept here, walking this same country, a long time ago.
>
> In a crack in the nearest niche is a single die, carved from bone, its spots worn almost smooth by somebody's fingers.
>

>
> Between the niches, at chest height, is a housing.

## Week 3, Day 5

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Up before the light, all of you, quiet as you can. I've wrapped bread and a little cheese for the walk, so nobody has to stop to eat, and nobody can say they went hungry. Boots on, laces tight. The ridges are loose underfoot in the dark.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> In the black ridges you found a chip of black glass in a hoof-scratch, and another fifty paces on. Wren is dropping them, and the men who have her have not noticed. A hundred yards off the riders' line, a buried doorway opened on a room cut from the ridge, where whoever wrote your fragments once slept. A bone die lay in one of its niches, and a fragment waited in a housing between them. At dusk you saw smoke from a hut to the north-east, and that night your own fire in the old doorway showed just as plainly. She is alive, and she is thinking. The hut, at first light.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The shepherd's hut"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: is she in the hut?*

> First light finds you on the last ridge above it. You can hear the spring before you see it: the first running water since Ashcombe.
>
> In a fold of the ridges a spring runs out of the black rock, and round it the world remembers how to be green: grass, a stand of trees, and a low stone hut with a sod roof.
>
> There are two ways down to it.

*⚙ A choice: [THE RIDERS' LINE] or [WREN'S LINE]. Pick one; only the picked branch plays.*

*⚙ If you pick [THE RIDERS' LINE]:*

> The hoof-scratches go the long way, round the head of the fold. You follow them in, in the open. Dev takes the goat-track that her chips mark, and comes down it with five more in hand.

*⚙ If you pick [WREN'S LINE]:*

> Her chips mark something shorter: a goat-track straight down through the rocks, which nobody on a horse could take. You find five more of them on the way. She walked this part.

> On the ridge above the hut a grey coat stands up from among the rocks, where he was left behind to watch.
>
> He comes down to meet you, and he takes his time about it.

**FIGHT: THE GREY COAT ON THE RIDGE**

> Polite, and in good boots.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 24 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 17 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. Who strikes first is set by your choice ([WREN'S LINE] lets the enemy strike first). A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: +300g. At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If you strike first:*

> You came by her track: you are on him before he has seen you.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> You came by the open line: he has been watching you for some time.

> He goes back up the way he came, without a word, onto the bare black rock, and the rock takes no prints. By the time you reach the top of the rise there is nothing on the ridge but the wind.

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> He goes without his hat. It is a good hat. Inside the band somebody has stitched two letters, very small, the way a mother would.

> The hut is empty.
>
> The door stands open. A thread of smoke is still going up from the roof-hole into the morning.

*⚙ A choice: [LOW ON THE DOORFRAME] or [THE BEAM OVER THE PALLET] or [THE FIRE]. Pick in any order until every branch has been seen.*

*⚙ If you pick [LOW ON THE DOORFRAME]:*

> Scratched fresh into the wood, at a child's height, with something small and sharp: **WREN.**
>
> The letters are small and pressed hard, and each one has been gone over twice. You run your thumb down them. The wood is still pale in the cuts.

*⚙ If you pick [THE FIRE]:*

> A pot is still on it, and catching. A tin cup lies on its side where it was dropped. There is a blanket, folded with care, and beside it a second one, thrown down.
>
> They were here within the hour. And they left at a run.

*⚙ If you pick [THE BEAM OVER THE PALLET]:*

> Cut deep and neat into the old beam over the pallet, in a grown hand, dark with years of smoke:
>
> *Until the throne rise whole.*
>
> The words that ended the Order's scripture under the mill hill. The words they chanted over Corin while the knife was out. Whoever sleeps here has said them every night for a long time.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The door was not pulled shut behind them. It was left swinging. *(Adept and up:)* They did not stop to close it.

- **Agility:** By the spring, the grass is still lifting where it was trodden. *(Adept and up:)* Not long. Not long at all.

- **Intellect:** A folded blanket and a dropped cup, in the same room. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody careful, who stopped being careful all at once.

- **Charm:** The pallet is by the fire: the warm place. *(Adept and up:)* They gave her the best bed in the house.

- **Craft:** The sod roof has been patched in three different years. *(Adept and up:)* This hut is kept. Somebody comes back to it.

- **Aim:** High on the black rock, a scuff of pale dust where a boot turned. *(Adept and up:)* Then nothing.

> Until today, these people have never once hurried.

> From the doorway you can see straight back across the ridges to the one you slept under last night.
>
> They saw your fire.

> Garrick crouches by the hearth and lays his palm flat over the ashes. *"They're always a step ahead of us."*

> The hut is newer than what it stands on. Under the hearthstone the footing is older, bigger stonework. Set into it:

*⚙ A button: [BRUSH YOUR HAND ACROSS IT]*

> The fire's heat is still in the stone above. What is inside is as cold as well-water.
>

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

## Week 3, Day 6

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> The highest ridge is a long climb, and the cairn on top is further than it looks from here. Short steps and steady breath. Stop when you need to, and drink when you stop. I walk at the back today. Somebody has to admire the view.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Five more of Wren's chips led you down to a shepherd's hut beside a spring. A grey coat left on watch came down at you, then fled over bare rock that takes no prints. The hut was empty. WREN was scratched fresh into the doorframe at a child's height, and cut long ago into the beam over her bed were the Order's words, *Until the throne rise whole.* They had been there within the hour and left at a run, because from that doorway they could see your fire. “They're always a step ahead of us,” Garrick said. Now it is a step and no more. The cairn on the highest ridge, before the light goes.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The cairn"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where have they taken her?*

> All afternoon the cairn has been in sight: a dark knuckle of piled stones far off on the skyline, that never seems to come any nearer. Every ridge you cross shows you another between you and it. The shadows are long now, and reaching.
>
> The cairn stands on the highest ridge in the burned country, and the light is going.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The lowest stones of that cairn are as big as sheep. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody carried those up there for a landmark.

- **Agility:** The wind is rising as the light goes. *(Adept and up:)* In an hour that ridge will be no place to stand.

- **Intellect:** The Blackglass, the buried room and the hut lie in a line. *(Adept and up:)* A line points somewhere.

- **Charm:** Nobody has said her name all day. *(Adept and up:)* There are eight of her chips among you.

- **Craft:** One hill out there is too round. *(Adept and up:)* The others were worn down. That one was melted.

- **Aim:** Over one hill, and only one, the air is moving like the air over a forge. *(Adept and up:)* Something under that hill is still hot.

**CHALLENGE: THE CLIMB**

> There is an hour of light, and more than an hour of ridge.

*⚙ Pick one: Might (straight up the face), or Agility (along the knife-edge, where it is shorter and worse). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 7: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 8+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you get there in the dark, and see nothing but what you carry, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You come up beside the cairn with the sun still on the ridges, and the whole burned country is spread out under you: the Blackglass shining dully to the west, the green fold of the hut far below.
>
> And one hill unlike all the others. Round, and black, with a crack across its crown, where the air above it shimmers as if the hill were breathing.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> The last of the ridge you climb by feel, with the wind pulling at your coat and the rock still warm under your hands.
>
> It is full dark when you reach the cairn. There is nothing to see from it but black on black, and the stars.

> The last fragment is at the cairn's foot: not hidden, only wedged between the lowest stones, where a careless boot would miss it.

*⚙ A button: [TAKE IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**
>
> That is six.

*⚙ A button: [LAY THEM OUT]*

*⚙ A button: [TURN IT TO THE SKYLINE]*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> *"The Kiln,"* Garrick says. *"That's the shepherds' name for it."*

> In through the crack. Down a way cut long ago. To a room beneath the hill.

> Mei came up the last of the ridge at your side. Whoever is still on the slope below is too far down to see what you hold.

*⚙ A choice: [READ IT OUT] or [LET Mei READ IT]. Pick one; the choice colours the moment, the story is the same.*

> The cold comes up fast once the sun is gone. Garrick builds the fire up, and gives whoever is shivering hardest the warm side of it.

> Not an hour after full dark, far below: a single lamp, moving across the black ground toward the Kiln.
>
> It reaches the foot of the hill. It climbs. And it goes out.
>
> Not blown out. Gone in.

## Week 3, Day 7: the Gathering

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You raced the light to the cairn on the highest ridge of the burned country, and found the sixth fragment wedged at its foot. The six made a map once more. It ended at a round black hill with a crack across its crown, which Garrick said the shepherds call the Kiln. The way was said aloud, twice, for the ones on the slope below: in through the crack, and down a way cut long ago to a room beneath the hill. Not an hour after full dark, a single lamp crossed the black ground, climbed the Kiln, and went in. Whatever happens under that hill has started. Into the Kiln, tonight.

### The Gathering (live, the whole party together in the party chat)

*⚙ That morning, in each friend's thread:*

> Tonight: the Gathering. The map goes in through the crack in the Kiln's crown. A lamp went in before you. Tonight calls on Agility.

*⚙ Lobby at the time the party picked (poll on Day 4); starts once everyone is in, or any friend may start after 10 minutes, or by itself after 30. MYTHIOS posts the story a passage at a time; [GO ON] moves it on. About 20 to 30 minutes with the table talking.*

#### Arrival

> The burned country, all week. Tonight the trail ends under it.
>
> You come to the Kiln in the dark, by the map. Close to, it is bigger than it looked from the cairn: a round black hill with nothing growing on it, warm through your boots all the way up. The lamp that went in before you is nowhere to be seen.
>
> At the crown the crack opens under your feet, wide enough for a man to climb down. The air coming up out of it is warm as breath, and smells of hot stone.
>
> Down there, a girl is crying. Quietly. The way children cry when they have learned it does not help.

#### The Approach

*⚙ A group vote (majority after 30 seconds; the vote shows who is best at each option): HOW DO YOU GO DOWN?*

1. **THE ROPE**: one at a time. Slow, and quiet.
2. **THE SCREE**: all together, down the slope of loose black glass beside it. Fast, and it sounds like a cart of broken crockery.

> Either way somebody has to make it safe. The glassed rock offers nothing to tie to, and the scree is only waiting for a reason to move.

**THE APPROACH: THE WAY DOWN**

*⚙ One friend steps up (whoever has rolled fewest Approaches) and rolls for the party, in front of everyone: By the rope → Craft (rig an anchor that will hold on glass); By the scree → Might (be the anchor). DC 9 (+2 if the party solved the week's map). Pass: +500g · **and the party strikes first**. Fail: it gives halfway, the last of the drop is a slide in a rattle of black glass, and **they strike first**. The result sets who strikes first in the Battle.*

*⚙ If the Approach passed:*

> It holds. Hand under hand, one after another, you go down without a sound.

*⚙ If the Approach failed:*

> Halfway, it gives. The last of the drop is a long slide in a rattle of black glass, loud enough to wake the hill.

> You drop through into the warm dark. Below you a lamp swings, and for one moment you see him: a robed figure hauling himself away up a vent in the rock, the hot air streaming past him, a small box clutched to his chest.
>
> Then the lamp swings back, and he is gone, up into the black.

#### The Battle

> They are waiting for you. Four of them, robed, a blade in one hand and a torch in the other, between you and a stone table in the middle of the floor. The heat down here lies on you like a hand. Nobody speaks. They do not break when the first of them falls.

**THE BATTLE: THE FAITHFUL**

> **Blade and torch:** when one of them lands a hit, the burn does a little more on the round after.

*⚙ Every friend fights their own The Faithful at once (DEF 25, ATK 18), in lockstep rounds every 10 seconds; the fight rules are the weekday ones. Condition: Blade and torch: when an enemy hits a friend, that friend loses 2 more HP at the start of the next round. A friend below 50 HP sends a help-call to the chat; a friend who has won joins another fight. The party wins when the last enemy falls: +740g each. A fallen friend is "DEFEATED." and stays for the night.*

*⚙ If the party strikes first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He strikes first.

> The last one standing does not run. He looks at the blood on the floor, then at you. He sets his blade down on the stone at his feet, very carefully, the way you would put down something borrowed.

> He lets himself be bound. He holds his wrists out for the rope, side by side, and does not look at them while it is tied.

#### The United Effort

> The girl is not by the table.
>
> She ran when the fight began, for the only way out a child could see: the slope of loose black glass, and the crack of sky at the top of it. She is two-thirds of the way up, on her knees, with one fist shut tight on something and the other hand flat on the glass. She has stopped, because the slope has started to move.
>
> It is a small sound, like a great many plates being stacked very gently.
>
> The glass will bear one of you at a time, and perhaps not that. So the rope goes up to her a stretch at a time. One of you climbs past where the last one stopped, drives a blade into the slope, makes the rope fast to it, and comes down. Then the next.

**THE UNITED EFFORT: THE SCREE**

*⚙ Two bars at 100 (60 for a friend alone): "The Climb" (the thing in the way) and "Wren's Hold" (the thing at risk). Friends take turns, nobody twice until all have gone. Each turn is a Agility Challenge against DC 7. Pass: 10 + d20 off the thing in the way. Fail: 10 + d20 off the thing at risk. It ends when either bar hits 0. Won: +300g to every friend present. One Relic per roll (+4). The story lines it can say, as the bars fall:*

- **On a pass:**
  - *(early)* You go up it the way you would cross a frozen pond: flat, and slowly, and thinking about something else. The blade goes in to the hilt. The rope is fast.
  - *(early)* Past the first blade, and on. The glass under you ticks, and holds.
  - *(middle)* You are close enough to see her shoulders shaking. You do not call to her. You make the rope fast and come down.
  - *(middle)* She has turned her head. She is watching the rope come.
  - *(late)* One more stretch. You could almost throw the end to her, if she had a hand free to catch it.
  - *(late)* She has seen what the rope is for. She has not moved, and that is the right thing.
  - *(the end)* You have her wrist. She weighs nothing at all. She comes down the rope inside your arm, and she does not open her fist.
- **On a fail:**
  - *(early)* A plate of glass the size of a door turns over without warning, and the slope takes you down it on your back. Above, the sheet she is on slides a yard, and stops.
  - *(early)* The slope shrugs. It does it by itself, the way a sleeper turns over. She is nearer the edge of her sheet than she was.
  - *(middle)* Something lets go higher up. It comes down past her like a thrown handful of knives. She flinches. There is blood on her sleeve when it has gone by.
  - *(middle)* The glass under her tilts. She has to take her flat hand off it, and find a new place to put it.
  - *(late)* She is on her stomach now, with nothing under her feet.
  - *(late)* She has started, very quietly, to slide.
  - *(the end)* The whole slope lets go, with a sound like the sea. She comes down in it.
- **Between turns:** Below you, the bound man is watching the slope too. / The warm air coming up from below smells of struck flint. / She has not made a sound since she stopped.

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> The girl is alive. Wren. She is nine or ten, and too frightened yet to say much. Her palm is cut and bound in the same white linen.
>
> Somebody took the time, again.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> When the glass has stopped she is at the foot of it, sitting up, cut in a dozen places, with her fist still shut.
>
> The girl is alive. Wren. She is nine or ten, and too frightened yet to say much. Under the new cuts, her palm is cut and bound in the same white linen.

> Her other fist is shut so tight the knuckles are white. For a long while she will not open it, for anyone.
>
> When she does, at last, there is a chip of black glass inside, polished smooth by her thumb.
>
> You think of her mother in Ashcombe, almost smiling: *She never lost it.*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [GIVE HER BACK THE EIGHT]*

> Arjun puts the eight from the ridges into her other hand, one by one. She looks at them, and then at Arjun, and closes her hand over them.

> Garrick takes off his coat, puts it round her, and says nothing at all. It comes down past her knees.

> Behind the stone table: the same hollow, cut to the same shape. Empty. Its rim has been swept clean. The rubbing, laid against it, fits.

#### Notice

*⚙ Notice: each friend gets one private line for their highest Attribute (a fallen friend gets the "from the floor" line):*

- **Agility:** That vent is too narrow for a man carrying anything large. Whatever is in the box is small.
- **Intellect:** The rim of the hollow is not dusty and not disturbed. It has been swept. Somebody tidied after.
- **Charm:** Wren has not looked at the captive once. She has looked at the vent four times.
- **Craft:** The stone case on his belt has no hinge, no catch and no seam. It was never made to be opened with a tool.
- **Aim:** There are four torches on the floor, and one bracket on the wall with nothing in it.
- **From the floor:** Down here the warm air is moving, along the stone, toward the vent. Something up there is drawing it.

> Inside his coat, wrapped in oilcloth and kept closer than his knife: bound pages of the Order's scripture. Unburned, this time.

#### The Verse of the Fall

> *From the First Book of the Order of the Dark Moon — The Verse of the Fall.*
>
> *Hear what became of the king who would not kneel.*
>
> *After the war, he kept his people as a shepherd keeps a flock through a hard winter. The power that had answered him on the night of the dark moon did not leave him, and he shared it. Those who stood nearest him were given the first of his gifts. They no longer felt the cold, nor the blade, nor the long fear of dying. They did not tire. They did not hunger. They stood their watches without end, and never once asked to be relieved. Where his armies camped, nothing grew — and nothing needed to. In time the gift would have reached every one of his people. In time, the whole world he had saved.*

#### Ask

> He has listened to all of it on his knees, with the calm of a man at a service.

*⚙ ASK: a board of questions; each friend taps one. Sealed questions the night needs are answered after two quiet minutes if nobody asks them.*

- **IS YOUR KING DEAD?** *(sealed)* *(Tonight turns on these)*: *"He sleeps."*
- **THE CHILDREN. WHY?** *(sealed)* *(Tonight turns on these)*: *"To wake him."*
- **THE FOURTH CHILD. WHERE IS HE?** *(sealed)* *(Tonight turns on these)*: *"Already with us."*
- **WHERE DOES HE SLEEP?** *(The king)*: *"Where he fell."*
- **WILL IT WORK?** *(The king)*: *"It is written that it will."*
- **GENERATIONS OF CHILDREN. WHY DOES HE STILL SLEEP?** *(The king)*: Silence.
- **WHO ARE YOU?** *(The faithful)*: *"The faithful."* And to whom: *"To the king who did not kneel."*
- **WHO LEADS YOU?** *(The faithful)*: *"One whose face I've never seen."*
- **DO YOU KNOW THIS MAN?** *(The faithful)*: *"No."* *(of Garrick)*
- **WERE YOU LYING JUST NOW?** *(The faithful)*: A look that is almost pity. *"The faithful do not lie."*
- **WILL WREN GO HOME?** *(The children)*: *"She has given. She may go."*
- **THE OTHERS YOU TOOK, BEFORE** *(The children)*: *"Most went home."* Pressed on the charcoal woman's nephew: *"Him we lost."* *"Not by our choosing."*
- **WHO SCRATCHED THE NAMES?** *(The children)*: *"The faithful write the names of those who will give, so that he may know them."*
- **WHY CARRY THAT CASE?** *(This room)*: *"So that we would know you."*
- **WHAT SAT IN THAT HOLLOW? / WHAT WAS IN THE BOX?** *(This room)*: Silence. Not refusal: the kind that costs him. *"Until the throne rise whole,"* he says instead, softly, the way you would end a prayer.
- **HOW DID YOU GET HERE FIRST?** *(This room)*: Silence.

> Nobody asks it. The answer comes anyway, as if the room had asked.

> The words cut into the beam over her bed, in the shepherd's hut: *Until the throne rise whole.*

> *Rise.*

# Week 4: The Water Stone

*⚙ This week: weekday DC 8; weekday enemy DEF 24, ATK 17; the Gathering calls on Craft. The week's question at the fire: "What do they need Joss for?"*

## Week 4, Day 1

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You went down through the crack in the Kiln's crown into the warm dark, where a girl was crying, and glimpsed a robed figure climbing away up a vent with a small box held to his chest. You brought Wren down alive from a slope of loose black glass, her palm already cut and bound in white linen. Behind the stone table was the same swept, empty hollow, and the rubbing fitted it. The captive's sealed stone case sprang open at your touch. “Seeker blood,” he whispered. His scripture said the king never lied to his people, only kept silent about the price of his gift, and that the Seekers who wrote your fragments, his own soldiers, struck him down. He did not die. He sleeps where he fell, and the Seekers locked their ways with their own blood. “Your blood. Their blood. The same.” Their book says the faithful shall not lie, so his answers stood: the children are taken to wake the king, and the fourth child is already with them. The Order is not mourning a dead king. It is waking a sleeping one. “We're not finished with you,” the captive said, and every lamp went out. When one was lit again, his ropes lay cut clean.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Not finished"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: how did he vanish from a room you were all standing in, and what did he mean?*

> *We're not finished with you.*
>
> Then the dark. Now one lamp is lit again, in a shaking hand, and the place where he knelt is empty. The wick smokes. The air still smells of hot stone, and of every lamp in the chamber dying at once.
>
> His ropes lie on the stone. One set of footprints leads away through the ash. Nobody saw a thing. Nobody heard a blade.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The ropes were cut with one stroke each. *(Adept and up:)* A sharp blade and a steady hand, in the pitch dark.

- **Agility:** Nobody brushed against you. *(Adept and up:)* In a room this size, with all of you in it.

- **Intellect:** Every lamp went at once, yours among them. *(Adept and up:)* That takes more than one pair of hands.

- **Charm:** Wren has not let go of the sleeve she found in the dark. *(Adept and up:)* She did not cry out. She has learned not to.

- **Craft:** The ropes lie where he knelt, still in their knots. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody untied anything. Nobody wasted a moment.

- **Aim:** The ash is smooth right across the floor, but for your own prints and one line of his. *(Adept and up:)* It will not stay that way for long.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*
