### Your thread: the chapter, "The north road"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who rode north ahead of you, and what are they carrying?*

> The north road runs long and empty through cold, open country: heather, wind, and now and then a shepherd's hut with its smoke going sideways. By midday the warm ground of the burned country is a day behind you, and the wind has an edge to it.
>
> At a ford, three men step out of the alders. They have counted your packs, and they have counted you wrong.

**FIGHT: THE MEN AT THE FORD**

> Not grey coats. Not good boots. Only hungry.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 24 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 17 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. You strike first. A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: no gold (today's Challenge is the paid beat). At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

> Between you, it is over quickly. These men do not vanish. They sit down in the shallows with their heads in their hands, and, being asked nothing, tell you everything.
>
> They let yesterday's lot go by. Four riders, armed, with a little closed cart between them. Nobody's easy purse.

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> The one you put down first looks up at you out of the water and says he is sorry. He seems to mean it.

> Low in the stone of the old ford, where the water runs over it, sits a carved housing.

*⚙ A button: [CLOSE YOUR HAND ON IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> It opens as your fingers shut. What is inside is colder than the river.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> Beyond the ford the road forks. Mei and Arjun take the lake road, the one to Reedham. You take the other.

*⚙ A button: [READ THE GROUND]*

> Mei and Arjun come back to the fork with nothing to tell but sheep.
>
> Your road has four horses and two thin wheels, a day old, going fast.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The ruts are shallow, even in the soft ground by the water. *(Adept and up:)* Whatever is in that cart weighs almost nothing.

- **Agility:** The horses were watered standing, and ridden on. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody got down.

- **Intellect:** Joss is on the lake road. These are not. *(Adept and up:)* They are not going where the boy is.

- **Charm:** Three hungry men let four riders pass, and tried you. *(Adept and up:)* You look like less trouble than you are.

- **Craft:** One of the cart's wheels is new, and the other is not. *(Adept and up:)* They have come a long way fast, and mended as they went.

- **Aim:** Two rode before the cart and two behind, all the way. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody rides like that around nothing.

> At the fork a carter is watering his horses, and he is glad of anyone to talk to.

**CHALLENGE: THE CARTER**

> He saw them. He will get round to it.

*⚙ Pick one: Charm (let him run, and steer him), or Intellect (ask the one question that matters). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 8: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 9+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: a long hour, and the best of the light, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> It takes less time than he would like. Somewhere between the weather and the price of hay, he gets to the point.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> It takes a long hour of his opinions on the weather, the road and his brother-in-law. But he gets there.

> *"Nothing heavy in that cart,"* he says. *"One box, no bigger than a bread crock. And they guarded it like it was the last gold in the world. Four armed men for one little box."*
>
> He shakes his head. *"No child, if that's what you're asking. I'd have heard a child."*

> He digs in his coat, and holds something out on his palm. *"Paid for the water with this. Nobody'll take it off me."*
>
> A coin, old and heavy. On one side the king's face has been scraped away to bare metal. The other side, somebody has kept polished.

*⚙ A button: [TAKE THE COIN]*

> It sits cold and heavy in your hand, heavier than a coin that size should be.
>
> A small box.
>
> You have seen one. Two nights ago, under the Kiln, held against a man's chest as he went up the vent.

> That night you camp in the lee of a dry-stone wall, with the wind going over the top of it all night like a river. Garrick passes his flask round. *"It's terrible. Drink it anyway."*
>
> When it comes back to him he takes the last swallow himself, and makes a face at it.
>
> Then he pours one more cup than there are people, and sets it down at the empty place by the fire.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Four armed men for one small box, riding away from the boy. What is in it? Reedham, by noon tomorrow.**

*⚙ The day summary card:*

- **THE NORTH ROAD** · Day 2
- You earned: [gold] · Charm or Intellect [growth]
- You learned: the riders ahead of you carry **no child. One small box**, under four swords, and at the fork they turned away from the lake.
- You hold: a coin with the king's face scraped off one side, and polished on the other
- Fragments: 2 found
- Tomorrow: **Reedham, on the Stillwater**

### The Seeker Fragment (in your Seeker Chest)

*⚙ Waiting in every friend's Seeker Chest from the morning; solved whenever you like, usually after the chapter or at the fire.*

> Earth, wind, water, fire — four we could name. The _____ had no _____ at all, and we feared it more than the other four together.

*⚙ Solved:*

> Earth, wind, water, fire — four we could name. The fifth had no element at all, and we feared it more than the other four together.

## Week 4, Day 3

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Show me any cuts from the ford before we set off, however small. I've a needle, clean water and a steady hand. A clean cut is a quick one to mend, and a dirty one is a week of grumbling.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> On the north road three hungry men tried you at a ford and lost. They told you what they had let go by the day before: four armed riders with a little closed cart, whose tracks turned away from the lake road and the boy. A carter at the fork paid you in an old coin with the king's face scraped off, and said he had watched them pass. “One box, no bigger than a bread crock. Four armed men for one little box.” You had seen a small box before, held to a robed man's chest as he climbed out of the Kiln, so what is in it? Reedham, by noon tomorrow.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Reedham"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: what happened to Joss?*

> Reedham sits on the shore of the Stillwater, a long grey lake so calm that it holds the sky like a mirror. Boats are drawn up on the shingle. Nets hang drying, and the whole shore smells of tar and cold water and yesterday's catch.
>
> And out on the water, a stone's throw from the shallows, a bell tower rises straight up out of the lake: roof gone, bell gone, its windows full of sky.
>
> *"That's the old village,"* a fisherman says, when he sees you staring. *"Old Reedham. The valley flooded in my great-grandfather's time and we moved uphill. Dry year like this, you can walk some of its streets at the shallow end. Nobody does."*

> Joss's grandmother keeps the house at the top of the lane. She lets you in, and says one thing.
>
> *"They asked for him by name,"* she says, *"and they knew the night he was born."*
>
> You have heard those words before: at the mill, and in Ashcombe.
>
> After that she only stands, with her hand on the doorpost. Under her fingers are notches, cut with a kitchen knife, fresh and pale in the old wood. One for every night.
>
> Eight.

> Eight nights. He was taken before you had even found Wren.
>
> They needed Wren *only the once*, and kept her two weeks. They have kept Joss eight nights already.
>
> They are waiting for something.

*⚙ A choice: [THE SHELF] or [THE WINDOW]. Pick in any order until every branch has been seen.*

*⚙ If you pick [THE SHELF]:*

> A row of little boats, each no longer than a finger, each better than the one before. The newest has its hull and no mast. The knife is lying beside it, where he put it down.

*⚙ If you pick [THE WINDOW]:*

> A lamp is burning in it, at noon.
>
> On the shingle, later, the fisherman tells you why. In Reedham a lamp is lit in every window on a dark-moon night; it always has been. Joss came into the world with the whole village lit up round him. She has not let that one go out since he went.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** Her skiff has been hauled up above the storm line. *(Adept and up:)* She did that alone.

- **Agility:** She crosses the room without once passing in front of the window. *(Adept and up:)* She will not put herself between that lamp and the lake.

- **Intellect:** A lamp at noon is oil wasted, in a house with none to waste. *(Adept and up:)* It is not for her to see by.

- **Charm:** There are two stools at the table, and she has sat on neither. *(Adept and up:)* She has not sat down since you came in.

- **Craft:** The skiff has a sprung plank, stuffed with rag. *(Adept and up:)* It will float. It will not stay dry.

- **Aim:** From her door you can see down the lane, over the shingle, to the tower in the water. *(Adept and up:)* And from the tower, this window.

> She takes you down to the shingle herself, and puts the skiff's rope into your hand. The tower Meira marked stands out in the water, waiting.

**CHALLENGE: HER SKIFF**

> It leaks, and the wind is getting up off the lake.

*⚙ Pick one: Craft (patch her first, and row her dry), or Might (row her hard, and beat the leak there). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 8: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 9+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you get there soaked, and bailing, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> The patch holds, near enough, and the strokes come long and even. You bring her in under the tower with the bilge still shallow and the wind rising at your back.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> The rag works loose a quarter of the way out, and the lake comes in round your boots faster than it can be thrown back. You arrive soaked and bailing. But you arrive.

> All the way out, Garrick has been bailing with a tin cup. He does not look up. *"I didn't leave a warm bar to drown in a lake. I'd like that noted."*

> The tower stands on stones far older than the rest of it. Worn into that rock, just above the waterline, is a shape you know: the mark from the Widow's Tooth, the mill, the Blackglass.
>
> Beside it is a housing, slick with lake-weed.

*⚙ A button: [LAY YOUR PALM ON IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> The lid lifts as if it had been waiting. What is inside is as cold as the water it has sat above for centuries.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> Back on the shingle, beside the place her skiff was lying, there are keel-marks that are not hers. Fresh ones, pressed deep into the wet stones, and the lapping of the lake has not yet smoothed them.
>
> Up the lane, the lamp burns on in her window, small and steady in the afternoon.
>
> **Someone has been rowing out to the drowned village by night. Old Reedham, at the shallow end, tomorrow.**

- You hold: her skiff

- Tomorrow: **Old Reedham, the streets in the shallows**

## Week 4, Day 4

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Mist on the Stillwater this morning, lying flat across the water like a sheet on a bed nobody's slept in. The reeds are grey with it. Keep your cloak close. Lake air gets into the bones, and mine have complaints enough already.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Reedham sits on the Stillwater, and a stone's throw out a bell tower stands up out of the lake, the last of the drowned old village. Joss's grandmother said they asked for him by name and knew the night he was born, and her doorpost held eight fresh notches, one for every night he has been gone. They needed Wren only the once and still kept her two weeks, so they are waiting for something. You rowed her leaking skiff out to the tower Meira marked, and found the old mark there with a cold fragment beside it. Back on the shore were fresh keel-marks that were not hers: someone has been rowing out to the drowned village by night. Old Reedham, at the shallow end, tomorrow.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Old Reedham"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who has been out in the drowned streets?*

> You walk the shore to the shallow end in the morning, and the old village comes up to meet you.
>
> The Stillwater has drawn back far enough to leave Old Reedham's upper streets standing in the open: cobbles slick with weed, doorways full of silt, walls water-marked green to the height of your chest. Everything smells of mud and old rope. A gull stands on a chimney that has no house under it.
>
> Further out, the same streets slope down, and simply go on under the water.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The water-mark on every wall is level with your chest. *(Adept and up:)* All of this was under the lake, and will be again.

- **Agility:** The weed is green on every cobble but those down the middle of one lane. *(Adept and up:)* It has been walked on.

- **Intellect:** Nobody in Reedham walks these streets. *(Adept and up:)* Then nobody in Reedham would see who does.

- **Charm:** One doorpost has notches cut up it, generations old: a child's height, year by year. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody counted on a doorpost here, too.

- **Craft:** The market cross still stands square in its silt. *(Adept and up:)* Its lowest course is older stone than the rest, and better cut.

- **Aim:** Out past the last roofs the reed island lies low on the water. *(Adept and up:)* It is the only thing on this lake a boat could hide behind.

> It takes the whole day to work down through the streets, house by silted house. By the time you reach the lowest lane the light is going.
>
> In the mud of the lowest lane there are footprints, a few days old. Grown men's boots.
>
> And between them, smaller ones.

**CHALLENGE: THE LOWEST LANE**

> Weed on wet stone, and the light going.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (follow them across the cobbles without going in), or Aim (pick them out by lamplight, stone by stone, and call the line). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 8: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 9+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you go into the water, and the lamp goes with you, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

> Stone by stone, with the lamp held low and the weed sliding under your boots, you follow them down the lane to the edge of the deep water, where they stop.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> You lose them twice. Then a drowned cobble turns under your foot, and you are in to the waist, with the lamp gone out in your fist. Dev finds the last of them by feel: at the edge of the deep water, where they stop.

> He was walked through here alive. And then they got into a boat.

> In the old market cross, at the centre of the drowned square, is a carved housing, half buried.

*⚙ A button: [SCRAPE THE SILT AWAY]*

>
> She had this line already. Every word of it, copied years ago from some other record, in her plain hand. And beside it, a note:
>
> *"'Moon-blood' — the old songs' word for the marked."*

> The marked. Tamsin. Corin. Wren. Joss.
>
> You.
>

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Fog so thick this morning I can barely see my own hand, and the lake has gone so still you can hear a drip land. On the water, keep within arm's reach of each other, and speak up before you sneeze. Sound travels strangely in fog.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> In the lowest lane of Old Reedham, the drowned streets a dry year had left above the lake, you found men's boot-prints a few days old with smaller ones between them. They ended at the deep water. Joss was walked through there alive, and taken on by boat. That evening you found the day's fragment already copied into Meira's notes, beside her line: “‘Moon-blood’ — the old songs' word for the marked.” The word takes in Tamsin, Corin, Wren, Joss and you, and the Seekers left a warning with it. A warning against what? The reed island, a mile out, is the only place on this lake a boat can put in unseen.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The reed island"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where did the boat take him, and what has it been doing since?*

> Fog comes down on the Stillwater in the morning, thick and white. You row into it in her skiff, and the shore is gone behind you in a dozen strokes. After that there is only the creak of the oars, the drip off the blades, and the grey shadow of the tower somewhere off to one side.

**CHALLENGE: THE FOG**

> A mile of white water, and one grey shadow in it.

*⚙ Pick one: Aim (hold your line on the tower, for as long as you can see it), or Craft (rig a trailing line off the stern, and keep her true by it). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 8: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 9+. Pass: +500g · **and you strike first**, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you row in a circle, and **he strikes first**, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> The reeds close round the bow with a dry hiss. You come in behind the island, where they are thickest, and nothing on it has heard you.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> You row in a slow circle for an hour. When the reeds loom up out of the white, you are on the open side, and somebody on the island has been listening to your oars for some time.

> The island is a low hummock of willow and rushes. On its far side, drawn up where nobody on shore could see it, is a boat.
>
> Painted on its prow, small and neat, is a black circle.
>
> And there is a man sitting on its gunwale.

**FIGHT: THE MAN WITH THE BOAT**

> He was left to mind it.

*⚙ 100 HP each. You hit when d20 + Weapon − enemy DEF 24 is 1 or more; the enemy when d20 + ATK 17 − your Armor is 1 or more. Damage = the margin × 5. One tap a round. Who strikes first is set by today's Challenge: you on a pass, the enemy on a failure. A Combat Relic gives +1 Weapon and +1 Armor for the rest of the fight. Win: no gold (today's Challenge is the paid beat). At 0 HP: "DEFEATED.", and the story goes on.*

*⚙ If you strike first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He strikes first.

> He does not go for the boat. He goes into the reeds, and then into the water, and the fog closes over the place.
>
> For a while you can hear him swimming, slow and steady, somewhere out in the white. Then you cannot.
>
> The boat is yours. Her skiff goes on a line behind it.

*⚙ If you won the fight:*

> One of his oars is still in your hands. He let go of it sooner than be taken.

*⚙ A button: [SEARCH IT]*

> Two pairs of oar-places, worn bright. A lamp bracket. Stern boards still wet.
>
> In the bottom, among the rope, lie pale curls of whittled wood, still fresh. And a tiny hull, half shaped, split down the middle, and thrown away.
>
> Somebody small has been sitting here, carving.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** Two pairs of oars, for a boat this size. *(Adept and up:)* It is rowed hard, by four, and often.

- **Agility:** He went into the reeds without a splash. *(Adept and up:)* He knows this island with his eyes shut.

- **Intellect:** The stern boards are wet through, not just beaded with fog. *(Adept and up:)* This boat was out on the lake last night.

- **Charm:** The little hull was not dropped. It was thrown. *(Adept and up:)* He was angry with it. He is still himself.

- **Craft:** The lamp bracket is set low, under the gunwale. *(Adept and up:)* A light carried there cannot be seen from the shore.

- **Aim:** The black circle is no bigger than a coin. *(Adept and up:)* You would have to be this close to know whose boat it was.

> It is a ferry. Somebody has been rowing between this island and the drowned village, night after night.

> Among the willow roots is a stone older than the island's soil, and in it a housing the roots have half swallowed.

*⚙ A button: [WORK YOUR FINGERS IN]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> The moment they touch the lid, it opens. The cold that comes out of it is the cold of the lake bed, under all that mud and dark.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> The fog lifts toward evening, and the dark comes down behind it. The island has one rise, no higher than a man, and from the top of it there is one clear line across the water.
>
> The drowned tower.
>
> And on the black lake between, moving away from you, low on the water: a lamp.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Why would anyone keep watch on an empty tower? After that lamp, tonight, in their own boat.**

- You earned: [gold] · Aim or Craft [growth]
- You learned: the Order runs **a ferry** between the island and the drowned village, by night. **Joss is alive, and still carving.** And something on this lake is watching the tower.

## Week 4, Day 6

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Sleep while the sun's up, if you can. Tonight is for rowing, and rowing goes better rested. I've made up a bed of cloaks in the dry corner, and the kettle's on for the first one awake.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You rowed through thick white fog to the reed island. On its far side a man minding a boat with a small black circle on its prow fought you, then slipped away into the reeds and left the boat to you. In its bottom lay fresh curls of whittled wood and a tiny split hull, thrown away, so Joss is alive and still carving. When the fog lifted you saw a lamp low on the black water, moving away toward the drowned tower. Why would anyone keep watch on an empty tower? After that lamp, tonight, in their own boat.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The tower at night"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where is he held?*

> You row out after the lamp in their own boat, with her skiff on its line behind, keeping the oars as quiet as you can make them. The lake is black, and so is the sky. The water talks softly against the hull.
>
> The lamp is out long before you reach the tower. When you climb in onto its stair, there is nobody in it.
>
> The last fragment is on the stair, not hidden at all: wedged in the angle of a step where the water once reached, as if it had waited out the flood.

*⚙ A button: [TAKE IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**
>
> That is six.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The boat you took sits lower than her skiff, and steadier. *(Adept and up:)* It was built to carry more than its rowers.

- **Agility:** The stair is wet to the fourth step, and dry above. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody stood here tonight, and went no higher.

- **Intellect:** A lamp came to this tower and went out, and no boat passed you going back. *(Adept and up:)* Then it has not gone back.

- **Charm:** Nobody has said the boy's name since the island. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody has his little split hull in a pocket.

- **Craft:** The bell-chamber's windows have no shutters left. *(Adept and up:)* A flame up there will not live long.

- **Aim:** From up here you can see every light in Reedham. *(Adept and up:)* One window is brighter than all the rest.

> Nobody suggests waiting for morning.
>
> You climb to the bell chamber and kneel on its floor with the six, by one lamp. The wind comes through the empty windows in gusts. Below, the lake slaps at the stones.

*⚙ A button: [LAY THEM OUT]*

> They settle, as they have three times before. A map, old and exact. But there is light enough for one pair of eyes, and only while the flame lives.
>
> Mei bends to read. The flame is yours to keep.

**CHALLENGE: THE FLAME**

> One lamp, four empty windows, and the wind.

*⚙ Pick one: Craft (build it a shelter from what you carry), or Aim (one spark, struck true and set where the gusts cannot reach it). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 8: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 9+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: it goes out halfway, and the way has to be called again, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> It goes out halfway through. It is lit again, and the way is called again from the start, slower, and louder, over the wind.

> Under Old Reedham. Down the stair of the drowned church, to a room below the streets where the lake stands knee-deep.

> Then a gust takes the lamp.
>
> And in the dark, close, so close that it must have been lying in the black water right under the tower: wood knocks on wood.
>
> Then oars. Not careful now. Pulling hard, away from you, toward the drowned village.

> Somebody was beneath you, and heard every word.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Down the stair, into the boat you took from them, and after that sound. Now.**

## Week 4, Day 7: the Gathering

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You rowed after the lamp in the Order's own boat, but it was out long before you reached the tower, and nobody was there. The sixth fragment lay on the stair, and in the bell chamber the six became a map once more, read aloud by the light of one struggling flame. It led under Old Reedham, down the stair of the drowned church, to a room below the streets where the lake stands knee-deep. When the flame blew out, wood knocked on wood right beneath the tower, and oars pulled hard away toward the drowned village: somebody had heard every word. Down the stair, into their boat, and after that sound, now.

### The Gathering (live, the whole party together in the party chat)

*⚙ That morning, in each friend's thread:*

> Tonight: the Gathering. After the oars, in the boat you took from them. Tonight calls on Craft.

*⚙ Lobby at the time the party picked (poll on Day 4); starts once everyone is in, or any friend may start after 10 minutes, or by itself after 30. MYTHIOS posts the story a passage at a time; [GO ON] moves it on. About 20 to 30 minutes with the table talking.*

#### Arrival and the Approach

> Oars, in the dark, pulling away toward the drowned village. You are already in the boat you took from them, and already rowing. The water is so black that the blades vanish into it at every stroke.

*⚙ A group vote (majority after 30 seconds; the vote shows who is best at each option): HOW DO YOU FOLLOW?*

1. **OPEN WATER**: quick, and nothing in it to steer by but the sound.
2. **THE DROWNED ROOFS**: a road you can feel with an oar. Slower, and every roof looks like the last.

**THE APPROACH: THE BLACK LAKE**

*⚙ One friend steps up (whoever has rolled fewest Approaches) and rolls for the party, in front of everyone: Across open water → Aim (hold a line on a sound); Along the roofs → Might (row hard, and keep the oars quiet). DC 10 (+2 if the party solved the week's map). Pass: +500g · **and the party strikes first**. Fail: you lose the sound twice, and **they strike first**. The result sets who strikes first in the Battle.*

*⚙ If the Approach passed:*

> You never lose them. The knock of their oars stays just ahead of you the whole way.

*⚙ If the Approach failed:*

> Twice the knock of their oars is swallowed by the dark. Twice you sit on the black water with nothing but your own breath, until it comes again.

> Nobody speaks. The lake is black, the sky is black, and the only thing to steer by is that faint, steady knock of wood on water somewhere ahead. Then the drowned church comes up out of the dark beside you, and the oars stop.
>
> It stands at the edge of the deep water, its roof long gone. Its flooded stair leads down under the streets into an undercroft, where the lake has come in through the old stone and stands knee-deep, black and still, at every chamber door.
>
> You go in with the cold climbing your legs and your lamps held high, and for the first time since Meira's list found you, you are close enough to hear it happening: a child's cry cut short, and the slap of feet running away through water.

#### The Battle

> They are waiting in the water. Four of them, robed, about a stone table, with the lamplight shaking on the black water at their knees. The cold has your legs to the bone already. They come at you through it, slow and certain.

**THE BATTLE: THE FAITHFUL**

> **Knee-deep:** a bad swing in water this deep costs you your footing, and your next stroke. It costs him the same.

*⚙ Every friend fights their own The Faithful at once (DEF 25, ATK 18), in lockstep rounds every 10 seconds; the fight rules are the weekday ones. Condition: Knee-deep: roll a 1, 2 or 3 on a strike and you lose your next strike. So does he. A friend below 50 HP sends a help-call to the chat; a friend who has won joins another fight. The party wins when the last enemy falls: +740g each. A fallen friend is "DEFEATED." and stays for the night.*

*⚙ If the party strikes first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> He strikes first.

> Those still on their feet do not stand, as they stood under the Kiln. They break for the far door and the lake beyond it.

> The boy on the table is alive. Joss. The cut on his palm is still bleeding through its linen. In his good hand he holds a little wooden boat, no longer than a finger, and he does not let go of it even while you lift him down.
>
> A chain runs from his ankle to a ring in the drowned floor. The lock on it is older than he is, and mostly rust. It breaks under the flat of a blade.
>
> Behind him, the socket.
>
> Empty — and this time the dust at its rim hasn't settled. It's still falling, a faint grey haze drifting down through the lamplight.

#### The United Effort

> From the far door comes a sound like a bell dropped down a well.
>
> It is the old water-gate: an iron grille as wide as the doorway, hung up out of the way on a rope for longer than anyone has been alive. The last of them cut that rope as he went under it, so that nobody could follow.
>
> He was not quite fast enough.
>
> He is on his back in the doorway with the gate across his legs, in water that would cover him if he lay flat. He is holding himself up on his elbows. His chin is just clear.
>
> Beyond the bars, on the black water: oars, pulling hard, going away. A second boat, the twin of the one you came in, with the same black circle painted on its prow. Then only the sound of it.
>
> He does not call after them.

> You have known since the Kiln what his kind are. *The faithful shall not lie.* He is the only one left who can answer you.

> The gate cannot be lifted. It came down crooked, and one corner has jammed in the groove it runs in. Heave at it, and the other corner grinds down on him.
>
> What holds it is a single iron pin, low in the groove, under the water. Work the pin out, and the gate will come up like a lid.
>
> There is room at it for one pair of hands, and for as long as one breath lasts.

**THE UNITED EFFORT: THE WATER-GATE**

*⚙ Two bars at 100 (60 for a friend alone): "The Pin" (the thing in the way) and "His Breath" (the thing at risk). Friends take turns, nobody twice until all have gone. Each turn is a Craft Challenge against DC 8. Pass: 10 + d20 off the thing in the way. Fail: 10 + d20 off the thing at risk. It ends when either bar hits 0. Won: +300g to every friend present. One Relic per roll (+4). The story lines it can say, as the bars fall:*

- **On a pass:**
  - *(early)* Your fingers find it in the dark: a pin of iron as thick as a thumb, furred with rust. You get the knife's point under its head and lean. Something gives, a grain at a time. Then your chest is burning, and you are up.
  - *(early)* You have it at once this time. The point finds the lip of the pin's head, and you feel the rust break like a crust.
  - *(middle)* It is turning now. Each twist brings a thread of rust up past your face like smoke. You feel it shift in its hole before your breath gives out.
  - *(middle)* A quarter-turn. Another. The gate above you makes a small, different sound.
  - *(late)* The pin is half out and loose as a bad tooth. You work it until your ears are singing, and come up with the taste of iron in your mouth.
  - *(late)* It moves under your thumb. One more like that.
  - *(the end)* It comes out in your hand.
- **On a fail:**
  - *(early)* The point skates off the rust and rings on the bars. The gate shudders, and settles the width of a finger. It is enough. His elbows go from under him, and the water closes over his face. They haul him up. He comes up coughing.
  - *(early)* You come up under the wrong bar, and the whole gate rings. He goes under; comes up; spits; says nothing.
  - *(middle)* The gate grinds down. He is under for a slow count of five before they get his face clear.
  - *(middle)* The gate takes a weight it did not have before, and gives. He has stopped holding himself up. They are doing it for him now.
  - *(late)* The knife slips. The gate drops again, and this time he does not struggle when he goes under. When they lift him his lips are blue, and his eyes do not find anyone.
  - *(late)* The gate grinds. The two holding his shoulders have to go under with him to keep hold.
  - *(the end)* The gate settles one last time. He goes under, and he is very quiet under their hands.
- **Between turns:** Out on the lake the oars are fainter. Whatever they carried out of here, it is not coming back. / From the table, Joss is watching. He has the little boat held so tight that his knuckles are white. / The man under the gate has not once asked you to hurry.

> Two of you take his shoulders. Somebody finds a knife with a good point.

*⚙ If one friend plays alone:*

> You get his shoulders across your knees. One hand for him, one for the knife. One breath.

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> For a moment nothing moves.
>
> Then every one of you has a hand on the bars, and the gate comes up out of its groove with a groan, and he is dragged clear by the collar, coughing black water.
>
> He does not thank you.
>
> Tied at his belt, sealed in a waxed pouch: pages. More of the Order's scripture, from a book you have not seen before.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> You get the pin out in the end. It takes all of you, and it takes too long.
>
> When the gate comes up, he comes with it like a sack.
>
> He is breathing. That is all. Whatever he could have told you, he will not tell it tonight.
>
> Tied at his belt, sealed in a waxed pouch: pages. More of the Order's scripture, from a book you have not seen before.

#### Notice

*⚙ Notice: each friend gets one private line for their highest Attribute (a fallen friend gets the "from the floor" line):*

- **Might:** Under the Kiln they stood their ground to the last man. Tonight they ran the moment they could.
- **Agility:** The water in the far doorway is still rocking. Whoever went out, went out as you came in.
- **Intellect:** The linen on his hand is soaked through. This cut is not an hour old.
- **Charm:** Joss has not let go of the little boat. He is listening to the oars, and he knows the sound.
- **Craft:** The dust is falling from inside the socket. Whatever sat there was lifted out, with care, not knocked loose.
- **Aim:** In the other boat, the one going away, with a circle on its prow like the one on yours: four at the oars, and a fifth in the stern who is not rowing. He is holding something in both arms.
- **From the floor:** Lying in the water you can see along its surface to the door. Something small is floating there: a curl of whittled wood.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [LOOK CLOSER]*

*⚙ [LOOK CLOSER]: an optional roll on that Attribute (DC 10); a pass adds +75g and this line:*

- **Might:** And the one you fought kept his back to the wall the whole time. He was not guarding the boy.
- **Agility:** A wet handprint on the door jamb. One hand. The other was full.
- **Intellect:** They have never before left a child still bleeding. They did not have the time.
- **Charm:** He has heard it every night since they took him.
- **Craft:** The inside is worn as smooth as a bowl. It held the same thing for a very long time.
- **Aim:** Not a child. It is far too small.

#### Ask

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> He is on his knees in the water, still coughing.
>
> **You may ask him.** Each of you, one question, whenever you choose. The board stays up for the rest of the night, and new questions will come as things are found.

*⚙ ASK: a board of questions; each friend taps one. Sealed questions the night needs are answered after two quiet minutes if nobody asks them.*

- **WHAT GOES IN THERE?** *(sealed)* *(Tonight turns on this)*: He doesn't answer. He flinches — the whole of him, like a struck animal — and looks anywhere but the socket.
- **WHO LEADS YOU?** *(The faithful)*: *"One whose face I've never seen."*
- **IS HE HERE?** *(The faithful)*: *"I don't know. I've never seen his face."*
- **DO YOU KNOW THIS MAN?** *(The faithful)*: *"No."* *(of Garrick)*
- **THE OTHERS YOU TOOK, BEFORE** *(The faithful)*: *"Most went home."* Pressed on the charcoal woman's nephew: *"Him we lost."* *"Not by our choosing."*
- **WHOSE IS THIS?** *(Added when the pen is found)*: *"The scholar's."*
- **IS SHE DEAD?** *(Added when the pen is found)*: *"Yes."*
- **DID YOUR PEOPLE KILL HER?** *(Added when the pen is found)*: *"Yes."* Asked why: silence.
- **DID YOU KILL HER?** *(Added when the pen is found)*: *"No."*
- **WHERE DID YOU GET IT?** *(Added when the pen is found)*: *"With her pages."*
- **HOW DOES THE PAGE END?** *(Added when the torn page is read)*: *"I have never seen it whole."*
- **WILL THE FIFTH STOP HIM?** *(Added when the torn page is read)*: Silence.
- **WHO GAVE YOU THIS PAGE?** *(Added when the torn page is read)*: *"It was given me to carry."*

*⚙ If the party moves on with a sealed question unasked:*

> Nobody asks it. Somebody only looks from him to the socket, and that is enough. He flinches — the whole of him, like a struck animal — and looks anywhere else.

#### The Verse of the Four

> *From the Second Book of the Order of the Dark Moon — The Verse of the Four.*
>
> *Hear what the moon left behind.*
>
> *Before there were kings, the moon broke. No telling survives of why. What fell from it fell everywhere — into the hills, into the deep water, into the dark beneath the roots of the world — and where each piece came to rest, it kept a little of the moon inside it, the way a coal keeps a little of the fire.*
>
> *So too the children born in the hour when the moon goes dark. The same light marks them. Their blood remembers what the shards remember. Set that blood upon a shard, and the shard remembers too — and wakes.*

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"Their blood remembers what the shards remember."*
>
> From the housing at the drowned tower

*⚙ The right row: "What fell when the moon broke still remembers the moon. So does anyone born in its dark."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [READ ON]*

> *For longer than any of us can count, the faithful gave the marked blood in his name, and he slept on. Blood poured out upon the ground is only blood. It must be given to a shard.*
>
> *When the Seekers had struck down their king, they took the shards that had served them and carried them to the far places of the land, and hid them, and set their own blood as the lock upon every way that led to them. Nothing of theirs, they said, would ever be found by any hand but their children's.*
>
> *They were right.*
>
> *Therefore the faithful have not waited for the ways to open. We have waited for those who can open them. Where the Seekers' children walk, the faithful follow, closer to them than their own shadows. Where a shard is uncovered, a marked child is brought to it, and the shard drinks, and wakes, and is carried home to him.*
>
> *One shard that has drunk is a coal in the dark, and no more. But when four have drunk, the sleep that holds him wears thin as ice in spring — and as each is carried home and set about him, it thins the faster, until the sky itself remembers the night he answered.*
>
> *Until the throne rise whole.*

#### The turn

> Under the Kiln: a robed figure going up the vent, a small box clutched to his chest.

> On the north road, the carter: four armed riders, and one closed box between them, *"no bigger than a bread crock"*.

> Tonight, past that doorway: a second boat, like yours, with the same black circle on its prow, and oars going away.

> And from the page you have just read: *Where the Seekers' children walk, the faithful follow.*

*⚙ A question to the table (anyone answers in the chat; MYTHIOS never grades it):*

> *Every socket was empty. Where did they go?*

*⚙ After sixty quiet seconds:*

> *Count the boxes.*

*⚙ After ninety, the answer:*

> Carried home to him. Every one, through a door that only your blood could open.

> **This was never about you.**

#### Garrick

> Garrick has read the page over your shoulder. His voice is low and steady. *"It's the Stones that wake him. Malakor."*
>
> A name scraped off every stone in the world, and he says it like a man who has always known it.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [HOW DO YOU KNOW THAT NAME?]*

> He doesn't look up from the page. *"My grandmother taught me that name."*

> *"Four Stones. Four children,"* he says. *"That's the shape of what's written here."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [IS HE AWAKE?]*

> *"Everything on this page says the sleep wears thin. Thin."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [CAN IT STILL BE STOPPED?]*

> He looks at the page a long moment. *"I won't promise you that."*

#### The pen

> There is one more thing in the pouch, under the pages: a pen. Plain brass, the nib stained with the same brown ink as every page of Meira's notes. Scratched small into the barrel, in her neat hand, are her initials.
>
> Nobody says anything for a while.
>
> Her satchel is opened, the way you would open the door of an empty room. Her hand is on every page. Every trail you have followed since the forest road, she walked first. And somewhere on a road, someone took this pen out of her hand, and she did not come back for it.
>
> The pen goes into the satchel, with the rest of her.

#### The torn page

> Under where the pen lay, one last page, burned black along one edge, the rest of it torn clean through. The top lines are in the Order's hand. The rest is set apart, in the older hand you first saw in the Order's scripture under the mill hill: the hand the Order itself calls older than its name.

> *From the Second Book of the Order of the Dark Moon. Even in the old telling, before we had our name, it was known—*
>
> *Here the hand changes. The rest is set apart, in the older hand:*
>
> **"Four were given to the wind, the water, the fire, and the earth, and drink deep whichever marked blood finds them first. The fifth heeds no element at all, but blood alone, and remembers whose. What the four begin, the fifth shall end—"**
>
> The page is torn there, its edge burned black. Whatever came next is gone.

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"The fifth heeds no element at all…"*
>
> From the old ford, on the north road

*⚙ The right row: "Earth, wind, water, fire — four we could name. The fifth had no element at all, and we feared it more than the other four together."*

> Two hands, centuries apart.

> Garrick says it quietly, like a man setting down something heavy. *"There's one more. A fifth Stone — she wrote about it."*
>
> And she did. Her footnote from the Blackglass, the one she wrote off: *"Five, the fragment says... A poet's count, perhaps."*

> *What the four begin, the fifth shall end—*

> Garrick says nothing at all.

#### The Cliffhanger

> You come up out of the undercroft into the open boat, Joss wrapped in someone's coat in the bow, the little boat still in his fist. It has been a long night, and it is nearly over: in the east, over the far shore of the Stillwater, the sky has begun to pale.
>
> Then it stops.
>
> The light does not grow. It goes grey and flat and hangs there, thin, as though something vast has leaned across the sun before it could rise. Along the shore, the birds that should be starting up stay silent, row by row.
>
> *Until the sky itself remembers the night he answered.*
>
> And from somewhere far to the north, so deep you feel it in your teeth more than hear it, comes a single sound. Slow. Enormous. Like a heartbeat.
>
> Then another.

# Week 5: The King's Stone

*⚙ This week: weekday DC 9; weekday enemy DEF 25, ATK 18; the Gathering calls on Charm. The week's question at the fire: "What will it take for the fifth to end this?"*

## Week 5, Day 1

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You followed the oars across the black lake to the drowned church, and down its stair to an undercroft where the water stood knee-deep. Joss lay on a stone table, alive, his cut palm still bleeding through its linen and a little wooden boat in his good hand. Behind him the socket was empty, and the dust at its rim had not settled. They had reached it barely ahead of you, the same night your map gave it up, and beyond the water-gate a second boat with a black circle on its prow pulled away. Their Second Book said marked blood set on a shard of the broken moon wakes it, and that the faithful follow the Seekers' children to every way only their blood can open. Every empty socket had held a Stone, carried home to him through a door you opened. It was never the children's blood alone. The blood wakes the Stones, and the Stones wake him. Garrick spoke the king's name like a man who has always known it. The captive carried Meira's pen, and Meira is dead. A torn page said a fifth Stone remembers whose blood, and broke off at “the fifth shall end—”. Coming out, you saw the dawn stop halfway, and from far to the north came a sound like a heartbeat.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The grey afternoon"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: Meira never found a fifth. Where do you look for a Stone no record names?*

> A third beat. Then a fourth. You feel each one in your teeth and the soles of your feet more than you hear it: slow as a sleeper's heart, from somewhere far to the north.
>
> The dawn never finishes. The morning stays grey and thin, as if the sun had been pushed a long way off, and along the whole shore of the Stillwater the birds have stopped singing. Under that light the lake has gone the colour of old pewter.

> You row Joss home. He sits in the bow with his bandaged hand in his lap, and the little boat in the other.
>
> His grandmother is already down on the shingle, in the strange light, with her hands over her mouth. Garrick holds the bow steady against the stones. She gets him out of the boat by herself.
>
> She does not put him down. She carries him up the shingle the way she must have carried him when he was two, with his feet knocking against her knees, and he lets her.
>
> At the top of the lane he twists round in her arms and holds the little boat up over her shoulder, high, so that all of you can see he still has it.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** She lifts him clear of the gunwale as if he weighed nothing. *(Adept and up:)* She has not been strong enough for that in years.

- **Agility:** You feel each beat come up through the boat's planks before it reaches your ears. *(Adept and up:)* It travels in the ground, and in the water.

- **Intellect:** It is long past sunrise, and the light has not changed. *(Adept and up:)* This is not weather.

- **Charm:** Up the lane, the lamp is still burning in her window. *(Adept and up:)* Nobody has thought to put it out. Nobody can tell that it is day.

- **Craft:** Joss's little boat has lost its mast. *(Adept and up:)* He held on to it so hard that it broke.

- **Aim:** The beats come from one quarter and no other. *(Adept and up:)* North, and a little east of north.

> Four children home. Every one you were sent to find.
>
> And the thing they were used for is waking up.

> You have had the torn page out so often since the undercroft that its fold has gone soft. Its last line has not changed: *what the four begin, the fifth shall end—*
>
> Meira never found a fifth place. Every record she had said four.

> In the afternoon you walk up through the village to where the north road leaves it. At its head stands a waystone older than Reedham, grey with lichen, its carving worn to nothing.

**CHALLENGE: THE WAYSTONE**

> It said something once.

*⚙ Pick one: Craft (charcoal and a sheet from Meira's notebook: take a rubbing, like the one you found on the forest road), or Intellect (read the old hand by the shapes it left, with the light held low across the stone). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 9: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 10+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: the stone keeps its word, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> One word comes up, in the old hand, letter by letter.
>
> **HIGHWALL**
>
> Beside the word, in the same hand, is a number: the miles. Whoever took the knife to this stone left the distance alone.
>
> Under it there was a second line. It did not wear away. Somebody cut it out, stroke by stroke, with a patient knife.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> Nothing comes up that you can read. The stone keeps its word, and you will have to hear it from someone else. Only this much is plain: under the worn part there was a second line, and it did not wear away. Somebody cut it out, stroke by stroke, with a patient knife.

*⚙ A button: [ASK GARRICK WHERE A FIFTH WOULD BE]*

> Garrick is looking north, the way the heartbeat comes from.
>
> *"The fifth wasn't hidden in the hills like the others,"* he says. *"Every telling I know ends at Highwall."*

> Highwall. You all learned it as children: the old capital, where the Grey Host broke of a fever in its own camp, and the city was spared by luck.
>
> You know now what the luck was.
>
> The carter's coin is in your pocket: a king's face, scraped down to bare metal. The line cut out of this stone is the same work. The story you were taught is what was left when the scraping was done.

> In a crack at the waystone's foot, with a rime of frost on it though the day is mild:

*⚙ A button: [TAKE IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> You stay that night in Reedham. Up the lane, the lamp burns on in her window.
>
> At dusk the heartbeat comes again, up through the floorboards and into the legs of your chair. It is louder than it was at dawn.
>
> **What is left in a city where nothing grows and nobody goes? The old kingdom's road: two days north.**

## Week 5, Day 2

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Two days of road ahead, and the old paving is hard on the feet, every slab of it laid for marching boots. Bind your feet now, before the blisters start, not after. Feet hold grudges, and they win every argument.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> The dawn after the undercroft never finished, and the heartbeat from the north went on under a thin grey sky. You rowed Joss home, and his grandmother carried him up the shingle in her arms, so every child you were sent to find was home. Meira's records only ever counted four Stones, but the torn page spoke of a fifth. On the old waystone at the head of the north road, a line had been cut away with a patient knife, the same work as the king's face scraped off the carter's coin. “Every telling I know ends at Highwall,” Garrick said. What is left in that dead old capital, two days north up the old kingdom's road, where nothing grows and nobody goes?

### Your thread: the chapter, "The old kingdom's road"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: what is the heartbeat doing to the country?*

> You leave Reedham in the grey morning, and by noon the lake is gone behind you.
>
> The road was built to carry armies. It still runs straight as a spear across the empty country, but grass has grown up through its stones, and nobody has mended it in longer than anyone can say. Larks should be up over country like this. None are.
>
> Every mile has its stone, and every stone a number, one less than the last.

> Late in the afternoon the road passes between two long, low rows of grassy mounds, one on each side, running away across the fields in perfect lines.

> Drawn up at the head of the rows are the only people you have met all day. A family, come down from the north, with all they own on one cart, and a cow tied behind.

## Week 5, Day 3

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Last of the good bread this morning, and a little cheese, and three dried apples I'd been saving. I've cut it all into fair shares, and I measured twice. Eat slowly and make it last.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You walked north on the old kingdom's road, an army road gone to grass, with a milestone counting down every mile. A family fleeing south told you their beasts would not settle since the sky turned. Beside the road ran rows of soldiers' graves from the old war, and one new grave, dug badly this week. At dusk the heartbeat came. The grass on the graves shivered with it, and the farm dogs came out of the dark at you, then wandered off when the beat stopped. The heartbeat is nearer every dusk, and it is coming from inside Highwall, which you will reach by noon.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The herald's gate"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: how do you get in, and where in a whole dead city do you start?*

> Highwall comes up out of the grey noon all at once: a wall so long that it bends out of sight in both directions, and behind it, rising in terraces, a city of pale stone.
>
> It is not ruined. The walls are whole. The towers are whole. But nothing grows between the stones, not moss, not the smallest green thing, and there are no birds. The air has no smell at all: no smoke, no dung, no green.
>
> On its hill at the very top stands a great hall. Its doors are shut.

> The road runs straight to a gate. You know the words. You heard them read under the mill hill. *Their herald rode up to the gate and named the hour of the city's ending — dawn.*
>
> This is the wall the Seekers held that night.
>
> The gate is shut.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The gate's timbers have not sagged a finger's width. *(Adept and up:)* Something on the far side is holding them up.

- **Agility:** The stair to the wall has lost its middle. *(Adept and up:)* What is left of it would take one person at a time.

- **Intellect:** A dead city has no reason to shut its gate. *(Adept and up:)* And nobody left to do it.

- **Charm:** Nobody has spoken since the wall came into sight. *(Adept and up:)* It is not a place that wants talking in.

- **Craft:** The gate-tower door is iron, and rusted into its frame. *(Adept and up:)* The hinges are on your side.

- **Aim:** High up, above the terraces, one grey thread stands in the grey air. *(Adept and up:)* It is not cloud.

**CHALLENGE: THE WALL**

> The gate will not open. The wall can still be climbed.

*⚙ Pick one: Aim (read the broken stair from its foot, and set your feet only where it will hold), or Might (force the rusted door of the gate-tower). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 9: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 10+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: it takes what is left of the afternoon, and you see the city only by dusk, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You are up in minutes, with the afternoon still in hand.
>
> There is light enough to follow one street all the way up to the hall. Something is standing in it: a dozen dark shapes, spaced out down the middle of the road like posts.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> It takes what is left of the afternoon. You are up, but the light is going, and the city below you is only roofs and shadow.

> Garrick passes his flask along the walkway, and waits until the last of you has had some before he takes any himself.

*⚙ A button: [LOOK DOWN AT THE GATE]*

> From above, you can see its inner side.
>
> It is barred. The bar is new timber, still pale.
>
> The iron brackets it lies in are another matter. They are worn smooth, and bright, by more bars than anyone could count.

**[PRESS ON]: GO DOWN TO IT**

*⚙ Offered only after a pass: inside the wall, by the gatehouse stair. 4 harder (DC 13). [PRESS ON] or [LEAVE IT]. Pass: +200g, and one more detail; fail costs nothing.*

*⚙ If you pressed on and passed:*

> Stacked against the gatehouse wall, one upon another, lie the old bars. Dozens. The topmost are grey. The lowest have rotted to a brown line on the stone.
>
> Whoever changes the bar has kept every one.

> On the walkway above the gate, where they would have stood shoulder to shoulder, a carved housing is set into the parapet at chest height.

*⚙ A button: [PRESS YOUR PALM FLAT TO IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> Its stone face swings inward. The cold that comes out to meet your hand is the cold of high places, of wind on a wall in winter.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> You are standing where they stood. The trail for the fifth begins on their own stretch of wall.

> Far up the hill, above the terraces, a thin grey thread stands in the grey air. From here you can see what it is. Smoke. One fire, below the hall.

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> You spend the night up on the wall-walk, in the lee of the parapet, with the barred gate beneath you and that one thread of smoke going up into the dark.
>
> **Who bars a dead city from the inside, year after year? Down into the streets, following the wall.**

## Week 5, Day 4

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> This city is so quiet you can hear the dust settle on the windowsills. Soft boots and low voices today, and stay where I can see you. A place this empty makes a man want to whistle, so I'd thank you all to stop me if I start.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> Highwall rose out of the grey noon whole and pale, with nothing green between its stones, no birds, and a great hall shut on the hill at its top. Its gate was shut, so you climbed the very wall the Seekers held on the war's last night, and found a fragment set into their parapet. From above you saw that the gate was barred on the inside with new, pale timber. The iron brackets it lay in were worn bright by more bars than anyone could count, and far up the hill one thread of smoke rose from a fire below the hall. Who bars a dead city from the inside, year after year, and who waits down in its streets?

### Your thread: the chapter, "The dead streets"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who else is in this city?*

> In the morning you go down off the wall into the city.
>
> Your footsteps are the loudest thing in Highwall. Doors stand open on bare rooms. Wells are dry. The streets inside the wall are whole, and empty, and they climb.
>
> Then, at a crossroads, you come round a corner into a street full of soldiers.
>
> They stand where they are, mid-stride, in armour so old that it has gone green and black. A dozen of them, strung out up the street, as if a company had been marching to his hall on the night he fell, and simply stopped.
>
> Their faces are hidden behind their helms. None of them moves.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** That armour should have fallen in on itself long ago. *(Adept and up:)* Something inside it is still holding it up.

- **Agility:** There is room to pass between them. *(Adept and up:)* Just. With your arms in.

- **Intellect:** A dozen, strung out, with gaps. *(Adept and up:)* This is the tail of a column. The rest got further up the hill.

- **Charm:** Not one of them has a face you can see. *(Adept and up:)* It is easier that way. It should not be.

- **Craft:** Every plate still hangs true on its buckles. *(Adept and up:)* Whoever fastened them expected to undo them again by nightfall.

- **Aim:** Every one of them is looking the same way. *(Adept and up:)* Up the hill, at the hall.

**CHALLENGE: THE STREET OF SOLDIERS**

> It is the short way. It is between them.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (slip through without touching one), or Intellect (work out what you are looking at, and where it is safe to tread). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 9: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 10+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you lose your nerve, or your footing, and go the long way round, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You walk the whole street between them, close enough to smell the old iron. Nothing happens.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> A pack scrapes a breastplate. For one terrible moment every one of you waits.
>
> Nothing happens. You back out the way you came, and take three streets to go round.

**[PRESS ON]: STOP BESIDE ONE**

*⚙ Offered only after a pass: and look at how it stands. 4 harder (DC 13). [PRESS ON] or [LEAVE IT]. Pass: +200g, and one more detail; fail costs nothing.*

>
> So is the next one's. So is every one's. They all stopped on the same step.

> Garrick walks last, between you and them, and does not look back once.

> You do. From the end of the street, you look back.
>
> The nearest one's helm is turned a little further toward you than it was.

> Set into the wall of a house at the end of that street is a housing, its carving as crisp as the day it was cut.

*⚙ A button: [FIND THE PATTERN]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> The stone turns on a hidden pin. Inside, the cold has kept as well as the carving.
>
> **A locked Seeker Fragment has been added to your Chest.**

> That night you bar the door of an empty house two streets away, and light no fire.
>
> Garrick finds cups on a shelf, wipes the dust out of them with his sleeve, and pours: one more than there are of you, as on every night of the road. He sets the last one at the empty place by the shuttered window, the one that faces up the hill.

> You have first watch. He sits up with you.

*⚙ A choice: [WHAT ARE THEY?] or [WHO IS THE CUP FOR?] or [SAY NOTHING]. Pick one; only the picked branch plays.*

*⚙ If you pick [WHAT ARE THEY?]:*

*⚙ If you pick [SAY NOTHING]:*

> The cup stands full by the shutter. Through the gap in it, nothing moves up the hill. Then, looking at the cup:
>
> *"My family's waited a long time for something. I'd like to live to see it."*

*⚙ The Pull (why tomorrow cannot wait):*

> **Was that helm turned before? Nobody is sure. And the smoke is higher up the hill. The square below the hall.**

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> There's broth left from last night's fire, small as it was, and I've kept it warm in the embers. Drink it slowly. It's the best thing in this city, and I say that as the man who made it.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> In the streets of Highwall you came on a dozen of the king's soldiers in armour gone green and black, standing mid-stride where they stopped on the night he fell. You got past them, and when you looked back from the end of their street, the nearest helm was turned a little further toward you than before. That night, in an empty house with no fire, Garrick poured one more cup than there were people and set it at the window facing the hall. On your watch he said, “My family's waited a long time for something. I'd like to live to see it.” Who keeps the smoke rising from the square below the hall?

### Your thread: the chapter, "Below the hall"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: who keeps a fire below the hall?*

> By afternoon the streets have climbed as high as they go, and for the first time in Highwall you smell woodsmoke.
>
> The last terrace ends in a square, and the hall fills the sky above it: a great pale building on the city's highest ground, its doors taller than houses, and shut. The heartbeat comes from behind them.
>
> And in the shell of a house across the square, a fire is burning.

**CHALLENGE: THE FIRE IN THE SQUARE**

> Somebody is sitting round it.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (get close enough to hear them), or Aim (stay where you are, and read the whole camp from here). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 9: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 10+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: you see the fire and little else, and a lantern swings your way, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you passed the Challenge:*

> You see everything, and they see nothing.
>
> Near dusk the watch changes. In the dead silence of the city, every word carries. A watchman walks out to the one at the wall, and touches his shoulder.
>
> *"Keep the dark."*
>
> And the other answers, *"It keeps us,"* and goes in to the fire.
>
> On the board propped by the wall there are nine strokes not yet crossed.

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> A stone skitters under your boot. A lantern swings your way, and for one long moment a watchman stares straight at the rubble you are lying behind.
>
> Then he lowers the lantern, and goes back to the fire without a word.

> Hooded figures: four, five, more. The Order. They have made their camp facing the doors, as if they had been here all their lives.
>
> Some of them have.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** There is a woodpile against that house, stacked to the eaves. *(Adept and up:)* In a city where nothing grows. They carry it in.

- **Agility:** The watchman at the wall has not shifted his feet once. *(Adept and up:)* He has done this every night since he could stand.

- **Intellect:** They are camped at the doors, and have not touched them. *(Adept and up:)* They are not trying to get in.

- **Charm:** Nobody at that fire is talking. *(Adept and up:)* It is the quiet of people who have said everything, long ago.

- **Craft:** The hearth is built of dressed stone, blackened right through. *(Adept and up:)* That fire is older than anyone sitting at it.

- **Aim:** A board is propped beside the wall, covered in chalk. *(Adept and up:)* Rows of strokes, and most of them crossed.

> As the light goes, one of them rises, faces the hall, and draws the chalk through one more stroke.

> They are not laying siege to him. They are keeping count.

> In the broken fountain at the edge of the square, facing the hall, a housing sits low in the dry basin.

*⚙ A button: [REACH DOWN TO IT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> It opens like a hand. The basin has been dry for centuries, and still what lies inside is as cold as well water.

## Week 5, Day 6

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Back to the wall this morning, the long way round. Stay together, and count heads at every corner. This city has too many corners, and not one decent inn among them.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> At the top of the city you reached a square below the hall, and the heartbeat came from behind its shut doors. Across the square, hooded figures of the Order were camped facing those doors, and some of them had kept that camp all their lives. They were not trying to get in, and as the light went one of them crossed out one more stroke on a chalk board, leaving eight. A fragment in the dry fountain could be read in part, “…where we could watch it, and watch him.” They are counting down. To what? You hold five pieces of the map, and the trail began on the wall.

### Your thread: the chapter, "The wall again"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where is the fifth?*

> The long way back round the street of soldiers takes all morning. Nobody looks at them twice. Nobody wants to know how many helms have turned.

> The herald's gate. The broken stair. Above it, the wall-walk, bare to the sky, and in plain sight of the square for forty paces.
>
> You were heard once already, under the tower at Reedham. Not again.
>
> You wait out the afternoon in the gate-tower's shadow, and choose who will watch the square.

*⚙ A choice: [POST Mei] or [POST Dev] or [POST Arjun]. Pick one; the choice colours the moment, the story is the same.*

> Mei crouches at the corner of the gate-tower, with the whole square in view, and one hand raised where you can see it.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The parapet on the city side is waist-high and a yard thick. *(Adept and up:)* The wall under you is thicker than any wall needs to be.

- **Agility:** Forty paces of open stone. *(Adept and up:)* Fewer, if you go when their heads are turned.

- **Intellect:** They change the watch at dusk. For a moment both men are looking at each other. *(Adept and up:)* That is the moment.

- **Charm:** From the square, a figure on the wall at dusk is only a shape. *(Adept and up:)* Shapes that walk like watchmen are not looked at twice.

- **Craft:** One stone in the walkway has a crack along it that runs too straight. *(Adept and up:)* It was cut, not broken.

- **Aim:** Below the walk, on the city side, there is a slit in the wall's face no wider than a hand. *(Adept and up:)* It looks straight at the hall.

> Up the hill a lantern crosses the square, and two voices carry down through the still air.
>
> *"Keep the dark."*
>
> *"It keeps us."*
>
> The watch is changing. Now.

**CHALLENGE: THE OPEN WALK**

> Forty paces, while the watch changes above you in the square.

*⚙ Pick one: Agility (low and fast, when the hand drops), or Charm (upright, at a watchman's pace, like someone who belongs on a wall). Roll d20 + that Attribute's tier bonus against DC 9: a Novice (+0) succeeds on 10+. Pass: +500g, and the Attribute grows. Fail: a lantern lifts, and you finish the crossing on your belly, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> Halfway, a lantern lifts in the square. You lie flat on cold stone until it goes down again, and cross the rest on your belly, a hand's width at a time.

> Whoever else is with you comes over the same way, one at a time, on the dropped hand. Mei comes last of all, at a watchman's unhurried walk.

> In a crack across the walkway, just where somebody keeping watch would stand, colder than the stone it lies in:

*⚙ A button: [TAKE IT]*

>

>

> It does not point into the city at all.
>

>
> They kept the fifth inside their own wall, and watched him from it for as long as they lived.

> It has been under your feet since the day you climbed up here.
>
> And the Order, camped in this city for longer than anyone can remember, has never known it was there.

> As the light goes, the heartbeat comes, louder than it has ever been. Overhead, in the grey, the moon is already up: fatter than it was a week ago, nearly half.
>
> Up the hill the new watchman has taken his post. Nothing else moves. Nobody heard.
