## Week 5, Day 7: the Gathering

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> You went the long way round the street of soldiers, back to the herald's gate, and posted a lookout on the gate-tower. While the Order's watch changed in the square, “Keep the dark” answered by “It keeps us”, you crossed forty paces of open wall-walk unheard. The sixth fragment lay in the walkway, and the map went from hand to hand without a word. It pointed into the wall itself, to a hidden room a stone's throw away with one slit of a window on the hall, where the Seekers kept the fifth and watched him all their lives. The Order has never found it. Into the wall, tonight.

### The Gathering (live, the whole party together in the party chat)

*⚙ That morning, in each friend's thread:*

> Tonight: the Gathering. Into the wall, to the room they never found. Tonight calls on Charm.

*⚙ Lobby at the time the party picked (poll on Day 4); starts once everyone is in, or any friend may start after 10 minutes, or by itself after 30. MYTHIOS posts the story a passage at a time; [GO ON] moves it on. About 20 to 30 minutes with the table talking.*

#### Arrival

> Night in Highwall. No lamp burns anywhere in the dead city but one: the Order's fire, high on the hill below his hall. There is no sound at all but your own breath.
>
> Across the city, on its hill, stands his hall. Doors shut. Sealed with something older than locks.
>
> The map ends a stone's throw from the herald's gate: a room hidden in the thickness of the old wall, one slit of a window facing the hall. The Order has kept its own watch on those same doors for centuries, from just up the hill, and never known the room was there.
>
> At the foot of the wall you light your lamps to find the Seekers' door. Up on the hill, the Order's watchmen see the light moving. They do not see the room. They never have.

#### The Approach

*⚙ A group vote (majority after 30 seconds; the vote shows who is best at each option): WHERE DO YOU MEET THEM?*

1. **THE GATE-TOWER DOOR**: they can come at you only one at a time, and you have nowhere to go.
2. **THE STREET**: under the herald's gate. Room for you, and room for them.

> You have as long to decide as it takes them to come down a hill. Wherever you stand, you meet them well short of the hidden room. Nobody leads them to that.

**THE APPROACH: MAKE YOUR GROUND**

*⚙ One friend steps up (whoever has rolled fewest Approaches) and rolls for the party, in front of everyone: At the door → Might (drag a fallen lintel across the way); In the street → Intellect (read the street, and choose where to stand). DC 11 (+2 if the party solved the week's map). Pass: +500g · **and the party strikes first**. Fail: they are on you before you are set, and **they strike first**. The result sets who strikes first in the Battle.*

*⚙ If the Approach passed:*

> When they reach you, you are standing exactly where you meant to be, and they have to come to you.

*⚙ If the Approach failed:*

> The first of them is round the corner before you are set. You take the fight where it finds you, with your back to whatever stone is nearest.

#### The Battle

> The Order's watchmen come down the hill fighting, and they are not alone. Something comes with them out of a side street: men in armour so old it has gone green and black, moving with a terrible steadiness. You cut one, and it does not bleed. You cut it again, and it does not stop. It keeps walking into your blade as if it had somewhere to be.

**THE BATTLE: THE HOLLOWED**

> **It does not fall until it is broken:** put down, it gets up once more.

*⚙ Every friend fights their own The Hollowed at once (DEF 26, ATK 19), in lockstep rounds every 10 seconds; the fight rules are the weekday ones. Condition: The hollowed: the first time an enemy is put down, it rises with 2 HP and loses its next strike. A friend below 50 HP sends a help-call to the chat; a friend who has won joins another fight. The party wins when the last enemy falls: +1030g each. A fallen friend is "DEFEATED." and stays for the night.*

*⚙ If the party strikes first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> It strikes first.

> When it is over, the armoured dead lie still at last, and the watchmen lie beside them, all but one.
>
> He is sitting against the foot of the wall where he fell, one hand pressed to his side, a crumpled page in the other. He is not looking at you. He is looking up the hill, at the hall, and his lips are moving.
>
> Kneel close enough and you can hear it. He is praying: the words of a page worn soft with saying.

#### The United Effort

> Up the hill, at the Order's fire, there is a lantern that was not there before. The relief has come, and found the fire burning and nobody at it.
>
> A voice comes down the hill, unhurried.
>
> *"Keep the dark."*
>
> Against the wall, the dying man lifts his head and answers it, as he has answered it every night of his life. *"It keeps us."* It is hardly more than breath. It would not carry to the next house.
>
> The voice on the hill waits. Then it calls again, a little to the left. It is calling the posts along the wall, one by one. Every man who should answer is lying in this street.
>
> So you answer for them. One post, one voice. They have kept this watch together all their lives, and they know how each of them sounds. The wind is all that is on your side.

**THE UNITED EFFORT: THE ROLL-CALL**

*⚙ Two bars at 100 (60 for a friend alone): "The Roll-Call" (the thing in the way) and "Their Trust" (the thing at risk). Friends take turns, nobody twice until all have gone. Each turn is a Charm Challenge against DC 9. Pass: 10 + d20 off the thing in the way. Fail: 10 + d20 off the thing at risk. It ends when either bar hits 0. Won: +300g to every friend present. One Relic per roll (+4). The story lines it can say, as the bars fall:*

- **On a pass:**
  - *(early)* You give it the way he gave it: flat, and a little bored. Up the hill, a lantern that had lifted comes down again.
  - *(early)* The answer goes up into the dark. A voice comes back with the next call, in no hurry.
  - *(middle)* You let the wind take half of it. That is how a man answers who has said it ten thousand times.
  - *(middle)* By now you have the cadence of it. The watch barely waits for you to finish.
  - *(late)* Somebody up there laughs at something, low. It is not at you.
  - *(late)* The calls are coming quicker. They want to be done, and sitting down.
  - *(the end)* The last post answers. On the hill, the lantern is set down beside the fire. Somebody sits.
- **On a fail:**
  - *(early)* A breath too quick. On the hill a lantern lifts, and hangs there, and is slow to come down.
  - *(early)* Too loud. A man on a wall at night does not answer like a man who wants to be heard. Nobody calls the next post for a long moment.
  - *(middle)* The words are right, and the voice is wrong. Up at the fire, somebody stands.
  - *(middle)* The call comes again for the same post. They did not like the first answer.
  - *(late)* Two of them are talking now, low, and not calling anyone.
  - *(late)* A second lantern is lit from the first.
  - *(the end)* A third lantern, and a fourth. They start down the hill.
- **Between turns:** Against the wall, his lips are still moving. / The wind is off the hill. It carries every word down to you, and yours up. / In the side street, something in armour shifts, and is still.

> *"Keep the dark."*

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> There is time, now.
>
> You kneel beside him in the street. His eyes have not left the hall, and his lips have not stopped moving. Now it is quiet enough to hear the words.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> There is no time. His eyes are still on the hall, but they no longer see it. The page comes gently out of his fist, and you are inside the wall before the first lantern reaches the gate.

#### The Verse of the Binding

> *From the Third Book of the Order of the Dark Moon — The Verse of the Binding.*
>
> *Hear how they laid him down.*
>
> *They came to his doors in the night — his own, who had held his wall — and his doors were opened to them, because they were his own. They did not come with swords alone. They had found a way in old writings, older than his house, and whole where his house's pages were not; they had gone out into the far places of the world and gathered the shards, and they carried them in their hands.*
>
> *He rose to meet them. He spoke each of their names. Not one of them answered him.*
>
> *Then there was light, and a blow — not the blow of a blade, but of his own gift, turned back upon him the way a river is turned back into its bed. And the king who would not kneel to the end of the world went down upon his knees, and then upon the floor; and his hand opened at his side, as a sleeper's hand opens.*
>
> *They had not tried to kill him. They laid on him a sleep: the Undying Sleep, which holds him still.*
>
> *Those who had stood nearest him, when he fell, stood on. They keep his watch to this day.*
>
> *The fifth they did not scatter with the others. They kept it near him, under their own eyes. Of the fifth, the old words say only this:*

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> *Here the hand changes. One line is set apart, in the older hand:*

> **"The fifth drinks deep of moon-blood, and of no other."**
>
> *Until the throne rise whole.*

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> On *Until the throne rise whole*, his voice stops, and does not start again.

> His page is the prayer, word for word, and one line on it is set apart in the older hand: *The fifth drinks deep of moon-blood, and of no other.* Folded behind it is a second, shorter page. It can wait.

#### Notice

*⚙ Notice: each friend gets one private line for their highest Attribute (a fallen friend gets the "from the floor" line):*

- **Might:** The one you fought did not bleed, and did not weigh what a man weighs. Under that armour there is very little left.
- **Agility:** None of the armoured ones came down the hill. They came out of the side street, where they have stood all week.
- **Intellect:** The watchman's page is worn through at its folds. He did not need to read it.
- **Charm:** He died looking at the hall, and not at any of you. Whatever is in there, he was not afraid of it.
- **Craft:** Every strap on that armour was mended once, long ago, and never since. Nobody has tended these men in centuries.
- **Aim:** Up the hill the Order's fire is still burning, and nobody is standing at it. Every watchman they had came down.
- **From the floor:** From the cobbles you watched one of the armoured ones walk past you without a glance. Its helm was turned to the hall the whole way.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [LOOK CLOSER]*

*⚙ [LOOK CLOSER]: an optional roll on that Attribute (DC 11); a pass adds +75g and this line:*

- **Might:** The cut you gave it is dry all the way in.
- **Agility:** Their footprints in the dust begin in the middle of the street. Before tonight they had not taken a step.
- **Intellect:** It is copied out in a child's round hand. He has carried it since he was a boy.
- **Charm:** At the end, his face was glad.
- **Craft:** The buckles were fastened once and never undone.
- **Aim:** The chalk lies ready under their board, and tonight's stroke has not been struck. The men who should have struck it came down the hill to you.

#### The hidden room

> The way into the hidden room is a stone's throw away, and it is Seeker work.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [OPEN IT]*

> It opens under Mei's hand. Inside, in a plain stone niche facing the window, lies the fifth. Nobody searched for it. The trail brought you straight to it, the way it was always meant to.
>
> It is not the shape on the rubbing. It is smaller, and colder, and it does not feel like earth or wind or water or fire. It feels like holding a piece of the night between the stars. It is the first Stone any of you has ever held in your own hands.

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"Those who had stood nearest him, when he fell, stood on."*
>
> From the house wall at the end of the soldiers' street

*⚙ The right row: "When he fell, the soldiers who had been nearest him stopped where they were, mid-stride. We could not bring ourselves to break them. We left them standing."*

> Tonight they were moving.

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"They kept it near him, under their own eyes."*
>
> From the broken fountain below the hall

*⚙ The right row: "We kept the fifth where we could watch it, and watch him."*

> You are standing in the room they kept it in. Its one window faces his hall.

#### The attempt

> The older hand, on the watchman's page: *The fifth drinks deep of moon-blood, and of no other.*

> Meira's margin, from the drowned market cross: *"'Moon-blood' — the old songs' word for the marked."*

> Garrick says nothing. He's watching the Stone.
>
> A knife goes round the room, hilt first.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [OPEN YOUR PALM]*

> Nothing.
>
> Not a flicker. Not a warmth. The Stone lies in your hands as cold and dark as it was when you found it, and your blood runs off it like rain off glass.
>
> Garrick binds each cut palm himself, without being asked, in the same neat white linen you've seen on every child you've saved. Nobody remarks on it. There's nothing strange about a man having linen to spare.

#### The turn

> For a long moment nobody can speak.

*⚙ A question to the table (anyone answers in the chat; MYTHIOS never grades it):*

> *What did the Seekers' last fragment say?*

*⚙ After sixty quiet seconds:*

> *It is in your Chest.*

*⚙ After ninety, the answer:*

> *"Where he fell, under the dark moon, the five were joined."*
>
> Not here. Not now.
>
> Garrick is looking not at the Stone but out of the window, at the hall on the hill. *"The old telling names a place, and a night."*

#### The Watch

> The second page from the watchman's fist is shorter, and older, and it is the one that makes the room go cold.

> *From the Third Book of the Order of the Dark Moon — The Watch.*
>
> *Hear, you who are faithful. Keep the watch where he fell. Keep his hall in sight, and let no night pass uncounted.*
>
> *The sleep is thinnest when the moon goes dark. On that night of all nights, he hears. On that night of all nights, the faithful gather where he fell, and do not leave him to wake alone.*
>
> *Count the nights.*
>
> *Until the throne rise whole.*

> Beneath it, in a living hand, is a watch-tally: a stroke crossed out for every night, down to the last few left. Seven.
>
> The page goes with you.

#### The Cliffhanger

> Seven strokes left on the tally. Seven nights.
>
> The fifth lies in your cut palms and stays cold. Garrick has bound every one of them in his white linen. He does not tell you it will be all right.
>
> You look out through the slit at the hall. The doors are shut, as they have been for centuries, sealed in a way no hand you know of can open. And somewhere inside, slow and heavy, you can hear it again: the heartbeat that first came to you from the north. It is not far away any more.
>
> Above the hall, framed in the slit, the moon hangs at the half: seven nights from full, seven nights from swallowed whole.
>
> Garrick looks at the moon a long moment. *"We're early."*
>
> Then, across the square below the hall, a new lantern comes out of the dark. A watchman, a fresh one, takes up the chalk and draws it through tonight's stroke, late. Now the board and the page agree: seven left.
>
> He turns, lantern raised, and looks straight down the hill at the wall. At the slit window you are standing behind.

*⚙ The summary card:*

- **THE WALL AT HIGHWALL** · Gathering 5
- You earned: [gold] · [growth, if you rolled] · [Match]
- The party: Battle won · the roll-call [answered / lost] · the watchman's prayer [said / read]
- You learned: **the Seekers did not kill him. They turned his own gift on him, and he sleeps.** His soldiers still stand his watch, and tonight they walked. You hold the fifth, the first Stone you have ever held. Your blood did nothing to it. The Seekers say why: *where he fell, under the dark moon.* The Order is counting to that night. Seven.
- You hold: **the fifth** · **the watch-page**
- Tomorrow: **a watchman, looking straight at your window**

# Week 6: The Finale

*⚙ This week: weekday DC 9; weekday enemy DEF 25, ATK 18; the Gathering calls on Aim. The week's question at the fire: "What will the fifth do in his hall?"*

## Week 6, Day 1

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> That night the Order's watchmen saw your lamps at the foot of the wall and came down the hill. The king's soldiers came with them in their green-black armour, walking at last. When it was over, one watchman lay dying with a page in his fist. It told how the Seekers laid the king down. They did not kill him. They turned his own gift back on him and laid on him the Undying Sleep, and the soldiers nearest him stood on to keep his watch. Of the fifth Stone, the older hand said only, “The fifth drinks deep of moon-blood, and of no other.” In the hidden room you found the fifth, the first Stone you have ever held. Your blood, which had woken the other four, did nothing to it. Garrick bound your cut palms in the same white linen you have seen on every child you saved. The Seekers' last fragment gave the reason, “Where he fell, under the dark moon, the five were joined.” Not here, and not yet. The watchman's second page counted the nights to that dark moon, and seven were left. Garrick looked at the half moon and said, “We're early.” Then a fresh watchman struck the board late, and looked straight down the hill at your window.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Six nights"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: he looked straight at the window. Are you found?*

> Nobody breathes.
>
> Up in the square the watchman stands with his lantern raised, and his face turned down the hill to the wall. To your window.
>
> Somebody pinches the lamp out between finger and thumb.
>
> Then he calls out, one short word, and all round the square other lanterns lift.

> Nobody lights a candle that night. For hours the lanterns move along the foot of the wall and up onto the wall-walk, and you sit in the dark and listen to them come and go.
>
> Twice, boots scrape right past the room's hidden door.
>
> Twice, they walk on.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The hidden door is one slab, a hand thick, hung so that a child could swing it. *(Adept and up:)* It was made to be shut in a hurry, and held.

- **Agility:** The boots on the walk came at the same pace both times. *(Adept and up:)* They are searching by rote. They do not expect to find anything.

- **Intellect:** They hunted the whole wall, and not this stretch more than any other. *(Adept and up:)* He saw a light. He did not see where.

- **Charm:** Your palm is stiff under its white linen. *(Adept and up:)* All of you are wearing it now. The same linen as the children.

- **Craft:** The floor by the slit is worn into two shallow hollows. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody sat here, on watch, for a lifetime. Then somebody else did.

- **Aim:** From the slit, the hall's doors sit square in the middle of the view. *(Adept and up:)* This window was cut for one thing.

> Morning in the dead city looks like evening anywhere else. The fifth is in a pack, wrapped in cloth, and you can feel the cold of it through the canvas.
>
> They kept it in this room for as long as they lived. In the stone niche where it lay, pressed flat beneath it all that time, is something as thin as a leaf.

*⚙ A button: [LIFT IT OUT]*

*⚙ The fragment moment:*

> It comes away from the stone with a dry whisper, and it is colder than the niche that held it.

> They lived in this room. If the Seekers left a way into his hall, the first step of it would be here.

**CHALLENGE: THE ROOM THEY KEPT**

*⚙ Pick one: Intellect (think as they did: where would a watcher leave word for the next watcher?), or Aim (from the doorway, sight along the walls and the floor for the one line that is out of true). Fail: it takes the whole grey day, and the boots come back while you are still looking, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

> Scratched small into the stone under the slit window, where only someone sitting watch would see it, is an arrow.
>

>

> It is a long wall, and from the square every slit in it looks the same. In all its centuries here the Order has never known this room was there. It still does not.
>
> You have a safe room in the enemy's city.

## Week 6, Day 4

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

*⚙ Garrick posts one line for the party:*

> Grey morning, the colour of old ash, and the light can't seem to make up its mind. Eat slowly, and keep your hands warm under your arms. It's not dignified, but it works.

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> At dusk you struck the page to four, and after dark you went up to the great square, where twice as many watchmen now sat round the Order's fire. Their board showed four strokes left, the same as your page. You crossed sixty paces of open ground to the hall's great wall and laid a palm on it. The stone was warm, as if something behind it had been warm for a very long time, and the heartbeat came through it into your hand. There is no way in at the front. The hall's back wall runs into the hill, so tomorrow, the cracked hill behind it.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Three nights"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: where is the Seekers' own way in?*

> The grey does not lift at all today. By noon it is as dim as dusk, and you go round the hall by the back lanes with a lamp lit in the middle of the day.

> Behind the hall, the city's highest ground runs out into a rise of cracked, bare rock, split and heaved as if something underneath had shifted a long time ago.

*⚙ A button: [LOOK FOR THE ONE THAT IS DIFFERENT]*

> A hundred cracks, all of them crooked.
>
> And one that runs straight up and down, as if it had been ruled.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The rock has closed in on that crack over the years. *(Adept and up:)* It is healing shut. It was cut, once, wide enough for a pack.

- **Agility:** A person could go into it only sideways. *(Adept and up:)* And could not turn round.

- **Intellect:** The hall's back wall and this hill are one piece of rock. *(Adept and up:)* A way into the hill is a way into the hall.

- **Charm:** Every one of you has gone quiet at the same moment. *(Adept and up:)* You have all seen what is standing in it.

- **Craft:** A straight crack in heaved rock. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody made it look like the others, and could not make it crooked.

- **Aim:** In the mouth of the crack there is a shape that is not rock. *(Adept and up:)* It has a helm.

> A soldier is standing in the mouth of the crack.
>
> Every one of them in this city faces the hall. This one has its back to it.
>
> It is facing you.

**FIGHT: THE ONE IN THE CRACK**

> It does not mean to let you by.

> You stand there getting your breath.
>

The same scored moon is cut high on the moon-tower at the city's edge.

### Your thread: the chapter, "Two nights"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: what does the scored-dark moon mean?*

> The heartbeat does not wait for dusk any more. It comes at noon. It comes again as you cross the city, and the second time the ground itself trembles.

> The moon-tower stands at the city's edge, slender and tall, with a hundred-odd steps winding up inside it to a round room open to the sky.
>
> Here the old kingdom's sky-watchers kept their records. The walls are cut with the moon's phases, full to dark and back, row on row, year on year.

> Garrick reaches the top a long minute after everyone else, and sits down on the top step with his back to the wall. *"Give me a minute."*
>
> He waves you on into the room with the back of his hand.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** The rows run higher than anyone could reach from the floor. *(Adept and up:)* The watchers cut ledges in the wall for their own feet.

- **Agility:** The steps are worn in the middle, every one, by the same feet night after night. *(Adept and up:)* The top three are hardly worn at all.

- **Intellect:** Row on row, year on year, and never a gap. *(Adept and up:)* Whoever kept this did not miss a night in generations.

- **Charm:** It is patient work, and it is loving. *(Adept and up:)* Somebody was proud of this wall.

- **Craft:** Every moon is cut to the same depth, by the same kind of tool. *(Adept and up:)* All but one.

- **Aim:** Follow the rows round, and they stop. *(Adept and up:)* After that, the stone is bare, all the way back to the door.

**CHALLENGE: THE SKY-WATCHERS' WALL**

> Thousands of moons. One of them is not like the others.

*⚙ Pick one: Craft (find it by the cut of the chisel), or Might (climb the watchers' ledges to the highest rows, and read them from there). Fail: hours of faded marks by lamplight, in a tower the whole city can see, and −150g. An Attribute Relic adds +4.*

*⚙ If you failed the Challenge:*

> One moon is cut deeper than all the rest: a full moon, struck dark.
>
> After it, the wall is blank. They never carved another.
>
> It is the night he fell.

### Morning, in the party chat (5:00am)

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> The heartbeat no longer waited for dusk, and by noon the ground trembled with it. At the top of the moon-tower, where the old sky-watchers cut every moon into the wall, you found the one cut deeper than the rest, the full moon struck out on the night he fell, with bare stone after it. Counting forward with a fingertip at their spacing, you came all the way round the room to the doorpost, and that same moon comes again in two nights. As the nearly full moon came up over the open roof, Garrick said quietly, “It always comes back,” and you struck the page to two. You have the hour, one piece of the map is left, and tomorrow is the last day.

### Your thread: the chapter, "One night"

*⚙ Played alone, in your own thread. Today's question: how does the blank rock open?*

> Everything is worse today. The grey never lifts. The heartbeat comes and comes, and each time the dust sifts down off the rooftops.
>
> And between you and the hill, the street is full of soldiers.
>
> They are not standing.
>
> They are walking: slowly, stiffly, all the same way. Up the hill, to the hall.

*⚙ Your build notices: you see the line for your highest Attribute (from Adept on, with its second sentence).*

- **Might:** They do not go round what is in their way. *(Adept and up:)* A cart, a fallen door: they walk through it.

- **Agility:** They keep step. *(Adept and up:)* There are gaps between the ranks, and the gaps move at the same pace.

- **Intellect:** Not one has turned its head. *(Adept and up:)* They are not looking for anything. They know where they are going.

- **Charm:** They are a company again. *(Adept and up:)* Whatever else has gone, that has not.

- **Craft:** Iron on stone, in time. *(Adept and up:)* It covers every other sound in the street. It would cover yours.

- **Aim:** At the top of the street, where the hill begins, one of them has stopped. *(Adept and up:)* It is not facing the hall.

I'll mind the fire.", "Same fire tomorrow?"), "What we did today" (one line per friend, only for what they really did), then the Pull again, and a message in a bottle for whoever comes next.*

## Week 6, Day 7: the Gathering

### The road so far

*⚙ Opens the day in your thread: yesterday's recap.*

> The street to the hill was full of the king's soldiers, walking slowly up to his hall, and you crossed between their ranks. At the mouth of the crack one of them had turned back, and you broke it where the last one fell. At the dead end the last fragment made the map whole, and it showed that the blank rock is a door, marked with one word, *here*, beside the scored moon. Back in the wall room you struck the page to one, and Garrick poured the last of his flask, with one cup more on the sill of the slit window. Tomorrow night, when the moon goes dark, you go through the Seekers' door into his hall with the fifth in your hands.

### The Gathering (live, the whole party together in the party chat)

*⚙ That morning, in each friend's thread:*

> Tonight: the Gathering. Through the Seekers' door, with the fifth, as the moon goes dark. Tonight calls on Aim.

*⚙ Lobby at the time the party picked (poll on Day 4); starts once everyone is in, or any friend may start after 10 minutes, or by itself after 30. MYTHIOS posts the story a passage at a time; [GO ON] moves it on. About 20 to 30 minutes with the table talking.*

#### Arrival

> The Seekers' way in is a crack in the hill behind the hall, so narrow you go through it sideways, so old the stone has healed half shut around it. At its end, the fitted stone that passes for rock swings inward under your hands, for your blood, and no one else's.
>
> Cold air breathes out of the dark beyond it. It smells of dust, and of lamp-oil burned a very long time ago.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [STRIKE THE LAST STROKE]*

> Mei strikes the last stroke through the watch-page by lamplight, there on the threshold. None left.
>
> Far above, in the thin strip of sky the crack lets through, the moon is full. At its edge, a sliver of it has already gone dark, and the dark is spreading.

#### The Approach

> Beyond the stone is a passage the Seekers cut for themselves: one turn, then a stair, then the back of the hall.

*⚙ A group vote (majority after 30 seconds; the vote shows who is best at each option): HOW DO YOU GO IN?*

1. **LAMPS DARK**: unseen, and half blind.
2. **LAMPS LIT**: you will see every step, and so will anything that is looking this way.

**THE APPROACH: THE SEEKERS' STAIR**

*⚙ One friend steps up (whoever has rolled fewest Approaches) and rolls for the party, in front of everyone: Lamps dark → Agility (bring everyone down the stair without a sound); Lamps lit → Intellect (read the Seekers' marks on the wall, and find where they meant a watcher to stand). DC 11 (+2 if the party solved the week's map). Pass: +500g · **and the party strikes first**. Fail: a lamp rings on stone, and **they strike first**. The result sets who strikes first in the Battle.*

*⚙ If the Approach passed:*

> You come out behind a pillar, unseen, with one long breath to take in the hall.

*⚙ If the Approach failed:*

> A lamp slips from somebody's fingers and rings on the stair, and the sound goes on and on up into the dark of the roof. By the time you step out, every face among the faithful is already turned your way, and their singing has not missed a note.

#### The Battle

> The hall is vast, and cold, and full.
>
> Across the floor, a trapdoor stands thrown back on a stair going down into the dark: the way his household ran, the night the Seekers came. The faithful came up it. They did not need the Seekers' door. They are here, more than you have ever seen together, robed, singing, and between them stand the hollowed, row on row of them, his soldiers from the last war, still standing their watch after all these centuries. Every blank face turns toward you at once.

**THE BATTLE: **

> **ONE OF THE FAITHFUL** · **The chant:** while more than half the faithful are standing, each of them is harder to put down. *or* **ONE OF THE HOLLOWED** · **It does not fall until it is broken:** put down, it gets up once more.

*⚙ Every friend fights their own The Faithful & The Hollowed at once (DEF 26, ATK 19), in lockstep rounds every 10 seconds; the fight rules are the weekday ones. Condition: Half faithful (the chant: DEF +1 while >50% stand), half hollowed (rises once at 2 HP). A friend below 50 HP sends a help-call to the chat; a friend who has won joins another fight. The party wins when the last enemy falls: +1030g each. A fallen friend is "DEFEATED." and stays for the night.*

*⚙ If the party strikes first:*

> You strike first.

*⚙ If the enemy strikes first:*

> They strike first.

#### The United Effort

> One line of them is left between you and the throne, shoulder to shoulder, still singing. Above them, on a chain as thick as a wrist, hangs the hall's great iron lamp, dark for centuries.
>
> The chain runs up over a roof-beam and down the far wall to an iron ring. The last yard of it, where it is tied off, is not chain. It is rope: tarred, black, as thick as an arm, and older than the Order.
>
> You cannot get to it. You can hit it.
>
> You are holding a line of your own, and it is all that is keeping theirs off you. One of you can step back out of it for as long as one shot takes. The gap is felt at once.

**THE UNITED EFFORT: THE HAWSER**

*⚙ Two bars at 100 (60 for a friend alone): "The Hawser" (the thing in the way) and "Your Line" (the thing at risk). Friends take turns, nobody twice until all have gone. Each turn is a Aim Challenge against DC 9. Pass: 10 + d20 off the thing in the way. Fail: 10 + d20 off the thing at risk. It ends when either bar hits 0. Won: +300g to every friend present. One Relic per roll (+4). The story lines it can say, as the bars fall:*

- **On a pass:**
  - *(early)* It goes in where the rope bends over the ring. A strand springs loose and stands out from it like a hair.
  - *(early)* Another strand. High above the singing line, the great lamp turns a little on its chain.
  - *(middle)* Half of it is hanging in tatters. The rest is drawn as tight as a bowstring.
  - *(middle)* The lamp is swinging now, slow, the width of a man.
  - *(late)* It is singing: one thin note, under theirs.
  - *(late)* One strand. You can see the light through it.
  - *(the end)* It parts.
- **On a fail:**
  - *(early)* Wide. It rings on the iron ring and is gone into the dark. While you were aiming, the line took a step.
  - *(early)* You hit the stone a hand's breadth from it. The place you left in your own line has closed up, a yard further back than it was.
  - *(middle)* Somebody takes a blow that was meant for the gap you left.
  - *(middle)* You loose with a hollowed face a sword's length from yours.
  - *(late)* Your backs are almost against the pillars.
  - *(late)* There is no room left to step back into.
  - *(the end)* There is nowhere left to shoot from.
- **Between turns:** They have not stopped singing. They have not once looked up. / Beyond their line, on the floor before the throne, something is giving off a faint light.

*⚙ If one friend plays alone:*

> There is a pillar at your back, and it is the only line you have. One step out from it, one shot, and back.

*⚙ If the United Effort was won:*

> For a moment nothing happens. Then, high above, the great iron lamp drops out of the dark, turning once as it falls.
>
> The lamp comes down like a bell falling, and the line breaks around it. The floor jumps under your feet, and through the dust their singing goes ragged.

*⚙ If the United Effort was lost:*

> The chain holds. The lamp hangs above you, dark and no use to anyone, and their line comes on.
>
> So you go through them the hard way, shoulder to shoulder, a step at a time, bleeding, until there is nobody left between you and the throne.

#### The king

> When you break through at last, the singing has stopped, and you see what is at the heart of the hall.
>
> Before the empty throne, on the stone floor where he fell, lies the king. He is not dust. He is not bones. He looks like a man asleep: pale, still, one hand open at his side as though something was once taken out of it. *Death's likeness, and not death.*
>
> Around him, set in a ring, faintly glowing, are the four Stones. Earth, wind, water, fire. Every one you ever found for them.

> You hold the only missing piece. Right place. Right night. The moon going dark overhead.

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [OPEN YOUR PALM]*

> Nothing.
>
> Your blood runs down over the Stone and drips onto the floor of his hall, and the fifth lies in your hand as cold and dark as the night you found it.

#### The first twist

> The silence is so complete you can hear the last of the faithful breathing in the dark behind you.
>
> Garrick crouches beside whoever is holding the fifth. He looks at the cut palm, at the Stone lying cold in it, at the dust of the long road on them. *"You've carried it far enough,"* he says.
>
> He says it gently, the way he has said everything to you since the night at the inn.
>
> Then he stands, and holds out his hand. *"Let me."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [WHY WOULD YOURS WORK?]*

> *"You've all tried tonight,"* he says. *"I haven't."*

*⚙ Every friend picks privately; the majority decides (a tie holds it back): THE FIFTH IS IN YOUR HANDS.*

1. **GIVE IT TO HIM**: 
2. **HOLD IT BACK**: 

*⚙ If option 1 was picked:*

> You hand him the fifth. You do it yourselves.
>
> He does not hesitate. The knife is already in his hand, and the cut is quick and practised. And the Stone *answers*.

*⚙ If option 2 was picked:*

> He does not argue. The fight has left his palm bleeding. He reaches out and closes his hand over the Stone where it lies in yours, and it answers him anyway. You still carried it to him, into this room.

> Not a flicker. A flood: cold light pouring up out of it the instant his blood touches it, hungry, as though it has been waiting for this for centuries. Around the king, the four Stones blaze in answer, and the light breaks over the ring like a wave and pins you all where you kneel. You can breathe. You can see. You cannot rise.
>
> *"The page said the fifth would end this,"* Garrick says. *"It never said whose blood."*

> The torn page: **"…drink deep whichever marked blood finds them first. The fifth heeds no element at all, but blood alone, and remembers whose."**

> The Verse of the Binding: **"The fifth drinks deep of moon-blood, and of no other."**

> The Seekers, on the wall above the herald's gate: **"Four of us gave to the four. None of us gave to the fifth."**

> The warning Meira glossed: **"Moon-blood woke it once. Moon-blood will wake it again, if it is ever let near."**

> *"You read them as one,"* Garrick says. He does not sound cruel. *"They cut his name out of everything they could reach. The word outlived him and wandered off onto any child born in the dark. Your Meira used it that way."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [DID YOU EVER LIE TO US?]*

> *"No,"* Garrick says. *"The faithful don't."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [MEIRA?]*

> *"My people,"* he says. *"On the road, the night she left with those pages."*
>
> Her pen is in that satchel still, with the rest of her.

> Around the ring, the faithful are getting up off the floor. None of them has ever seen Garrick's face. They do not need to: the fifth answered him. One by one they bow their heads to him, and the first of them says it, the words you heard the watchmen trade in the square below this hall.
>
> *"Keep the dark."*
>
> *"It keeps us,"* Garrick says.

#### The Verse of the Name

> As the light climbs the four Stones, Garrick takes a book from inside his coat. Not a page, not a scrap: his own copy of the Order's scripture, whole, worn soft with handling, nothing torn, nothing burned. He opens it the way a man opens something his family has kept for generations, and reads.

> *From the First Book of the Order of the Dark Moon, as his own house kept it — The Verse of the Name.*
>
> *Hear how he came by the name we keep.*
>
> *On the morning of the answering, when the enemy's camp was only ash and silence, his people came up onto the wall to look at him.*
>
> *They had known him all his life as one of the marked — born in the dark moon's hour, as many are. That morning they knew the word no longer fit him. The marked carry the moon's light the way a lamp carries fire. He did not carry it. He had been answered by it. A piece of the moon had drunk of him, and would drink of no one else, ever again — no one but him, and his children, and their children, for as long as his blood ran anywhere under the sky.*

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"They had known him all his life as one of the marked."*
>
> From the barracks hearth, where the Seekers slept

*⚙ The right row: "We were marked, every one of us. That is how we found the shards."*

*⚙ A button any friend may tap: [READ ON]*

> *So they gave him a new name, there on the wall. Not marked. Moon-blood. It was a title, and it was his alone, and after him it was his house's.*
>
> *When the Seekers came into his hall, his house fled into the night. They let us go. We were only servants, they said. We have been servants ever since — of him, of his sleep, of the long and patient waiting. We kept the old words his house had always kept. We lost not one of them, in all the long dark. And we have never forgotten how they end.*

**MATCH**

*⚙ WHICH OF YOUR FRAGMENTS IS THIS? The card shows this line, and the week's six fragments as rows; each friend picks privately. Each right pick +50g (100g if every friend present is right). Wrong picks are never shown.*

> *"We were only servants, they said."*
>
> From the wall room, where they kept their watch

*⚙ The right row: "His household ran the night we came to the hall. We let them go. They were only servants."*

> *"Bloodlines outlast kingdoms. That is why kingdoms fear them."*

> *His house had kept a thing since before his house had a name.* It was never lost. It was the fifth.

#### The second twist

> He turns the page, and you know it before he reads it: the same page you carried up out of the drowned village, burned and torn. Only his copy is whole.
>
> On his page the older hand runs on, unbroken, past the place where yours was torn:
>
> **"Four were given to the wind, the water, the fire, and the earth, and drink deep whichever marked blood finds them first. The fifth heeds no element at all, but blood alone, and remembers whose. What the four begin, the fifth shall end, and the throne rise whole."**

> He reads it aloud as far as the tear, *what the four begin, the fifth shall end*, and stops. He does not need to go on. All around the hall, low and together, the faithful finish it for him, in the same voice you first heard chanting under the mill hill: *"…and the throne rise whole."*
>
> *"They used his own blood on the fifth while his power was pouring out of him,"* Garrick says, softly, watching the light rise. *"Tonight he sleeps. Same key. Turned the other way."*

*⚙ If option 2 was picked:*

> He takes the fifth out of your hand. Pinned in the light, you cannot close your fingers on it.

> He sets the book down open on the stone. From his coat he takes a tin cup, the one that sat on the sill of the wall room last night, and sets it on the floor beside the sleeping king, with the same small care he gave every cup on the road.
>
> Every night on the road, one more cup than there were people. It was always for him.
>
> Then he kneels, and closes the king's open hand around the fifth Stone: the hand that has lain empty at his side since the moment he fell. *"One stayed close,"* Garrick says. *"It always did."*
>
> As the fifth leaves his hand, the light shifts to the king, and for one heartbeat, its grip on one of you slackens.

#### The act

> The light lets go of Arjun. One heartbeat. One act.
>
> **Everyone else: you have thirty seconds. Tell Arjun what to do.**

*⚙ One friend, chosen at random, picks alone*

1. **TEAR THE PAGE**: snatch the open book from the stone and rip the untorn page out of it.
2. **BREAK THE RING**: strike one of the four Stones out of the ring.
3. **DO NOTHING**: 

*⚙ If option 1 was picked:*

> It is done before anyone can stop it. The page is in Arjun's fist.

*⚙ If option 2 was picked:*

> One blow, and the Stone skids across the floor, still glowing, out of the light and away into the dark of the hall.

*⚙ If option 3 was picked:*

> Arjun does not move. The heartbeat passes, and the light closes round Arjun again like a hand.

> *"Your Seekers made their children the only key,"* Garrick says, looking down at you from the king's side. *"The key that locks is also the key that unlocks."* A breath. *"You were the key."*

> *"If you are reading this, you are ours — and if he ever stirs, finish what we could not."*

#### The Cliffhanger

> High in the hall's windows, the last of the moon goes dark.

*⚙ If option 1 was picked:*

> The four Stones flare white. The fifth, in the king's closed hand, burns so cold it frosts the air.
>
> The page is still in Arjun's fist.

*⚙ If option 2 was picked:*

> Three flare white in the ring, and far off in the dark of the hall the fourth answers them alone. The fifth, in the king's closed hand, burns so cold it frosts the air.

*⚙ If option 3 was picked:*

> The four Stones flare white. The fifth, in the king's closed hand, burns so cold it frosts the air.

> Nobody breathes.
>
> And on the floor of his hall, where he fell, the king opens his eyes.
>
> They pass over Garrick, kneeling at his side, as if he were not there. They move across the ring of light. And they settle on you. Whatever he is now, he knows Seeker blood when he sees it.
>
> *The power that gives is also the power that takes.*
>
> Garrick looks down at you, at all of you, kneeling in the ring of light, palms cut open again tonight, the same white linen from a week ago still knotted at every wrist. His face is courteous, and nothing more.
>
> *"You saved every child you were sent to save,"* he says.

1. **TAMSIN**: 
2. **CORIN**: 
3. **WREN**: 
4. **JOSS**: 

> **Tamsin,** humming her counting song so she wouldn't cry. *Mei.*

> **Corin,** saying your names back to keep them. *Arjun.*

> **Wren,** pressing a chip of black glass into your palm.

> **Joss,** holding on to his little boat.

#### The last notice

*⚙ Notice: each friend gets one private line for their highest Attribute (a fallen friend gets the "from the floor" line):*

- **Might:** The light holds you by nothing you can push against. For the first time since the forest road, strength is not what is being asked of you.
- **Agility:** The trapdoor his household ran by is still standing open, twenty paces behind the faithful. Nobody is watching it.
- **Intellect:** He knew Seeker blood the moment he saw it. Nobody has yet told him how long he slept.
- **Charm:** He is looking at each of you in turn, the way Corin did. As if to keep you.
- **Craft:** The knot at your wrist is the one at Tamsin's. You watched it tied a week ago, and thought nothing of it.
- **Aim:** Through the high windows there is no moon at all. Not a rim of it.

#### The last lines

> Tamsin's hand. Corin's. Wren's. Joss's. Yours. The same linen. The same careful knot, every time.
>
> **To be continued.**

His people killed Meira. The fifth did not stop the waking; it finished it. The king is awake, and he is looking at you.
- You saved every child you were sent to save.
- What you did in that last heartbeat is remembered. Season 2 begins from it.
